On the Geometric Side of the Jacquet-Rallis Relative Trace Formula
Weixiao Lu

Abstract
. We study some aspects of the geometric side of the Jacquet-Rallis relative trace formula. Globally, we compute each geometric term of the Jacquet-Rallis relative trace formula on the general linear group for regular supported test functions. We prove that it can be described by the regular orbital integral. Locally, we show that the regular orbital integral can be compared with the semisimple orbital integral on the unitary group.



1.
Introduction

The global Gan–Gross–Prasad(GGP) conjecture
[
14
]
relates the non-vanishing of period integral on classical groups to the non-vanishing of the central value of certain L-functions. In
[
15
]
, Jacquet and Rallis proposed a relative trace formula(RTF) approach to the GGP conjecture for the Bessel periods on
\U(n)\times\U(n+1)
. In
[
23
]
, Zhang solved the smooth transfer conjecture and proved the global Gan-Gross-Prasad conjecture for
\U(n)\times\U(n+1)
under some local conditions as a consequence.

We briefly review the Jacquet-Rallis RTF on the general linear group here. Let
E/F
be a quadratic extension of number fields. For
k\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 1}
, let

\mathrm{G}^{\prime}_{k}:=\mathrm{Res}_{E/F}\GL_{k,E}.

Let
\mathrm{G}^{\prime}=\mathrm{G}^{\prime}_{n}\times\mathrm{G}^{\prime}_{n+1}
.
\mathrm{G}^{\prime}
has two subgroups
\mathrm{H}_{1},\mathrm{H}_{2}
, where

(1.1)

\mathrm{H}_{1}=(h,\begin{pmatrix}h&\\
&1\end{pmatrix})(h\in\mathrm{G}^{\prime}_{n}),\quad\mathrm{H}_{2}=\GL_{n,F}\times\GL_{n+1,F}.

Let
\mathbb{A}=\mathbb{A}_{F}
, the adèle ring of
F
and
[\mathrm{G}^{\prime}]:=\mathrm{G}^{\prime}(F)\backslash\mathrm{G}^{\prime}(\mathbb{A})
. We write
\eta
for the quadratic character on
\mathbb{A}^{\times}
associated with
E/F
. By an abuse of notation, we also write
\eta
for the character on
\mathrm{H}_{2}(\mathbb{A})
defined by

\eta(h_{2,n},h_{2,n+1})=\eta(h_{2,n})^{n+1}\eta(h_{2,n+1})^{n}.

Let
f\in\mathcal{S}(\mathrm{G}^{\prime}(\mathbb{A}))
be a Schwartz function and let
K_{f}(x,y)
be its automorphic kernel function:

K_{f}(x,y)=\sum_{\gamma\in\mathrm{G}^{\prime}(F)}f(x^{-1}\gamma y),\quad(x,y)\in[\mathrm{G}^{\prime}]\times[\mathrm{G}^{\prime}].

Consider the distribution

(1.2)

I(f)=\int_{[\mathrm{H}_{1}]}\int_{[\mathrm{H}_{2}]}K_{f}(h_{1},h_{2})\eta(h_{2})\mathrm{d}h_{1}\mathrm{d}h_{2}.

Under some conditions on
f
, this integral is absolutely convergent and has a geometric and spectral expansion.

In
[
25
]
, Zydor defined a regularization of the integral (
1.2
) for all compactly supported smooth functions
f\in C_{c}^{\infty}(\mathrm{G}^{\prime}(\mathbb{A}))
, hence (
1.2
) makes sense for such
f
, and it has geometric and spectral expansion. The regularization and geometric/spectral expansions were extended by Beuzart-Plessis, Chaudouard, and Zydor in
[
3
]
and
[
11
]
for general Schwartz functions, hence a full coarse Jacquet-Rallis RTF is established. Together with other techniques developed in
[
20
]
,
[
6
]
,
[
19
]
,
[
11
]
,
[
5
]
, the endoscopic case of the global GGP conjecture for
\U(n)\times\U(n+1)
was proved in
[
3
]
. It was extended to certain Eisenstein series and higher corank cases by Beuzart-Plessis–Chaudouard
[
4
]
. In this article, we will study some aspects of the geometric side of the Jacquet–Rallis relative trace formula.

1.1.
Global Results

Let
\mathrm{B}=\mathrm{H}_{1}\backslash\mathrm{G}^{\prime}/\mathrm{H}_{2}
be the GIT quotient and let
q:\mathrm{G}^{\prime}\mathrm{B}
be the quotient map, for any
b\in\mathrm{B}(F)
, we write
\mathrm{G}^{\prime}_{b}
for the fiber of
b
under the quotient map. Then there is a
b
-part of the distribution
I
, denoted by
I_{b}
, which is a regularization of the integral

\int_{[\mathrm{H}_{1}]}\int_{[\mathrm{H}_{2}]}\left(\sum_{\gamma\in\mathrm{G}^{\prime}_{b}(F)}f(h_{1}^{-1}\gamma h_{2})\right)\eta(h_{2})\mathrm{d}h_{1}\mathrm{d}h_{2}.

The geometric expansion of
I
is then the identity

I(f)=\sum_{b\in\mathrm{B}(F)}I_{b}(f).

If
b
is regular semi-simple, pick any
\gamma\in\mathrm{G}^{\prime}(F)
with image
b
, the distribution
I_{b}
can be computed via the orbital integral

I_{b}(f)=\int_{\mathrm{H}_{1}(\mathbb{A})}\int_{\mathrm{H}_{2}(\mathbb{A})}f(h_{1}^{-1}\gamma h_{2})\eta(h_{2})\mathrm{d}h_{1}\mathrm{d}h_{2}.

But for general
b\in\mathrm{B}(F)
, there is no easy interpretation of
I_{b}
. One of the main goals of this paper is to compute
I_{b}(f)
, for general
b\in\mathrm{B}(F)
, under the assumption that there is a place
v
of
F
such that
f=f_{v}f^{v}
, where
f^{v}\in\mathcal{S}(\mathrm{G}^{\prime}(\mathbb{A}^{v})),f_{v}\in\mathcal{S}(\mathrm{G}^{\prime}(F_{v}))
and
f_{v}
is supported in the regular subset
\mathrm{G}^{\prime}_{\mathrm{reg}}(F_{v})
of
\mathrm{G}^{\prime}(F_{v})
, where
\mathrm{G}_{\mathrm{reg}}^{\prime}
is the Zariski open subset of
\mathrm{G}^{\prime}
consisting of elements whose stabilizer under the
\mathrm{H}_{1}\times\mathrm{H}_{2}
action is trivial.

Let
\gamma\in\mathrm{G}^{\prime}_{\mathrm{reg}}(F)
be a regular element and
f\in\mathcal{S}(\mathrm{G}^{\prime}(\mathbb{A}))
(with no support condition), in Subsection
6.1
, we will define a distribution
I_{\gamma}(f)
, which can be written formally as

(1.3)

I_{\gamma}(f)=\int_{\mathrm{H}_{1}(\mathbb{A})}\int_{\mathrm{H}_{2}(\mathbb{A})}f(h_{1}^{-1}\gamma h_{2})\eta(h_{2})\mathrm{d}h_{1}\mathrm{d}h_{2},

note that this integral is not convergent in general, therefore some regularization is needed. Once the distribution
I_{\gamma}
is defined, our main theorem can be summarized as follows:

Theorem 1.1

(See Theorem
6.1
)
.

If
f\in\mathcal{S}(\mathrm{G}^{\prime}(\mathbb{A}))
is of the form
f_{v}f^{v}
, and
f_{v}
is regular supported, then for any
b\in\mathrm{B}(F)

I_{b}(f)=\sum_{\gamma}I_{\gamma}(f),

where in the summation,
\gamma
runs through any representative of
\mathrm{H}_{1}(F)\times\mathrm{H}_{2}(F)
orbits of regular elements in
\mathrm{G}^{\prime}_{b}(F)
.

In Subsection
2.6
, we will describe the regular orbits of
\mathrm{G}^{\prime}_{b}(F)

explicitly. In particular, we will see that the sum above is a finite sum.

There is a Zariski open subset
\mathrm{G}^{\prime}_{+}
of
\mathrm{G}^{\prime}
such that
\mathrm{G}^{\prime}_{+}\subset\mathrm{G}^{\prime}_{\mathrm{reg}}
(see Subsection
2.4
). When
f_{v}
is supported in
\mathrm{G}^{\prime}_{+}(F_{v})
, we will have a better description of
I_{\gamma}(f)
as follows:

Theorem 1.2

(See Theorem
4.2
)
.

If
f\in\mathcal{S}(\mathrm{G}^{\prime}(\mathbb{A}))
is of the form
f_{v}f^{v}
, and
f_{v}
is supported in
\mathrm{G}^{\prime}_{+}(F_{v})
. Let
\gamma\in\mathrm{G}^{\prime}_{+}(F)
, then we have the following assertions:

(1)

For
s\in\mathbb{C}
with
\mathrm{Re}(s)
sufficiently negative, the integral

\int_{\mathrm{H}_{1}(\mathbb{A})}\int_{\mathrm{H}_{2}(\mathbb{A})}f(h_{1}^{-1}\gamma h_{2})\eta(h_{2})\lvert\det h_{1}\rvert^{s}\mathrm{d}h_{1}\mathrm{d}h_{2}

is absolutely convergent, and has a meromorphic continuation to
\mathbb{C}
which is holomorphic at
0
. The value at
s=0
coincides with
I_{\gamma}(f)
.

(2)

For each
b\in\mathrm{B}(F)
, there is a unique
\mathrm{H}_{1}(F)\times\mathrm{H}_{2}(F)
regular orbit inside
\mathrm{G}^{\prime}_{b}(F)\cap\mathrm{G}^{\prime}_{+}(F)
. Choose any
\gamma
in this orbit, then

I_{b}(f)=I_{\gamma}(f).

In fact, we will first prove Theorem
1.2
and a Lie algebra version of it in Section
4
, and then deduce Theorem
1.1
from it in Section
6
.

1.2.
Local Results

Let
\gamma\in\mathrm{G}^{\prime}(F)
be a regular element, our regularization of the integral (
1.3
) will make the distribution
I_{\gamma}(f)
Eulerian. In fact, to define
I_{\gamma}(f)
, we first study a local analogue of it in Section
5
. Let
v
be a place of
F
, and let
f\in\mathcal{S}(\mathrm{G}^{\prime}(F_{v}))
(with no support condition), we will prove the following result in Section
5
.

Proposition 1.3
.

[See Subsection
5.3
and Proposition
5.12
]
Let
\gamma\in\mathrm{G}^{\prime}_{\mathrm{reg}}(F_{v})
, then there is a map

\mathcal{S}(\mathrm{G}^{\prime}(F_{v}))\{\text{meromorphic functions on }\mathbb{C}\},\quad fI_{\gamma}(f,s)

which is a regularization of the integral

(1.4)

\int_{\mathrm{H}_{1}(F_{v})}\int_{\mathrm{H}_{2}(F_{v})}f(h_{1}^{-1}\gamma h_{2})\lvert\det h_{1}\rvert^{s}\eta(h_{2})\mathrm{d}h_{1}\mathrm{d}h_{2}.

with the following properties:

(1)

I_{\gamma}(f,s)
only depends on the value of
f
on the orbit of
\gamma
.

(2)

For
x\in\mathrm{H}_{1}(F_{v})
and
y\in\mathrm{H}_{2}(F_{v})
, let
f^{x,y}(\gamma)=f(x\gamma y^{-1})\in\mathcal{S}(\mathrm{G}^{\prime}(F_{v}))
, then

I_{\gamma}(f^{x,y},s)=\lvert\det x\rvert^{s}\eta(y)I_{\gamma}(f,s).

(3)

If
\gamma\in\mathrm{G}^{\prime}_{+}(F_{v})
, then the integral (
1.4
) is absolutely convergent for
\mathrm{Re}(s)
sufficiently negative, and coincides with
I_{\gamma}(f,s)
when convergent. In particular, this holds when
\gamma
is regular semisimple.

(4)

There is an abelian
L
-function
L_{\gamma}(s)
such that
I^{\natural}_{\gamma}(f,s):=I_{\gamma}(f,s)/L_{\gamma}(s)
is entire for any
f
, and when
f=1_{\mathrm{G}^{\prime}(\mathcal{O})}
and everything is unramified,
I_{\gamma}(f,s)=L_{\gamma}(s)
.

When
\gamma
is regular semisimple, the orbital integral
I_{\gamma}(f,s)
has been studied in depth. One key step towards the proof of the global GGP conjecture is to show that regular semisimple orbital integral
I_{\gamma}(f,0)
can be compared with similarly defined regular semisimple orbital on the unitary groups.

Let
\mathcal{H}_{v}
be the isometric classes of
n
-dimensional non-degenerate
E_{v}/F_{v}
Hermitian spaces. For
V\in\mathcal{H}_{v}
, let
V^{\prime}
be the
n+1
-dimensional Hermitian space
V\oplus E_{v}e_{0}
, the Hermitian form on
V^{\prime}
is defined by orthogonal direct sum of
V\oplus h_{0}
where
h_{0}(e_{0},e_{0})=1
. We put
\mathrm{G}^{V}=\U(V)\times\U(V^{\prime})
and
\mathrm{H}^{V}:=\U(V)
as a diagonal subgroup of
\mathrm{G}^{V}
. Let
f^{V}\in\mathcal{S}(\mathrm{G}^{V}(F_{v}))
and let
\gamma\in\mathrm{G}^{V}(F_{v})
be a regular semisimple element, the orbital integral
J_{\gamma}(f)
is defined by

J_{\gamma}(f)=\int_{\mathrm{H}^{V}(F_{v})\times\mathrm{H}^{V}(F_{v})}f(x^{-1}\gamma y)\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}y.

Let
f\in\mathrm{G}^{\prime}(F_{v})
and for each
V\in\mathcal{H}_{v}
let
f^{V}\in\mathcal{S}(\mathrm{G}^{V}(F_{v}))
, there is a notion of matching between
f
and
f^{V}
(See Subsection
7.2
), which means
f
and
f^{V}
have the “same” regular semisimple orbital integrals. In Section
7
, we will prove a version of “local singular transfer”, which shows that for
f
and
(f^{V})_{V\in\mathcal{H}_{v}}
, if their regular semisimple orbital integrals match (i.e., they are matching), then the more singular terms, i.e. the regular orbital integral
I^{\natural}_{\gamma}(f,0)
and the semisimple orbital integral of
f^{V}
also match.

Theorem 1.4
.

(See Theorem
7.9
)
Suppose that
f
and
(f^{V})_{V\in\mathcal{H}_{v}}
match, let
\gamma\in\mathrm{G}^{\prime}_{\mathrm{reg}}(F_{v})
, then we have

I^{\natural}_{\gamma}(f,0)=\sum_{(V,\mathcal{O})}c_{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}J_{\mathcal{O}}(f^{V}),

where the summation runs through the set
\{(V,\mathcal{O})\mid V\in\mathcal{H}_{v},\mathcal{O}\text{ is a semisimple orbit corresponds to }\gamma\}
, and
c_{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}
are some explicit constants independent of
f
and
f^{V}
.

This result can be regarded as a local analogue of the singular transfer theorem of Chaudouard–Zydor in
[
11
]
*Théorème 1.1.6.1.

1.3.
Remarks

In
[
21
]
, Zhang proposed an RTF approach to the arithmetic Gan–Gross–Prasad (̃AGGP) conjecture. In
[
13
]
, a
p
-adic version of the AGGP conjecture is proved. One key difficulty in
[
13
]
is caused by the use of a very specific test function at some split places, and these functions have regular support but not regular semisimple support, in order to
p
-adically interpolate the orbital integral. The result in this article could be applied to
loc.cit.
.

Liu
[
17
]
proposed an RTF to attack the global GGP conjecture for the Fourier-Jacobi period on unitary groups, and using this RTF, Xue
[
18
]
proved the global GGP conjectures for Fourier-Jacobi period under some local conditions. Some of the local conditions were removed in
[
19
]
, and a full coarse RTF was established by Boisseau, Xue, and the author
[
10
]
. Using the coarse RTF, the global GGP conjectures for the Fourier-Jacobi period, together with the Eisenstein and higher corank case, are proved in
loc.cit.
. We will also consider the Fourier-Jacobi analogue of the main results in Section
8
.

We give an outline of this article. After some preliminaries in Section
2
, in Section
3
, we extend the coarse Jacquet-Rallis RTF on the general linear group and its Lie algebra by allowing for a general character on
\mathrm{H}_{1}
. In Section
4
, we will prove Theorem
1.2
. In Section
5
, we will define the local regular orbital integral and study its properties, and in Section
6
we use it to define the global regular orbital integral and prove Theorem
1.1
. In Section
7
we prove the local singular transfer theorem. In Section
8
, we give a Fourier-Jacobi analogue of the results. In the appendix, we extend the asymptotic of the modified kernel in
[
3
]
*Theorem 3.3.7.1 to general parabolic subgroups and its Levi subgroups.

Acknowledgement

The author would like to thank Wei Zhang for the suggestion of this problem and helpful discussion on many parts of this article, he is also very grateful to Daniel Disegni, Ziqi Guo, Linli Shi, Yiyang Wang, Hang Xue, Hongfeng Zhang, and Zhiyu Zhang for helpful comments and discussions.

2.
Preliminaries

2.1.
General notation

•

We fix an integer
n>0
throughout this article.

•

For a matrix
A
, we write
A^{t}
for the transpose of
A
.

•

Let
F
be a number field and let
v
be place of
F
, we write
F_{v}
for the completion of
F
at the place
v
. In general, if
\mathtt{S}
is a finite set of places of
F
, we write
F_{\mathtt{S}}:=\prod_{v\in\mathtt{S}}F_{v}
and
\mathbb{A}^{\mathtt{S}}_{F}
for the restricted product
\prod_{v\not\in\mathrm{S}}^{\prime}F_{v}
. We also write
F_{\infty}:F\otimes_{\mathbb{Q}}\mathbb{R}
. If
E/F
is a finite extension, we put
E_{\mathtt{S}}:=E\otimes_{F}F_{\mathtt{S}}
.

•

Let
f
and
g
be positive functions on a set
X
, we write
f\ll g
if there exists
C>0
such that
f(x)\leq Cg(x)
for all
x\in X
. We write
f\ll_{c_{1},\cdots,c_{r}}g
if the constant
C
depends on the parameters
c_{1},\cdots,c_{r}
.

•

Let
r
be a real number. We denote by
\mathcal{H}_{<r}
(resp.
\mathcal{H}_{>r}
) the left half plane
\{z\in\mathbb{C}\mid\mathrm{Re}(s)<r\}
(resp. right half plane
\{z\in\mathbb{C}\mid\mathrm{Re}(s)>r\}
).

•

For a vector space
V
over a field
k
, we write
V^{*}:=\Hom_{k}(V,F)
for the dual space of
V
. We denote by
k^{n}
and
k_{n}
the
n
dimensional column/row vectors respectively.

•

Let
G
be a reductive group over a field
k
of characteristic 0, and assume that
G
acts on a finite type affine
k
-scheme
X
. Let
X/G
be the GIT quotient and let
b\in(X/G)(k)
, we denote the fiber of
b
under the quotient map
XX/G
by
X_{b}
, which is a closed subscheme of
X
.

•

Let
R
be a ring and assume that we have a homomorphism
\chi
from
R
to some abelian group
A
. By an abuse of notation, we also denote the homomorphism
\chi\circ\det
from
\GL_{n}(R)
to
A
by
\chi
.

2.2.
Algebraic groups

In this subsection, we follow the notations in
[
3
]
where the reader can find more details there. Let
F
be a number field and let
G
be an algebraic group over
F
, we write
\mathfrak{g}_{\infty}
for the Lie algebra of the Lie group
G(F_{\infty})
, and let
\mathcal{U}(\mathfrak{g}_{\infty})
be the universal enveloping algebra of
\mathfrak{g}_{\infty}
. We denote by
[G]:=G(F)\backslash G(\mathbb{A})
the adèlic quotient of
G
. We fix the Tamagawa measure
dg
on
G(\mathbb{A})
as described in
[
3
]
*Section 2.3.

For the remainder of this subsection, we assume that
G
is connected and reductive. Fix a maximal split torus
A_{0}
of
G
and a minimal parabolic subgroup
P_{0}
containing
A_{0}
. A parabolic subgroup
P
of
G
is called standard if
P\supset P_{0}
, and it is called semi-standard if
P\supset A_{0}
. Let
P
be a semi-standard parabolic subgroup of
G
, then
P
has a unique Levi decomposition
P=M_{P}N_{P}
such that
M_{P}\supset A_{0}
, this is called the standard Levi decomposition. When we say Levi decomposition of a semi-standard parabolic subgroup, we will always mean the standard Levi decomposition.

Let
W
be the Weyl group of
(G,A_{0})
, that is the quotient by
M_{0}(F)
of the normalizer of
A_{0}
in
G(F)
.

For a semi-standard parabolic subgroup
P
of
G
, define

\mathfrak{a}_{P}^{*}:=X^{*}(P)\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}\mathbb{R},\quad\mathfrak{a}_{P}:=\Hom_{\mathbb{Z}}(X^{*}(P),\mathbb{R}).

We endow
\mathfrak{a}_{P}
with the Haar measure such that the lattice
X^{*}(P)
has covolume 1.

Let
\mathfrak{a}_{0}:=\mathfrak{a}_{P_{0}}
and
\mathfrak{a}_{0}^{*}:=\mathfrak{a}_{P_{0}}^{*}
. For
P\subset Q
, there is a natural direct sum decomposition

\mathfrak{a}_{P}=\mathfrak{a}_{P}^{Q}\oplus\mathfrak{a}_{Q},\,\mathfrak{a}^{*}_{P}=\mathfrak{a}_{P}^{Q,*}\oplus\mathfrak{a}_{Q}^{*}.

In particular, we will view
\mathfrak{a}_{P}
(resp.
\mathfrak{a}_{P}^{*}
) as a subspace of
\mathfrak{a}_{0}
(resp.
\mathfrak{a}_{0}^{*}
). For
P\subset Q
, we put

\epsilon_{P}^{Q}:=(-1)^{\dim\mathfrak{a}_{P}-\dim\mathfrak{a}_{Q}}.

If
Q=G
, we put
\mathfrak{a}_{P}^{Q}=\mathfrak{a}_{P}
.

Let
P_{0}^{\prime}
be a semi-standard minimal parabolic subgroup containing
P
, we denote by
\Delta_{P_{0}^{\prime}}^{P}
the set of simple roots under the
A_{0}
action, relative to
P_{0}^{\prime}
, on the Lie algebra of
N_{P}
. We put
\Delta_{P_{0}^{\prime}}:=\Delta_{P_{0}^{\prime}}^{G}
. For semi-standard parabolic subgroups
P\subset Q
, define
\Delta_{P}^{Q}
to be the image of
\Delta_{P_{0}^{\prime}}^{Q}\setminus\Delta_{P_{0}^{\prime}}^{P}
by the projection map
\mathfrak{a}_{0}^{*}\mathfrak{a}_{P}^{*}
. We also have a set of coroots
\Delta_{P}^{Q,\vee}
, the set of weights
\widehat{\Delta}_{P}^{Q}
, and the set of coweights
\widehat{\Delta}_{P}^{Q,\vee}
. Let
\rho_{P}\in\mathfrak{a}_{P}^{*}
be the half of the sum of the roots of the action of
A_{P}
on
N_{P}
.

For any semi-standard parabolic subgroup
P
, and for any
T\in\mathfrak{a}_{0}
, we define a point
T_{P}\in\mathfrak{a}_{P}
, such that for any
w\in W
such that
wP_{0}w^{-1}\subset P
, the point
T_{P}
is the projection of
w\cdot T
on
\mathfrak{a}_{P}
under the projection map
\mathfrak{a}_{0}\mathfrak{a}_{P}
.

Let
\tau_{P}^{Q}
be the characteristic function of

\{X\in\mathfrak{a}_{0}\mid\langle\alpha,X\rangle>0,\text{ for all }\alpha\in\Delta_{P}^{Q}\},

and put
\mathfrak{a}_{0}^{+}:=\{X\in\mathfrak{a}_{0}\mid\tau_{P_{0}}^{G}(X)=1\}
.
We also write
\widehat{\tau}_{P}^{Q}
for the characteristic function of

\{X\in\mathfrak{a}_{0}\mid\langle\varpi,X\rangle>0,\text{ for all }\varpi\in\widehat{\Delta}_{P}^{Q}\}.

For a semi-standard parabolic subgroup
P
of
G
, we put

[G]_{P}:=N_{P}(\mathbb{A})M_{P}(F)\backslash G(\mathbb{A}).

We fix a norm
\|\cdot\|
on
G(\mathbb{A})
as in
[
7
]
*Appendix A. It induces a norm on
[G]_{P}
by

\|g\|_{P}:=\inf_{\gamma\in N_{P}(\mathbb{A})M_{P}(F)}\|\gamma g\|.

There is a notion of weight functions on
[G]_{P}
as in
[
3
]
*Subsection 2.4.3. In particular, for any
\alpha\in\mathfrak{a}_{0}^{*}
, there is a weight
d_{P,\alpha}
on
[G]_{P}
.

We denote by
A_{G}^{\infty}
the neutral component of real points of the maximal split central torus of
\mathrm{Res}_{F/\mathbb{Q}}G
. For a semi-standard parabolic subgroup
P
of
G
, let
A_{P}^{\infty}:=A_{M_{P}}^{\infty}
. We also define
A_{0}^{\infty}:=A_{P_{0}}^{\infty}=A_{M_{0}}^{\infty}
.

We fix a maximal compact subgroup
K
of
G(\mathbb{A})
, which is in good position with
P_{0}
. Hence we have the Iwasawa decomposition
G(\mathbb{A})=P(\mathbb{A})K
for all semi-standard parabolic subgroup
P
of
G
. The map

H_{P}:P(\mathbb{A})\mathfrak{a}_{P},\,p\left(\chi\log\lvert\chi(g)\rvert\right)

extends to
G(\mathbb{A})
, by requiring it trivial on
K
. The map
H_{P}
induces an isomorphism
A_{P}^{\infty}\cong\mathfrak{a}_{P}
, we endow
A_{P}^{\infty}
with the Haar measure such that this isomorphism is measure-preserving. Let
G(\mathbb{A})_{P}^{1}
be the preimage of 0 under
H_{P}
.
G(\mathbb{A})^{1}:=G(\mathbb{A})^{1}_{G}
is a subgroup of
G(\mathbb{A})
. For general
P
, the product in
G(\mathbb{A})
induces a direct product decomposition (of sets)

G(\mathbb{A})=G(\mathbb{A})_{P}^{1}\times A_{P}^{\infty}.

The subset
G(\mathbb{A})_{P}^{1}
descends to a subset of
[G]_{P}
, we denote this by
[G]_{P}^{1}
.

Let
\mathfrak{X}(G)
be the set of cuspidal datum of
G
, see
[
3
]
*Section 2.9. Let
P
be a semi-standard parabolic subgroup, we have the following Langlands decomposition of the
L^{2}
space:

(2.1)

L^{2}([G]_{P})=\bigoplus_{\chi\in\mathfrak{X}(G)}L^{2}_{\chi}([G]_{P}).

Fix a norm
\|\cdot\|
on
\mathfrak{a}_{0}
. We say
T\in\mathfrak{a}_{0}
is
sufficiently positive
, if
there exists
C>0
and
\varepsilon>0
such that

\inf_{\alpha\in\Delta_{0}}\langle\alpha,T\rangle\geq\max\{C,\varepsilon\|T\|\}.

For
T\in\mathfrak{a}_{0}
and a semi-standard parabolic subgroup
P
of
G
, let
F^{P}(\cdot,T)
be the function on
[G]_{P}
introduced in
[
4
]
*Subsubsection 2.3.3. It is a characteristic function of a subset of
[G]_{P}
, and this subset is compact modulo center.

2.3.
Function spaces

Let
F
be a number field and let
G
be an algebraic group over
F
. We say a function
f:G(\mathbb{A})\mathbb{C}
is smooth, if there exists a compact open subgroup
J\subset G(\mathbb{A}_{f})
such that
f
is invariant under the right translation by
J
, and for all
g_{f}\in G(\mathbb{A}_{f})
, the function
g_{\infty}f(g_{f}g_{\infty})
is smooth on Lie group
G(F_{\infty})
. We say that the function on
[G]_{P}
is smooth if it pulls back to a smooth function on
G(\mathbb{A})
.

Let
C
be a compact subset of
G(\mathbb{A}_{f})
and let
J\subset G(\mathbb{A}_{f})
be a compact subgroup. Let
\mathcal{S}(G(\mathbb{A}),C,J)
be the space of smooth functions
f:G(\mathbb{A})\mathbb{C}
which are biinvariant by
J
, supported in the subset
C\times G(F_{\infty})
and such that

\|f\|_{N,X,Y}=\sup_{g\in G(\mathbb{A})}\lvert(\mathrm{R}(X)\mathrm{L}(Y)f)(g)\rvert<\infty

for any
N>1
and
X,Y\in\mathcal{U}(\mathfrak{g}_{\infty})
. Let
\mathcal{S}(G(\mathbb{A}))
be a union of all
\mathcal{S}(G(\mathbb{A}),C,J)
, it carries a natural strict LF topology. In particular, if
V
is a vector space over
F
, we have a space
\mathcal{S}(V(\mathbb{A}))
of Schwartz function on
V(\mathbb{A})
.

Let
G
be a reductive group over
F
, and let
P
be a semi-standard parabolic subgroup of
G
. We denote by
\mathcal{S}^{0}([G]_{P})
the space of the rapidly decreasing measurable functions on
[G]_{P}
. In general, let
X\subset[G]_{P}
be a measurable subset, let
\mathcal{S}^{0}(X)
be the set of measurable functions
f
on
X
such that

\|f\|_{\infty,N}:=\sup_{x\in X}\|x\|_{P}^{N}\lvert f(x)\rvert

is finite for all
N
. Equipped with the semi-norms
\|\cdot\|_{\infty,N}
,
\mathcal{S}^{0}(X)
is a Frechét space.

We denote by
\mathcal{T}^{0}([G]_{P})
the space of complex Radon measure
\varphi
on
[G]_{P}
such that there exists
N>0
making the integral

\int_{[G]_{P}}\|x\|_{P}^{-N}\lvert\varphi(x)\rvert

finite. It carries a natural LF topology.

Let
w
be a weight on
[G]_{P}
, we write
\mathcal{S}_{w}([G]_{P})
for the LF space of weighted Schwartz functions on
[G]_{P}
. It consists of smooth functions
f
on
[G]_{P}
such that there exists
N>0
, such that for all
X\in\mathcal{U}(\mathfrak{g}_{\infty})
and
r>0
,

\|f\|_{\infty,X,r,N}:=\sup_{x\in[G]_{P}}\lvert R(X)f(x)\rvert w(x)^{r}\|x\|_{P}^{-N}<\infty.

In particular, if
w=\|\cdot\|_{P}
,
\mathcal{S}_{w}([G]_{P}):=\mathcal{S}([G]_{P})
is the space of Schwartz functions on
[G]_{P}
, and if
w=1
,
\mathcal{S}_{w}([G]_{P}):=\mathcal{T}([G]_{P})
is the space of functions of uniform moderate growth on
[G]_{P}
. For more details on these global function spaces, see
[
3
]
*Section 2.5.

Now let
F
be a local field of characteristic 0, and let
G
be an algebraic group over
F
, fix a norm
\|\cdot\|
on
G(F)
as in
[
16
]
*Section 18. We denote by
\mathcal{S}(G(F))
the space of Schwartz function on
F
. If
F
is non-archimedean,
\mathcal{S}(G(F))
consists of compactly supported and locally constant function, if
F
is archimedean, it consists of smooth functions
f
on
G(F)
such that for any
X\in\mathcal{U}(\mathfrak{g}_{\infty})
and
N\geq 0

\|f\|_{X,N}:=\sup_{g\in G(F)}\|x\|^{N}\lvert\mathrm{R}(X)f(x)\rvert<\infty.

The space
\mathcal{S}(G(F))
carries a natural Fréchet topology when
F
is archimedean, and we endow
\mathcal{S}(G(F))
with the finest locally convex topology if
F
is non-Archimedean.

2.4.
The symmetric space
\mathrm{S}
and its variants

From now on until the end of Section
2
, we fix a field
F
of characteristic 0 and let
E
be a quadratic étale algebra over
F
. Let
\mathtt{c}
be the unique nontrivial evolution of
E
that fixes
F
. For an
F
algebra
R
, we will write
aa^{\mathtt{c}}
for the involution on
R\otimes_{F}E
induced by
\mathtt{c}
. For
k\geq 1
, write
\GL_{n}:=\GL_{n,F}
.

Let
\mathrm{S}
be the algebraic variety over
F
such that

\mathrm{S}(R)=\{x\in\GL_{n+1}(R\otimes E)\mid xx^{\mathtt{c}}=1\},

where
1
stands for the identity matrix of size
(n+1)\times(n+1)
.

We regard the group
\GL_{n}
as a subgroup of
\GL_{n+1}
via the embedding
g\begin{pmatrix}g&\\
&1\end{pmatrix}
. The group
\GL_{n}
has a right action on
\mathrm{S}
by

x\cdot g=g^{-1}xg.

There is a
\GL_{n}
-equivariant isomorphism

\nu:\mathrm{Res}_{E/F}\GL_{n+1,E}/\GL_{n+1}\cong\mathrm{S},\quad ggg^{-1,\mathtt{c}}

where
\GL_{n}
acts on
\mathrm{Res}_{E/F}\GL_{n+1,E}/\GL_{n+1}
by left translation. Let
\mathrm{B}:=\mathrm{S}/\GL_{n}
be the GIT quotient.

Note that there is a natural identification
\mathrm{G}^{\prime}/\mathrm{H}_{1}\times\mathrm{H}_{2,n+1}\cong\mathrm{Res}_{E/F}\GL_{n+1,E}/\GL_{n+1,F}
induced by the map
(g_{n},g_{n+1})g_{n}^{-1}g_{n+1}
, therefore
\mathrm{B}
can also be identified with the GIT quotient
\mathrm{G}^{\prime}/\mathrm{H}_{1}\times\mathrm{H}_{2}
. More concretely, the map

(2.2)

\alpha:\mathrm{G}^{\prime}\mathrm{S},\quad(g_{n},g_{n+1})\nu(g_{n}^{-1}g_{n+1})

identifies
\mathrm{G}^{\prime}/\mathrm{H}_{1}\times\mathrm{H}_{2}
with
\mathrm{B}
.

Let
\sigma\in E^{\times}
with
\sigma\sigma^{\mathtt{c}}=1
, and let
\mathrm{S}^{\sigma}
be the Zariski open subset of
\mathrm{S}
consisting of
x\in\mathrm{S}
such that the matrix
x-\sigma\cdot 1
is invertible.

We write
\gl_{n+1}:=\gl_{n+1,F}
for the vector space of
(n+1)\times(n+1)
matrices with coefficient in
F
. The group
\GL_{n}
has a right action on
\gl_{n+1}
given by

A\cdot g:=g^{-1}Ag.

Let
\mathcal{B}:=\gl_{n+1}/\GL_{n}
be the GIT quotient. From
[
23
]
*Lemma 3.1, we see that
\mathcal{B}
is isomorphic to the affine space
\mathbf{A}^{2n+1}
of dimension
2n+1
.

Fix
\tau\in E
such that
\tau^{\mathtt{c}}=-\tau
, let
\gl_{n+1}^{\tau}
be the Zariski open subset of
\gl_{n+1}
consists of
Y\in\gl_{n+1}
such that the matrix
Y-\tau\cdot 1
is invertible.

The Cayley map

(2.3)

\mathfrak{c}_{\sigma}:\gl_{n+1}^{\tau}\mathrm{S}^{\sigma},\quad Y-\sigma\frac{1+\tau^{-1}Y}{1-\tau^{-1}Y}

defines a
\GL_{n}
-equivariant isomorphism between
\gl_{n+1}^{\tau}
and
\mathrm{S}^{\sigma}
. The open subsets
\gl_{n+1}^{\tau}
and
\mathrm{S}^{\sigma}
is
\GL_{n}
invariant and descends to open subsets of
\mathcal{B}^{\tau}
of
\mathcal{B}
and
\mathrm{B}^{\sigma}
of
\mathrm{B}
respectively, and
\mathfrak{c}_{\sigma}
induces an isomorphism
\mathcal{B}^{\tau}\mathrm{B}^{\sigma}
which we will still denote by
\mathfrak{c}_{\sigma}
.

Write an element
x
of
\mathrm{S}
as

x=\begin{pmatrix}A&b\\
c&d\end{pmatrix},

where
A,b,c,d
has size
n\times n,n\times 1,1\times n,1\times 1
respectively. We put

(2.4)

\Delta^{+}(x)=\det(b,Ab,A^{2}b,\cdots,A^{n-1}b),\quad\Delta^{-}(x)=\det\begin{pmatrix}c\\
cA\\
\cdots\\
cA^{n-1}\end{pmatrix}.

Let
\mathrm{S}_{+}
(resp.
\mathrm{S}_{-}
) be the Zariski open subset of
\mathrm{S}
where
\Delta^{+}
(resp.
\Delta^{-}
) is non vanishing, and let
\mathrm{G}^{\prime}_{+}
and
\mathrm{G}^{\prime}_{-}
be the preimage of
\mathrm{S}_{+}
and
\mathrm{S}_{-}
under the map
\alpha
(see (
2.2
)) respectively. Let
e_{n+1}
be the column vector
(0,\cdots,0,1)^{t}
of size
(n+1)\times 1
, we can directly check that

(2.5)

\Delta^{+}(x)=(-1)^{n}\det(x,xe_{n+1},\cdots,x^{n}e_{n+1}),\quad\Delta^{-}(x)=(-1)^{n}\det\begin{pmatrix}e_{n+1}^{t}\\
\cdots\\
e_{n+1}^{t}x^{n}\end{pmatrix}.

Similarly, for
X=\begin{pmatrix}A&v\\
u&d\end{pmatrix}\in\gl_{n+1}
with
A\in\gl_{n},v\in F^{n},u\in F_{n},d\in F
, define

\delta^{+}(X)=\det(v,Av,\cdots,A^{n-1}v),\quad\delta^{-}(X)=\det\begin{pmatrix}u\\
uA\\
\cdots\\
uA^{n-1}\end{pmatrix}.

Let
\gl_{n+1,+}
(resp.
\gl_{n+1,-}
) be the Zariski open subset of
\gl_{n+1}
where
\delta^{+}
(resp.
\delta^{-}
) is non-vanishing. We have

(2.6)

\delta^{+}(X)=(-1)^{n}\det(e_{n+1},\cdots,X^{n}e_{n+1}),\quad\delta^{-}(X)=(-1)^{n}\det\begin{pmatrix}e_{n+1}^{t}\\
e_{n+1}^{t}X\\
\cdots\\
e_{n+1}^{t}X^{n}\end{pmatrix}.

Direct computation shows that for
X\in\gl^{\tau}_{n+1}(F)
, we have

(2.7)

\Delta^{+}(\mathfrak{c}_{\sigma}(X))=(-2\sigma\tau^{-1})^{\frac{n(n+1)}{2}}\det(1-\tau^{-1}Y)^{-n}\delta^{+}(X).

and

(2.8)

\Delta^{-}(\mathfrak{c}_{\sigma}(X))=(-2\sigma\tau^{-1})^{\frac{n(n+1)}{2}}\det(1-\tau^{-1}Y)^{-n}\delta^{-}(X).

As a consequence, for any
Y\in\gl_{n+1}^{\tau}(F)
, we have

(2.9)

\Delta^{+}(\mathfrak{c}_{\sigma}(Y))\text{ (resp. }\Delta^{-}(\mathfrak{c}_{\sigma}(Y)))\neq 0\iff\delta^{+}(Y)\text{ (resp. }\delta^{-}(Y))\neq 0

For a vector space
V
over
F
, put
\widetilde{\gl_{V}}:=\gl(V)\times V\times V^{*}
, it carries a right action by
\GL(F)
:

(A,v,u)\cdot g=(g^{-1}Ag,g^{-1}v,ug).

We write
\widetilde{\gl_{n}}:=\widetilde{\gl_{F^{n}}}
. Note that we have a
\GL_{n}
-equivariant isomorphism

(2.10)

\gl_{n+1}\cong\widetilde{\gl_{n}}\times F,\quad\begin{pmatrix}A&v\\
u&d\end{pmatrix}\left((A,v,u),d\right)

where
F
is endowed with the trivial action of
\GL_{n}
. Thus, we can view
\widetilde{\gl_{n}}
as a variant of
\gl_{n+1}
under the
\GL_{n}
-action.

Let
\mathcal{A}_{V}=\widetilde{\gl_{V}}/\GL(V)
be the GIT quotient. We put
\mathcal{A}:=\mathcal{A}_{F^{n}}
, by (
2.10
), we have an identification
\mathcal{B}\cong\mathcal{A}\times F
. If
\dim V=d
,
\mathcal{A}_{V}
can be identified with the affine space
\mathbf{A}^{2d}
, where the quotient map
q:\widetilde{\gl_{V}}\mathcal{A}_{V}
is given by

(2.11)

(A,u,v)\left((\mathrm{Trace}\wedge^{i}A)_{1\leq i\leq d},(uA^{i}v)_{0\leq i\leq d-1}\right).

We see from this description that when
\dim V_{1}=\dim V_{2}
,
\mathcal{A}_{V_{1}}
and
\mathcal{A}_{V_{2}}
are canonically identified.

For
X=(A,v,u)\in\widetilde{\gl_{n}}
, we put

\delta^{+}(X)=\det(v,Av,A^{2}v,\cdots,A^{n-1}v),\quad\delta^{-}(X)=\det(u,uA,uA^{2},\cdots,uA^{n-1}).

Note that the definition of
\delta^{\pm}
depends on the choice of basis of
F^{n}
, so there is no canonical definition of
\delta^{\pm}
on
\widetilde{\gl_{V}}
for a general vector space
V
. Let
\widetilde{\gl_{V}}_{,+}
(resp.
\widetilde{\gl_{V}}_{,-}
) be the Zariski open subset of
\widetilde{\gl_{V}}
where
\{v,Av,\cdots,A^{n-1}v\}
form a basis of
V
(resp.
\{u,uA,\cdots,uA^{n-1}\}
form a basis of
V^{*}
). If
V=F^{n}
, this is the open subset of
\widetilde{\gl_{n}}
where
\delta^{+}
(resp.
\delta^{-}
) is non-vanishing.

Under the map (
2.10
), we have

\gl_{n+1,+}=\widetilde{\gl_{n}}_{,+}\times F,\quad\gl_{n+1,-}=\widetilde{\gl_{n}}_{,-}\times F.

Recall that if we have a reductive group
G
over
F
acting on a finite-type
F
-scheme
X
, then
x\in X(F)
is called regular if its stabilizer
G_{x}
has minimal dimension, and
x\in X(F)
is called semisimple if its (scheme-theoretic) orbit is Zariski closed. We denote by
X_{\mathrm{reg}}
(resp.
X_{\mathrm{rs}}
) the Zariski open subset of
X
consisting of regular (resp. regular semisimple) elements. In this article, we are mainly interested in the following four cases of
X
and
G
.

(2.12)

\displaystyle X

\displaystyle=\mathrm{G}^{\prime},

\displaystyle G=\mathrm{H}_{1}\times\mathrm{H}_{2};

\displaystyle X

\displaystyle=\mathrm{S},

\displaystyle G=\GL_{n};

\displaystyle X

\displaystyle=\gl_{n+1},

\displaystyle G=\GL_{n};

\displaystyle X

\displaystyle=\widetilde{\gl_{n}},

\displaystyle G=\GL_{n}.

In these cases, a rational point is regular if and only if its stabilizer is trivial, and it is known that (see e.g.
[
23
]
)
X_{\mathrm{rs}}=X_{+}\cap X_{-}
.

For
g\in\GL_{n}
, and
x\in\mathrm{S}(F)
(resp.
Y\in\gl_{n+1}(F)
or
Y\in\widetilde{\gl_{n}}(F)
), note that
\Delta^{\pm}(x\cdot g)=\det g\cdot\Delta^{\pm}(x)
(resp.
\delta^{\pm}(Y\cdot g)=\det g\cdot\delta^{\pm}(Y)
). Therefore for
X
and
G
in these four cases (
2.12
), we have
X_{+}\cup X_{-}\subset X_{\mathrm{reg}}
. We remark that for
n\geq 2
, this inclusion is strict.

2.5.
Descent on
\widetilde{\gl_{n}}

Let
\mathfrak{z}_{V}:=\{(\lambda\cdot\mathrm{id},0,0)\mid\lambda\in F\}\subset\widetilde{\gl_{V}}
. Then
\mathfrak{z}_{V}
is the center of
\widetilde{\gl_{V}}
under the
\GL(V)
action (i.e. the set of fixed points). The composition
\mathfrak{z}_{V}\hookrightarrow\widetilde{\gl_{n}}\mathcal{A}
induces a closed embedding
\mathfrak{z}_{V}\hookrightarrow\mathcal{A}
. We call the image of this embedding the center of
\mathcal{A}
, an element lying in the center will be called central.

We now recall the descent construction on
\widetilde{\gl_{n}}
, cf.
[
23
]
*Section 3.2 or
[
11
]
*Section 3.4. It allows us to approximate any element
a\in\mathcal{A}(F)
by regular semisimple elements and central elements in smaller GIT quotients. The construction consists of two steps: first approximate
a
by
(a_{0},a^{0})
where
a_{0}
is regular semisimple and
a^{0}
is close to being central, and then approximate
a^{0}
by central elements
(a_{1},\cdots,a_{k})
. We now describe them in detail.

Step 1

For any vector space
V
over
F
, We have a stratification of
\mathcal{A}_{V}
as described in
[
11
]
*Paragraph 3.1.4. For an integer
r
with
1\leq r\leq\dim V
, and
X=(A,v,u)\in\widetilde{\gl_{V}}(F)
, let

(2.13)

d_{r}(X)=\det(uA^{i+j-2}v)_{1\leq i,j\leq r}.

Let
\widetilde{\gl_{V}}^{(r)}
be the locally closed subscheme of
\widetilde{\gl_{V}}
such that
d_{r}\neq 0
but
d_{s}=0
for
s>r
. The subscheme
\widetilde{\gl_{V}}^{(r)}
is
\GL(V)
-invariant and descends to a locally closed subscheme
\mathcal{A}_{V}^{(r)}
of
\mathcal{A}_{V}
. Note that
\mathcal{A}_{V}^{(\dim V)}
is the open subset
\mathcal{A}_{V,\mathrm{rs}}
consisting of regular semisimple elements.

Choose an isomorphism
i:F^{n}\cong F^{r}\oplus F^{n-r}
, it determines an embedding

(2.14)

\displaystyle\begin{split}\iota:\widetilde{\gl_{r}}^{(r)}\times\widetilde{\gl_{n-r}}&\hookrightarrow\widetilde{\gl_{n}},\\
(A_{1},v_{1},u_{1}),(A_{2},v_{2},u_{2})&\left(\begin{pmatrix}A_{1}&v^{\prime}u_{2}\\
v_{2}u^{\prime}&A_{2}\end{pmatrix},v_{1},u_{1}\right)\end{split}

where
u^{\prime}\in F_{r}
satisfies

u^{\prime}A_{1}^{i}v_{1}=\begin{cases}0&\text{ if }0\leq i<r-1\\
1&\text{ if }i=r-1.\end{cases}

and
v^{\prime}\in F^{r}
satisfies

u_{1}A_{1}^{i}v^{\prime}=\begin{cases}0&\text{ if }0\leq i<r-1\\
1&\text{ if }i=r-1.\end{cases}

The map (
2.14
) descends to an isomorphism of varieties

(2.15)

\iota:\mathcal{A}_{F^{r}}^{(r)}\times\mathcal{A}_{F^{n-r}}\cong\mathcal{A}^{(\geq r)}

where
\mathcal{A}^{(\geq r)}:=\cup_{k\geq r}\mathcal{A}^{(k)}
is an open subset of
\mathcal{A}
. The isomorphism
\iota
is independent of the choice of
i
and restricts to an isomorphism

(2.16)

\mathcal{A}_{F^{r}}^{(r)}\times\mathcal{A}_{F^{n-r}}^{(s)}\cong\mathcal{A}^{(r+s)}.

In particular, for
a\in\mathcal{A}^{(r)}(F)
, there exists unique
(a_{0},a^{0})\in\mathcal{A}_{F^{r}}^{(r)}\times\mathcal{A}_{F^{n-r}}^{(0)}
such that
\iota(a_{0},a^{0})=a
. Note that
a_{0}
is regular semisimple in
\mathcal{A}_{F^{r}}(F)
.

Step 2

Fix
a\in\mathcal{A}^{(r)}(F)
. As we discussed in Step 1,
a
can be uniquely written as
\iota(a_{0},a^{0})
. Assume that
a^{0}
is represented by
(A,v,u)\in\widetilde{\gl_{F^{n-r}}}(F)
, then
uA^{i}v=0
for any
i\geq 0
(see
[
11
]
*3.1.4.1). We therefore see that
a^{0}
is determined by the characteristic polynomial
P
of
A
. Assume
P
decomposes in the polynomial ring
F[x]
as
P=P_{1}^{n_{1}}\cdots P_{k}^{n_{k}}
, where
P_{i}
are distinct and irreducible. For
1\leq i\leq k
, put
F_{i}=F[x]/P_{i}(x)
, which is a finite extension of
F
. Put

(2.17)

H_{i}=\mathrm{Res}_{F_{i}/F}\GL_{n_{i},F_{i}},\quad H^{0}=\prod_{i=1}^{k}H_{i},\quad\widetilde{\mathfrak{h}}_{i}=\mathrm{Res}_{F_{i}/F}\widetilde{\gl_{F_{i}^{n_{i}}}},\quad\widetilde{\mathfrak{h}}^{0}=\prod_{i=1}^{k}\widetilde{\mathfrak{h}}_{i}.

Let
\mathcal{A}_{H^{0}}
be the GIT quotient
\widetilde{\mathfrak{h}^{0}}/H^{0}
, which can be identified with
\prod_{i=1}^{k}\mathcal{A}_{i}
with

\mathcal{A}_{i}=\mathrm{Res}_{F_{i}/F}(\widetilde{\gl_{F^{i}}}/\GL_{n_{i},F_{i}}).

Choose an isomorphism
i^{0}:\bigoplus_{i=1}^{k}\mathrm{Res}_{F_{i}/F}F_{i}^{n_{i}}\cong F^{n-r}
. Then we get the embedding

(2.18)

\iota_{H^{0}}:H^{0}\hookrightarrow\GL_{n-r},\,(g_{i})\oplus g_{i}

and

(2.19)

\iota_{\mathfrak{h}^{0}}:\widetilde{\mathfrak{h}^{0}}\hookrightarrow\widetilde{\gl_{F^{n-r}}},\,(A_{i},v_{i},u_{i})(\oplus A_{i},\oplus v_{i},\oplus u_{i}),

where we have used the identification.

\Hom_{F_{i}}(F_{i}^{n_{i}},F_{i})\xrightarrow{\sim}\Hom_{F}(F_{i}^{n_{i}},F),\quad f(v\mathrm{tr}_{F_{i}/F}f(v))

The embedding (
2.19
) induces a natural map
\mathcal{A}_{H^{0}}\mathcal{A}_{F^{n-r}}
and this map is independent of the choice of
i^{0}
.

Let
\widetilde{\mathfrak{h}^{0}}^{\prime}
denote the open subset of
\widetilde{\mathfrak{h}^{0}}
consisting of
(A_{i},u_{i},v_{i})
such that
\det Q_{i}(A_{j})\neq 0
, where
Q_{i}
is the characteristic polynomial of
A_{i}
over
F
.
\widetilde{\mathfrak{h}^{0}}^{\prime}
descends to an open subset
\mathcal{A}^{\prime}_{H^{0}}
of
\mathcal{A}_{H^{0}}
.

We put

H_{0}=\GL_{r},\quad H=H_{0}\times H^{0},\quad\widetilde{\mathfrak{h}_{0}}=\widetilde{\gl_{F^{r}}},\quad\widetilde{\mathfrak{h}}=\widetilde{\mathfrak{h}_{0}}\times\widetilde{\mathfrak{h}^{0}}.

The GIT quotient
\mathcal{A}_{H}:=\widetilde{\mathfrak{h}}/H
can be identified with
\mathcal{A}_{F^{r}}\times\mathcal{A}_{H^{0}}
. The isomorphisms
i
and
i^{0}
determine the maps
\iota_{\mathfrak{h}^{0}}
in (
2.19
) and
\iota
in (
2.14
), and in terms gives a map
\iota_{\mathfrak{h}}:\widetilde{\mathfrak{h}_{0}}^{(r)}\times\widetilde{\mathfrak{h}^{0}}\widetilde{\gl_{n}}
. Let
\widetilde{\mathfrak{h}}^{\prime}=\widetilde{\mathfrak{h}_{0}}^{(r)}\times\widetilde{\mathfrak{h}^{0}}^{\prime}
. Then
\widetilde{\mathfrak{h}}^{\prime}
is an open subset of
\widetilde{\mathfrak{h}}
, and descends to an open subset
\mathcal{A}_{H}^{\prime}
of
\mathcal{A}_{H}
. The map
\iota_{\mathfrak{h}}
restricts to a map
\widetilde{\mathfrak{h}}^{\prime}\widetilde{\gl_{V}}
, and descends to a map
\iota_{\mathcal{A}}:\mathcal{A}_{H}^{\prime}\mathcal{A}
. The map
\iota_{\mathcal{A}}
is independent of the isomorphisms
i
and
i^{0}
and by
[
23
]
*Appendix B (see also
[
11
]
*Subsection 6.4), the map
\iota_{\mathcal{A}}
is étale.

Let
\alpha_{i}
be the image of
T
in
F_{i}=F[T]/(P_{i})
, and let
a_{i}
be the image of
(\alpha_{i}\cdot\mathrm{id},0,0)
under the quotient map
\widetilde{\gl_{V_{i}}}\mathcal{A}_{i}
. Let
a_{H}:=(a_{0},a_{1},\cdots,a_{k})\in\mathcal{A}_{H}
. From the definition, we see that
\iota_{\mathcal{A}}(a_{H})=a
.

By
[
23
]
*Appendix B (see also
[
11
]
*Subsection 6.4), we have a Cartesian diagram

(2.20)

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\widetilde{\mathfrak{h}}^{\prime}\times^{H}\GL_{n}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\widetilde{\gl_{n}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{A}_{H}^{\prime}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{A}}

where

•

The right vertical map is the quotient map, and the left vertical map is induced by the quotient map
\widetilde{\mathfrak{h}^{\prime}}\mathcal{A}_{H}
, trivial on the second component.

•

The bottom horizontal map is
\iota_{\mathcal{A}}
, the top horizontal map sends
(X,g)
to
\iota_{\mathfrak{h}}(X)\cdot g
.

By the vanishing of Galois cohomology, we have

(2.21)

(\widetilde{\mathfrak{h}}^{\prime}\times^{H}\GL_{n})(F)=\widetilde{\mathfrak{h}}^{\prime}(F)\times^{H(F)}\GL_{n}(F).

Hence the map
\iota_{\mathfrak{h}}
induces a natural bijection between the
H(F)
-orbit of
\widetilde{\mathfrak{h}}_{a_{H}}
and the
\GL_{n}(F)
-orbit of
\widetilde{\gl_{n}}_{,a}
.

We finally remark that the above construction is compatible with base change. More precisely, if
K/F
is a field extension (not necessarily algebraic) and for
a\in\mathcal{A}_{V}(F)
, the above procedure gives
H,\widetilde{\mathfrak{h}}
and the diagram (
2.20
). If we regard
a
as an element of
\mathcal{A}_{V}(K)=\mathcal{A}_{V\otimes_{F}K}(K)
, assume that the above procedure gives
H_{K},\widetilde{\mathfrak{h}_{K}}
and the diagram (
2.20
) over
K
. Then one check easily that
H_{K}=H\times_{F}K
is the base change of
F
, and the diagram (
2.20
) over
K
is also a base change of the corresponding diagram over
F
. We will use this fact for
F
is a number field and
K=F_{v}
for a place
v
of
F
.

2.6.
Classification of regular orbits

In either of the four cases in (
2.12
), let
X/G
be the GIT quotient, and
q:XX/G
be the quotient map. For
b\in X/G(F)
, we denote by
X_{b}
the fiber of
b
(see Subsection
2.1
). We now use the results in Subsection
2.5
to classify the regular orbits. Let us first begin with some special regular orbits.

Lemma 2.1
.

In either of the four cases in (
2.12
), for any
b\in X/G(F)
,
G(F)
acts simply transitively on
X_{b}(F)\cap X_{+}(F)
and
X_{b}(F)\cap X_{-}(F)
. In other words, there exists a unique orbit
O^{+}(b)
(resp.
O^{-}(b)
) of
G(F)
action on
X_{b}(F)
such that this orbit is contained in
X_{+}(F)
(resp.
X_{-}(F)
).

Proof.

We prove the result for
X_{+}
, and the proof for
X_{-}
is then similar.

When
X=\widetilde{\gl_{n}}
, the lemma follows from
[
22
]
*Proposition 6.3
1
1

1

In
[
22
]
*Proposition 6.3 such result is only stated for local field, but the proof works for any field of characteristic 0.
. By (
2.10
), it holds for
X=\gl_{n+1}
. For
X=\mathrm{S}
, take
\sigma\in E
such that
\mathrm{Nm}_{E/F}(\sigma)=1
and
b\in\mathrm{B}^{\sigma}(F)
. By (
2.9
), as a set with
\GL_{n}(F)
action,
\mathrm{S}_{b}(F)\cap\mathrm{S}_{+}(F)
is isomorphic to
\gl_{n+1,\mathfrak{c}_{\sigma}^{-1}(b)}(F)\cap\gl_{n+1,+}(F)
, therefore the lemma holds for
X=\mathrm{S}
. Finally, since
\mathrm{G}^{\prime}/\mathrm{H}_{1}\times\mathrm{H}_{2,n+1}
is isomorphic as a
\GL_{n}
-variety to
\mathrm{S}
, the lemma holds for
X=\mathrm{G}^{\prime}
.
∎

If
b\in(X/G)(F)
is regular semisimple, then
O^{+}(b)=O^{-}(b)=X_{b}
(see
[
21
]
). We also have the following result

Lemma 2.2
.

For
X=\widetilde{\gl_{n}}
and
G=\GL_{n}
, and
a\in\mathcal{A}(F)
is central, then
O^{+}(a)
and
O^{-}(a)
are the only regular orbits of in
\widetilde{\gl_{n}}_{,a}
.

Proof.

Let
a
be the image of
(\lambda\cdot\mathrm{id},0,0)\in\widetilde{\gl_{n}}(F)
. If
\lambda=0
, then this is proved in
[
22
]
*Lemma 6.1. For general
\lambda
, we only need to note that
A(A-\lambda\cdot\mathrm{id},0,0)
gives a
\GL_{n}
equivariant bijection between
\widetilde{\gl_{n}}_{,a}
and
\widetilde{\gl_{n}}_{,0}
.
∎

If
a\in\mathcal{A}(F)
is central, the orbit
O^{+}(a)
and
O^{-}(a)
can be described explicitly. If
a
is the image of
(\lambda\cdot\mathrm{id},0,0)
then
O^{+}(a)
is the orbit of

(2.22)

Z^{+}_{\lambda}:=\left(\begin{pmatrix}\lambda&1&0&\cdots&0\\
0&\lambda&1&\cdots&0\\
0&0&\lambda&\cdots&0\\
\cdots&\cdots&\cdots&\cdots&\cdots\\
0&0&0&\cdots&\lambda\end{pmatrix},\begin{pmatrix}0\\
0\\
0\\
\cdots\\
1\end{pmatrix},0\right).

And
O^{-}(a)
is the orbit of

(2.23)

Z^{-}_{\lambda}:=\left(\begin{pmatrix}\lambda&0&\cdots&0&0\\
1&\lambda&\cdots&0&0\\
0&1&\cdots&0&0\\
\cdots&\cdots&\cdots&\cdots&\cdots\\
0&0&\cdots&1&\lambda\end{pmatrix},0,(0,\cdots,0,1)\right).

Now we classify general regular orbits. We first consider the case where
X=\widetilde{\gl_{n}}
and
G=\GL_{n}
. Let
a\in\mathcal{A}(F)
, we use our notation in Subsection
2.5
. We have field extensions
F_{i}(1\leq i\leq k)
of
F
and after choosing the isomorphism
i
and
i^{0}
, we associate
a\in\mathcal{A}(F)
with a map
\iota_{\mathfrak{h}}:\widetilde{\mathfrak{h}}\widetilde{\gl_{n}}
.

Let
\mathcal{E}
be the set of maps from the set
\{1,2,\cdots,k\}
to
\{+,-\}
. For
\varepsilon\in\mathcal{E}
, we write
\varepsilon_{i}:=\varepsilon(i)
.

Proposition 2.3
.

Pick any
X_{0}\in\widetilde{\mathfrak{h}_{0}}_{,a_{0}}(F)
. Then a complete set of representative of orbit of
\GL_{n}(F)
action on
\widetilde{\gl_{n}}_{,a}(F)\cap\widetilde{\gl_{n}}_{,\mathrm{reg}}(F)
is given by

(2.24)

\left\{\iota_{\mathfrak{h}}(X_{0},Z^{\varepsilon_{1}}_{\alpha_{1}},\cdots,Z_{\alpha_{k}}^{\varepsilon_{k}})\mid\varepsilon\in\mathcal{E}\right\},

where
\varepsilon
runs through every element in
\mathcal{E}
and we recall that
\alpha_{i}
is the image of
T
in
F_{i}=F[T]/(P_{i})
. Moreover

(1)

For each
\varepsilon\in\mathcal{E}
, the orbit of
\iota_{\mathfrak{h}}(X_{0},Z^{\varepsilon_{1}}_{\alpha_{1}},\cdots,Z^{\varepsilon_{k}}_{\alpha_{k}})
is independent of
X_{0}
and the choice of the isomorphisms
i
and
i^{0}
.

(2)

The orbit of
\iota_{\mathfrak{h}}(X_{0},Z_{\alpha_{1}}^{+},\cdots,Z_{\alpha_{k}}^{+})
(resp.
\iota_{\mathfrak{h}}(X_{0},Z_{\alpha_{1}}^{-},\cdots,Z_{\alpha_{k}}^{-})
) is
O^{+}(a)
(resp.
O^{-}(a)
).

Proof.

By the diagram (
2.20
) and the equality (
2.21
), the map
\iota_{\mathfrak{h}}
induces a
\GL_{n}(F)
-equivariant bijection

\widetilde{\mathfrak{h}}_{a_{H}}(F)\times^{H(F)}\GL_{n}(F)\widetilde{\gl_{n}}_{,a}(F),

therefore,
\iota_{\mathfrak{h}}
induces a bijection between the
H(F)
orbit on
\widetilde{\mathfrak{h}}_{a_{H}}(F)
and
\GL_{n}(F)
orbit on
\widetilde{\gl_{n}}_{{}_{a}}(F)
, and regular orbits corresponds to regular orbit. Since each component
a_{i}
of
a_{H}
is either regular semi-simple (
i=0
) or central (
i>0
), Lemma
2.2
implies that the regular orbits in
\widetilde{\gl_{n}}_{,a}(F)
are given by (
2.24
). Different choices of
i
and
i^{0}
will yield
\iota_{\mathfrak{h}}
composed with an
\GL_{n}(F)
action on
\widetilde{\gl_{n}}(F)
, which certainly does not affect the orbit, therefore (1) is proved. Part (2) follows from
[
11
]
*Proof of Lemme 3.2.2.2 and Lemme 3.4.1.1.
∎

For
\varepsilon\in\mathcal{E}
, we call a regular orbit in
\widetilde{\gl_{n}}_{,a}(F)
of
type

\varepsilon
, if it is the orbit of
\iota_{\mathfrak{h}}(X_{0},Z_{\alpha_{1}}^{\varepsilon_{1}},\cdots,Z_{\alpha_{k}}^{\varepsilon_{k}})
.

Corollary 2.4
.

In each of the cases in (
2.12
), for
b\in X/G(F)
, there are finitely many regular orbits of
G(F)
-action on
X_{b}(F)
. Indeed, the number of orbits is a power of 2.

Proof.

The case when
X=\widetilde{\gl_{n}}
is proved in Proposition
2.3
, the other cases are reduced to this case (see the proof Lemma
2.1
).
∎

3.
Coarse relative trace formulae

3.1.
Preliminaries

In this section, we denote by
E/F
a quadratic extension of number fields. Let
\mathtt{c}
be the non-trivial element in the Galois group
\Gal(E/F)
. For an
F
-algebra
R
,
\mathtt{c}
will induce an involution on
R\otimes_{F}E
, we denote this by
aa^{\mathtt{c}}
. Let
\mathbb{A}:=\mathbb{A}_{F}
and
\mathbb{A}_{E}
be the ring of adèles of
F
and
E
respectively.

Let
k\geq 1
be an integer, we write
\GL_{k}:=\GL_{k,F}
for the general linear group of rank
k
over
F
. Let
\mathrm{G}^{\prime}_{k}:=\mathrm{Res}_{E/F}\GL_{k,E}
and let
\mathrm{G}^{\prime}:=\mathrm{G}^{\prime}_{n}\times\mathrm{G}^{\prime}_{n+1}
.
\mathrm{G}^{\prime}
has two subgroups
\mathrm{H}_{1}
and
\mathrm{H}_{2}
as in (
1.1
). We put
\mathrm{H}_{2,n}=\GL_{n},\mathrm{H}_{2,n+1}=\GL_{n+1}
, they are both regarded as subgroups of
\mathrm{H}_{2}
. For an element
h_{2}\in\mathrm{H}_{2}
, we write
h_{2,n}
and
h_{2,n+1}
for its corresponding components. Let
K_{1}
and
K_{2}
be the standard maximal compact subgroup of
\mathrm{H}_{1}(\mathbb{A})
and
\mathrm{H}_{2}(\mathbb{A})
respectively.

For
k\geq 1
, let
B_{k}
be the upper triangular Borel subgroup of
\mathrm{G}^{\prime}_{k}
. We choose
B_{k}
as our fixed minimal parabolic subgroup of
\mathrm{G}^{\prime}_{k}
, and we choose
B_{n}\times B_{n+1}
as our fixed minimal parabolic subgroup of
\mathrm{G}^{\prime}
. We put
\mathfrak{a}_{k}:=\mathfrak{a}_{B_{k}}
. Let
B_{k,F}:=B_{k}\cap\GL_{n}
be the upper triangular Borel subgroup of
\GL_{n}
.

Let
k,a,b
be integers such that
k\geq 1
and
0\leq a<b\leq k
, let
e_{1},\cdots,e_{k}
be the standard basis of
E^{k}
and let
E^{a,b}
be the subspace of
E^{k}
generated by
e_{a+1},\cdots,e_{b}
.

Let
\mathcal{F}_{\mathrm{RS}}
be the set of Rankin-Selberg parabolic subgroup of
\mathrm{G}^{\prime}
introduced in
[
3
]
*Subsection 3.1. It consists of semi-standard parabolic subgroups of
\mathrm{G}^{\prime}
of the form
P_{n}\times P_{n+1}
, such that
P_{n}
is standard and
P_{n+1}\cap\mathrm{G}_{n}^{\prime}=P_{n}
. When
P_{n}
is the stabilizer of the flag

(3.1)

0=V_{0}\subset V_{1}\subset\cdots\subset V_{r}=E^{n}

with
V_{i}=E^{0,a_{i}}
, then there are
2r+1
possible choices such that
P_{n}\times P_{n+1}\in\mathcal{F}_{\mathrm{RS}}
. It is either the stabilizer of the flag

(3.2)

0=V_{0}\subset\cdots\subset V_{k}\subset V_{k+1}\oplus Ee_{n+1}\subset\cdots\subset V_{r}\oplus Ee_{n+1}=E^{n+1}

with
0\leq k\leq r-1
or the stabilizer of the flag

(3.3)

0=V_{0}\subset\cdots\subset V_{k}\subset V_{k}\oplus Ee_{n+1}\subset\cdots\subset V_{r}\oplus Ee_{n+1}=E^{n+1}

with
0\leq k\leq r
.

In the case when
P_{n+1}
stabilizes the flag (
3.2
), the standard Levi subgroup
M_{P_{n+1}}
of
P_{n+1}
can be identified with

\prod_{\begin{subarray}{c}0\leq i\leq r-1\\
i\neq k\end{subarray}}\GL(E^{a_{i},a_{i+1}})\times\GL(E^{a_{k},a_{k+1}}+Ee_{n+1}).

Let
M_{P_{n+1}}(\mathbb{A})^{\mathbbm{1}}
be the subgroup of
M_{P_{n+1}}(\mathbb{A})
consisting of elements

\prod_{\begin{subarray}{c}0\leq i\leq r-1\\
i\neq k\end{subarray}}\GL(E^{a_{i},a_{i+1}})(\mathbb{A})^{1}\times\GL(E^{a_{k},a_{k+1}}+Ee_{n+1})(\mathbb{A})

under the above identification. We denote by
\mathrm{G}^{\prime}_{n+1}(\mathbb{A})_{P_{n+1}}^{\mathbbm{1}}
the product
M_{P_{n+1}}(\mathbb{A})^{\mathbbm{1}}N_{P_{n+1}}(\mathbb{A})
.

In the case when
P_{n+1}
stabilizes the flag (
3.3
),
M_{P_{n+1}}
can be identified with

\prod_{0\leq i\leq r}\GL(E^{a_{i},a_{i+1}})\times\GL(Ee_{n+1}).

Let
M_{P_{n+1}}(\mathbb{A})^{\mathbbm{1}}
be the subgroup of
M_{P_{n+1}}(\mathbb{A})
consisting of elements

\prod_{0\leq i\leq r}\GL(E^{a_{i},a_{i+1}})(\mathbb{A})^{1}\times\GL(Ee_{n+1})(\mathbb{A})

under the identification above and we also set
\mathrm{G}^{\prime}_{n+1}(\mathbb{A})_{P_{n+1}}^{\mathbbm{1}}=M_{P_{n+1}}^{\mathbbm{1}}N_{P_{n+1}}(\mathbb{A})
.

For
P\in\mathcal{F}_{\mathrm{RS}}
, we write
P_{\mathrm{H}_{1}}:=P\cap\mathrm{H}_{1}\cong P_{n}
and
P_{\mathrm{H}_{2}}=P\cap\mathrm{H}_{2}
. Let
\mathcal{F}_{\mathrm{RS},F}
be the set of semi-standard parabolic subgroups of
\mathrm{H}_{2}
of the form
P_{n}\times P_{n+1}
, such that
P_{n}
is standard and
P_{n+1}\cap\GL_{n}=P_{n}
. The map
PP\cap\mathrm{H}_{2}
induces a bijection between
\mathcal{F}_{\mathrm{RS}}
and
\mathcal{F}_{\mathrm{RS},F}

The natural map
\mathrm{G}^{\prime}_{n}\hookrightarrow\mathrm{G}^{\prime}_{n+1}
induces a map
\mathfrak{a}_{n}\hookrightarrow\mathfrak{a}_{n+1}
. For
P\in\mathcal{F}_{\mathrm{RS}}
, it induces an embedding
\mathfrak{a}_{P_{n}}\hookrightarrow\mathfrak{a}_{P_{n+1}}
. We define

\mathfrak{a}_{P,\mathrm{H}_{1}}:=\mathfrak{a}_{P_{n}}\cap\mathfrak{a}_{P_{n+1}}\cong\mathfrak{a}_{P_{\mathrm{H}_{1}}}\cap\mathfrak{a}_{P}.

The natural map
\iota:\mathfrak{a}_{P,\mathrm{H}_{1}}\mathfrak{a}_{P_{n+1}}^{\mathrm{G}^{\prime}_{n+1}}
induced by
\mathfrak{a}_{P,\mathrm{H}_{1}}\hookrightarrow\mathfrak{a}_{P_{n+1}}\twoheadrightarrow\mathfrak{a}_{P_{n+1}}^{\mathrm{G}^{\prime}_{n+1}}
is an isomorphism.

The subspace
\mathfrak{a}_{P,\mathrm{H}_{1}}
is the Lie algebra of
A_{P,\mathrm{H}_{1}}^{\infty}:=A_{P_{n}}^{\infty}\cap A_{P_{n+1}}^{\infty}\cong A_{P_{\mathrm{H}_{1}}}^{\infty}\cap A_{P}^{\infty}
. Note that we have the decomposition

M_{P_{n+1}}(\mathbb{A})=M_{P_{n+1}}^{\mathbbm{1}}(\mathbb{A})\times A_{P,\mathrm{H}_{1}}^{\infty},\quad\mathrm{G}^{\prime}_{n+1}(\mathbb{A})=\mathrm{G}^{\prime}_{n+1}(\mathbb{A})^{\mathbbm{1}}_{P_{n+1}}\times A_{P,\mathrm{H}_{1}}^{\infty}.

For
s\in\mathbb{C}
, define
s\det\in\mathfrak{a}_{P,\mathrm{H}_{1},\mathbb{C}}^{*}
by requiring the expression

\langle s\det,H_{P_{n}}(a)\rangle=\lvert\det a\rvert^{s}

holds for all
A_{Q,\mathrm{H}_{1}}^{\infty}\subset\mathrm{G}^{\prime}_{n}(\mathbb{A})
. Define
\rho_{P_{n}}
(resp.
\rho_{P_{n+1}}
)
\in\mathfrak{a}_{P,\mathrm{H}_{1}}^{*}
be the element pulling back from
\rho_{P_{n}}\in\mathfrak{a}_{P_{n}}^{\mathrm{G}_{n}^{\prime},*}
(resp.
\rho_{P_{n+1}}\in\mathfrak{a}_{P_{n+1}}^{\mathrm{G}_{n+1}^{\prime},*}
). Let
\underline{\rho}_{P,s}
be the element in
\mathfrak{a}_{P,\mathrm{H}_{1},\mathbb{C}}^{*}
defined by

\underline{\rho}_{P,s}:=\rho_{P_{n+1}}-\rho_{P_{n}}+s\det.

We also regard
\underline{\rho}_{P,s}
as an element of
\mathfrak{a}_{P_{n+1},\mathbb{C}}^{\mathrm{G}^{\prime}_{n+1}}
via the isomorphism
\iota
. Write
c_{P}\in\mathbb{R}
for the Jacobian of
\iota
.

Let
V
be a finite dimensional vector space over
\mathbb{R}
, define
V^{*}_{\mathbb{C}}:=\Hom_{\mathbb{R}}(V,\mathbb{C})
. An exponential polynomial function on
V
is a function of the form

p(x)=\sum_{i=1}^{r}P_{i}(x)e^{\langle\lambda_{i},x\rangle},\quad x\in V,

where
\lambda_{i}\in V^{*}_{\mathbb{C}}
and
P_{i}
are non-zero polynomial functions on
V
. If
\lambda_{i}
are distinct, then
P_{i}
are uniquely determined. The term corresponding to
\lambda_{i}=0
is called the
pure polynomial term
. The complex numbers
\lambda_{i}
are called exponents of
p
.

Let
\eta_{E/F}:\mathbb{A}^{\times}\{\pm 1\}
be the quadratic character associated to
E/F
. By an abuse of notation, we define a character
\eta
on
\mathrm{H}_{2}(\mathbb{A})
by

\eta(h_{2,n},h_{2,n+1})=\eta_{E/F}(h_{2,n})^{n+1}\eta_{E/F}(h_{2,n})^{n}.

3.2.
Coarse Jacquet-Rallis RTF

For
f\in\mathcal{S}(\mathrm{G}^{\prime}(\mathbb{A}))
and
P\in\mathcal{F}_{\mathrm{RS}}
, the right translation action
\mathrm{R}(f)
on
L^{2}([\mathrm{G}^{\prime}]_{P})
is given by the kernel function

K_{f,P}(x,y)=\sum_{m\in M_{P}(F)}\int_{N_{P}(\mathbb{A})}f(x^{-1}mny)\mathrm{d}n,

where
x,y\in[\mathrm{G}^{\prime}]_{P}
. For
\chi\in\mathfrak{X}(\mathrm{G}^{\prime})
, let
K_{f,P,\chi}(x,y)
be the kernel function of the operator
p_{\chi}\circ R(f)
on
L^{2}([\mathrm{G}^{\prime}]_{P})
, where
p_{\chi}
denotes the projection to
\chi
-component in the Langlands spectral decomposition (
2.1
). For
(h_{1},h_{2})\in[\mathrm{H}_{1}]_{P_{\mathrm{H}_{1}}}\times[\mathrm{H}_{2}]_{P_{\mathrm{H}_{2}}}
we put

K_{f,P,b}(h_{1},h_{2})=\sum_{m\in M_{P}(F)\cap\mathrm{G}^{\prime}_{b}(F)}\int_{N_{P}(\mathbb{A})}f(h_{1}^{-1}mnh_{2}),

where we recall that
\mathrm{G}^{\prime}_{b}
is the fiber of
b
(see Subsection
2.1
).

For each
\bullet\in\mathfrak{X}(\mathrm{G}^{\prime})\sqcup\mathrm{B}(F)
and
T\in\mathfrak{a}_{0}
, the modified kernel is defined as

K_{f,\bullet}^{T}(h_{1},h_{2})=\sum_{P\in\mathcal{F}_{\mathrm{RS}}}\epsilon_{P}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\gamma\in P_{\mathrm{H}_{1}}(F)\backslash\mathrm{H}_{1}(F)\\
\delta\in P_{\mathrm{H}_{2}}(F)\backslash\mathrm{H}_{2}(F)\end{subarray}}\widehat{\tau}_{P_{n+1}}(H_{P_{n+1}}(\delta_{n}h_{2,n})-T_{P_{n+1}})K_{f,P,\bullet}(\gamma h_{1},\delta h_{2}),

where
(h_{1},h_{2})\in[\mathrm{H}_{1}]\times[\mathrm{H}_{2}]
. More generally, for
Q\in\mathcal{F}_{\mathrm{RS}}
and
\bullet\in\mathfrak{X}(\mathrm{G}^{\prime})\sqcup\mathrm{B}(F)
, we put

K_{f,\bullet}^{Q,T}(h_{1},h_{2})=\sum_{P\in\mathcal{F}_{\mathrm{RS}}}\epsilon_{P}^{Q}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\gamma\in P_{\mathrm{H}_{1}}(F)\backslash Q_{\mathrm{H}_{1}}(F)\\
\delta\in P_{\mathrm{H}_{2}}(F)\backslash Q_{\mathrm{H}_{2}}(F)\end{subarray}}\widehat{\tau}_{P_{n+1}}^{Q_{n+1}}(H_{P_{n+1}}(\delta_{n}h_{2,n})-T_{P_{n+1}})K_{f,P,\bullet}(\gamma h_{1},\delta h_{2}),

where
(h_{1},h_{2})\in[\mathrm{H}_{1}]_{Q_{\mathrm{H}_{1}}}\times[\mathrm{H}_{2}]_{Q_{\mathrm{H}_{2}}}
.

We have the following asymptotic of modified kernel in
[
3
]
*Theorem 3.3.7.1: for any
N>0
, there exists a continuous semi-norm
\|\cdot\|
on
\mathcal{S}(\mathrm{G}^{\prime}(\mathbb{A}))
such that

(3.4)

\sum_{\chi\in\mathfrak{X}(\mathrm{G}^{\prime})}\left|K_{f,\chi}^{T}(h_{1},h_{2})-F^{\GL_{n+1}}(h_{2,n},T)K_{f,\chi}(h_{1},h_{2})\right|\leq e^{-N\|T\|}\|h_{1}\|_{H_{1}}^{-N}\|h_{2}\|_{H_{2}}^{-N}\|f\|

holds for all
f\in\mathrm{S}(\mathrm{G}^{\prime}(\mathbb{A})),(h_{1},h_{2})\in[\mathrm{H}_{1}]\times[\mathrm{H}_{2}]
and
T\in\mathfrak{a}_{n+1}
sufficiently positive.

In Proposition
A.1
, we generalize the result to any
K_{f,\chi}^{Q,T}
, we showed that for every
Q\in\mathcal{F}_{\mathrm{RS}}
and
N>0
, there exists a continuous semi-norm
\|\cdot\|
on
\mathcal{S}(\mathrm{G}^{\prime}(\mathbb{A}))
such that

(3.5)

\sum_{\chi\in\mathfrak{X}(\mathrm{G}^{\prime})}\lvert K_{f,\chi}^{Q,T}(h_{1},h_{2})-F^{Q_{n+1}}(h_{2,n},T_{Q_{n+1}})K_{f,Q,\chi}(h_{1},h_{2})\rvert\leq e^{-N\|T\|}\|h_{1}\|_{Q_{\mathrm{H}_{1}}}^{-N}\|h_{2}\|_{Q_{\mathrm{H}_{2}}}^{-N}\|f\|.

holds for
f\in\mathcal{S}(\mathrm{G}^{\prime}(\mathbb{A})),(h_{1},h_{2})\in[\mathrm{H}_{1}]_{Q_{\mathrm{H}_{1}}}\times[\mathrm{H}_{2}]^{\mathbbm{1}}_{Q_{\mathrm{H}_{2}}}
and
T\in\mathfrak{a}_{n+1}
sufficiently positive.

We also showed in Proposition
A.3
that for any
f\in\mathcal{S}(\mathrm{G}^{\prime}(\mathbb{A}))
and
N>0
, we have

(3.6)

\sum_{b\in\mathrm{B}(F)}\lvert K_{f,b}^{Q,T}(h_{1},h_{2})-F^{Q_{n+1}}(h_{2,n},T_{Q_{n+1}})K_{f,Q,b}(h_{1},h_{2})\rvert\ll e^{-N\|T\|}\|h_{1}\|_{Q_{\mathrm{H}_{1}}}^{-N}\|h_{2}\|_{Q_{\mathrm{H}_{2}}}^{-N}.

We say that an idèle class character
\xi:\mathbb{A}_{E}^{\times}\mathbb{C}^{\times}
is strictly unitary if it is trivial on the subgroup
A_{\mathbb{G}_{m}}^{\infty}\subset\mathbb{A}_{E}^{\times}
. Note that any idèle class character can be uniquely written as
\xi\cdot|\cdot|^{s}
, where
\xi
is strictly unitary and
s\in\mathbb{C}
.

The next theorem slightly generalizes the result in
[
3
]
*Section 3 by allowing a general character on
\mathrm{H}_{1}(\mathbb{A})
.

Theorem 3.1
.

Let
\xi
be a strictly unitary character of
\mathbb{A}_{E}^{\times}
.

(1)

For any
f\in\mathcal{S}(\mathrm{G}^{\prime}(\mathbb{A}))
,
s\in\mathbb{C}
and
T
sufficiently positive, we have

(3.7)

\sum_{\chi\in\mathfrak{X}(\mathrm{G}^{\prime})}\int_{[\mathrm{H}_{1}]}\int_{[\mathrm{H}_{2}]}\lvert K_{f,\chi}^{T}(h_{1},h_{2})\rvert\lvert\det h_{1}\rvert^{\mathrm{Re}(s)}\mathrm{d}h_{1}\mathrm{d}h_{2}<\infty

and

(3.8)

\sum_{b\in\mathrm{B}(F)}\int_{[\mathrm{H}_{1}]}\int_{[\mathrm{H}_{2}]}\lvert K_{f,b}^{T}(h_{1},h_{2})\rvert\lvert\det h_{1}\rvert^{\mathrm{Re}(s)}\mathrm{d}h_{1}\mathrm{d}h_{2}<\infty.

(2)

For
\bullet\in\mathfrak{X}(\mathrm{G}^{\prime})\sqcup\mathrm{B}(F)
, as a function of
T
, the integral

I_{\bullet}^{T}(f,\xi,s):=\int_{[\mathrm{H}_{1}]}\int_{[\mathrm{H}_{2}]}K^{T}_{f,\bullet}(h_{1},h_{2})\xi(h_{1})\lvert\det h_{1}\rvert^{s}\eta(h_{2})\mathrm{d}h_{1}\mathrm{d}h_{2}

is an exponential polynomial. If
s\not\in\{-1,1\}
, then the pure polynomial term is a constant, we denoted it by
I_{\bullet}(f,\xi,s)
. For a fixed
f
and
\xi
,
I_{\bullet}(f,\xi,s)
is meromorphic on
\mathbb{C}\setminus\{-1,1\}
. We write
I_{\bullet}(f,\xi):=I_{\bullet}(f,\xi,0)
.

(3)

For each
\chi\in\mathfrak{X}(\mathrm{G}^{\prime})
and
s\not\in\{-1,1\}
, the distribution
I_{\chi}(\cdot,\xi,s)
is continuous and the sum

I(f,\xi,s):=\sum_{\chi\in\mathfrak{X}(\mathrm{G}^{\prime})}I_{\chi}(f,\xi,s)

is absolutely convergent and defines a continuous distribution
I(\cdot,\xi,s)
on
\mathcal{S}(\mathrm{G}^{\prime}(\mathbb{A}))
.

(4)

For each
b\in\mathrm{B}(F)
and
s\not\in\{-1,1\}
, the distribution
I_{b}(\cdot,\xi,s)
is continuous on
\mathcal{S}(\mathrm{G}^{\prime}(\mathbb{A}))
and

I(f,\xi,s)=\sum_{b\in\mathrm{B}(F)}I_{b}(f,\xi,s).

Where the sum on the right-hand side is absolutely convergent.

The proof is similar to
[
3
]
*Section 3, for later use, we briefly sketch the proof here.

Proof of Part (1).

Since
|h_{1}|^{\mathrm{Re}(s)}\ll\|h_{1}\|_{\mathrm{H}_{1}}^{\left|\mathrm{Re}(s)\right|}
. By
[
7
]
*Theorem A.1.1(vi), for
N
large enough

\int_{[\mathrm{H}_{1}]}\int_{[\mathrm{H}_{2}]}e^{-N\|T\|}\|h_{1}\|^{-N}_{H_{1}}\|h_{2}\|_{H_{2}}^{-N}|\det h_{1}|^{s}\mathrm{d}h_{1}\mathrm{d}h_{2}

is finite and defines continuous semi-norm on
\mathcal{S}(\mathrm{G}^{\prime}(\mathbb{A}))
.

By
[
3
]
*(3.2.3.2) for any
f\in\mathcal{S}(\mathrm{G}^{\prime}(\mathbb{A}))
and any
(h_{1},h_{2})\in[\mathrm{H}_{1}]\times[\mathrm{H}_{2}]
, we have,

\sum_{\chi\in\mathfrak{X}(\mathrm{G}^{\prime})}\left|K_{f,\chi}(h_{1},h_{2})\right||\det h_{1}|^{\mathrm{Re}(s)}\ll\|h_{2,n}\|_{\GL_{n}}^{N}\|h_{2,n+1}\|_{\GL_{n+1}}^{-N}\|h_{1}\|_{\mathrm{H}_{1}}^{-N+|\mathrm{Re}(s)|}.

Since
F^{\GL_{n+1}}(\cdot,T)
is compactly supported as a function on
[\GL_{n}]
, we see that

\int_{[\mathrm{H}_{1}]}\int_{[\mathrm{H}_{2}]}\sum_{\chi\in\mathfrak{X}(\mathrm{G}^{\prime})}F^{\GL_{n+1}}(h_{2,n},T)\left|K_{f,\chi}(h_{1},h_{2})\right|\mathrm{d}h_{1}\mathrm{d}h_{2}

is finite. Combining with (
3.4
) ,we see that the integral (
3.7
) is finite. Using the estimate (
3.6
), the finiteness of (
3.8
) is proved in a similar way.
∎

For each
Q\in\mathcal{F}_{\mathrm{RS}}
, let
\Gamma^{\prime}_{Q_{n+1}}
be the function on
\mathfrak{a}_{Q_{n+1}}^{\mathrm{G}^{\prime}_{n+1}}\times\mathfrak{a}_{Q_{n+1}}^{\mathrm{G}^{\prime}_{n+1}}
defined in
[
2
]
*Section 2. It is compactly supported in the first variable when the second variable stays in a compact subset.

By
[
25
]
*Lemme 3.5, for any
Q\in\mathcal{F}_{\mathrm{RS}}
, the function

p_{Q,s}:X\int_{\mathfrak{a}_{Q_{n+1}}^{\mathrm{G}^{\prime}_{n+1}}}e^{\langle\underline{\rho}_{Q,s},H\rangle}\Gamma^{\prime}_{Q_{n+1}}(H,X)\mathrm{d}H,\quad X\in\mathfrak{a}_{n+1}

is an exponential polynomial function and when
s\not\in\{-1,1\}
, the pure polynomial term is constant.

Proof of Part(2)-(4).

For
Q\in\mathcal{F}_{\mathrm{RS}}
and
(h_{1},h_{2})\in[\mathrm{H}_{1}]_{Q_{\mathrm{H}_{1}}}\times[\mathrm{H}_{2}]_{Q_{\mathrm{H}_{2}}}
, we have the following equality

K_{f,\bullet}^{T}(h_{1},h_{2})=\sum_{Q\in\mathcal{F}_{\mathrm{RS}}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\gamma\in Q_{\mathrm{H}_{1}}(F)\backslash\mathrm{H}_{1}(F)\\
\delta\in Q_{\mathrm{H}_{2}}(F)\backslash\mathrm{H}_{2}(F)\end{subarray}}\Gamma^{\prime}_{Q_{n+1}}(H_{Q_{n+1}}(\delta_{n}h_{2,n})-T^{\prime}_{Q_{n+1}},T_{Q_{n+1}}-T^{\prime}_{Q_{n+1}})K_{f,\bullet}^{Q,T}(\gamma h_{1},\delta h_{2}).

We hence see that
I^{T}_{\bullet}(f,\xi,s)
is equal to

\sum_{Q\in\mathcal{F}_{\mathrm{RS}}}\int_{[\mathrm{H}_{1}]_{Q_{\mathrm{H}_{1}}}}\int_{[\mathrm{H}_{2}]_{Q_{\mathrm{H}_{2}}}}\Gamma^{\prime}_{Q_{n+1}}\left(H_{Q_{n+1}}(h_{2,n})-T^{\prime}_{Q_{n+1}},T_{Q_{n+1}}-T^{\prime}_{Q_{n+1}}\right)K_{f,\bullet}^{Q,T}(h_{1},h_{2})\xi(h_{1})\lvert\det h_{1}\rvert^{s}\eta(h_{2})\mathrm{d}h_{1}\mathrm{d}h_{2}.

Using Iwasawa decomposition, the summand corresponding to
Q
is

\displaystyle\int_{[M_{Q_{\mathrm{H}_{1}}}]}\int_{[M_{Q_{\mathrm{H}_{2}}}]}\int_{K_{1}\times K_{2}}

\displaystyle e^{\langle-2\rho_{Q_{\mathrm{H}_{1}}},H_{Q_{\mathrm{H}_{1}}}(m_{1})\rangle}e^{\langle-2\rho_{Q_{\mathrm{H}_{2}}},H_{Q_{\mathrm{H}_{2}}}(m_{2})\rangle}\Gamma^{\prime}_{Q_{n+1}}\left(H_{Q_{n+1}}(m_{2,n})-T^{\prime}_{Q_{n+1}},T_{Q_{n+1}}-T^{\prime}_{Q_{n+1}}\right)

\displaystyle K_{f,\bullet}^{Q,T}(m_{1}k_{1},m_{2}k_{2})\xi(m_{1}k_{1})\lvert\det m_{1}\rvert^{s}\eta(m_{2}k_{2})\mathrm{d}m_{1}\mathrm{d}m_{2}\mathrm{d}k_{1}\mathrm{d}k_{2}.

Define
f_{Q}\in\mathcal{S}(M_{Q}(\mathbb{A})))
by

f_{Q}(m)=e^{\langle\rho_{Q},H_{Q}(m)\rangle}\int_{K_{1}\times K_{2}}\int_{N_{Q}(\mathbb{A})}f(k_{1}^{-1}mnk_{2})\xi(k_{1})\eta(k_{2})\mathrm{d}k_{1}\mathrm{d}k_{2}\mathrm{d}n.

Then one readily check that for all
(m_{1},m_{2})\in[M_{Q_{\mathrm{H}_{1}}}]\times[M_{Q_{\mathrm{H}_{2}}}]
, we have

\int_{K_{1}}\int_{K_{2}}K^{Q,T}_{f,\bullet}(m_{1}k_{1},m_{2}k_{2})\xi(k_{1})\eta(k_{2})\mathrm{d}k_{1}\mathrm{d}k_{2}=e^{\langle\rho_{Q},H_{Q}(m_{1})+H_{Q}(m_{2})\rangle}K_{f_{Q},\bullet}^{M_{Q},T}(m_{1},m_{2}).

Let
M_{1}:=M_{Q_{\mathrm{H}_{1}}}
and
M_{2}:=M_{Q_{\mathrm{H}_{2}}}
. We obtain

(3.9)

\displaystyle I_{\bullet}^{T}(f,\xi,s)=\sum_{Q}\int_{[M_{1}]}\int_{[M_{2}]}

\displaystyle e^{\langle\underline{\rho}_{Q},H_{Q_{\mathrm{H}_{1}}}(m_{1})\rangle}\Gamma^{\prime}_{Q_{n+1}}\left(H_{Q_{n+1}}(m_{2,n})-T^{\prime}_{Q_{n+1}},T_{Q_{n+1}}-T^{\prime}_{Q_{n+1}}\right)

\displaystyle K_{f_{Q}}^{M_{Q},T^{\prime}}(m_{1},m_{2})\xi(m_{1})\lvert m_{1}\rvert^{s}\eta(m_{2})\mathrm{d}m_{1}\mathrm{d}m_{2}

\displaystyle=\sum_{Q\in\mathcal{F}_{\mathrm{RS}}}c_{Q}p_{Q,s}(T_{Q_{n+1}}-T^{\prime}_{Q_{n+1}})

\displaystyle\int_{A_{Q,\mathrm{H}_{1}}^{\infty}\backslash[M_{1}]\times[M_{2}]}e^{\langle\underline{\rho}_{Q,s},-H_{Q_{n+1}}(m_{2,n})+T^{\prime}_{Q_{n+1}}\rangle}

\displaystyle K_{f_{Q}}^{M_{Q},T^{\prime}}(m_{1},m_{2})\xi(m_{1})\lvert m_{1}\rvert^{s}\eta(m_{2})\mathrm{d}m_{1}\mathrm{d}m_{2}.

Using Proposition
A.4
, the last integral in (
3.9
) is finite as the same argument of part (1), hence (2) is proved. Part (3) and (4) then follow from the expression of
I^{T}_{\bullet}(f,\xi,s)
in (
3.9
).
∎

3.3.
Coarse infinitesimal Jacquet-Rallis RTF

There is also a Lie algebra version of the Jacquet-Rallis relative trace formula formulated by Zydor in
[
24
]
. Let
P=MN\in\mathcal{F}_{\mathrm{RS},F}
, we put
\mathfrak{p}
(resp.
\mathfrak{m}
,
\mathfrak{n}
) be the Lie algebra of
P_{n+1}
(resp.
M_{P_{n+1}}
,
N_{P_{n+1}}
). They are Lie subalgebras of
\gl_{n+1}
. For
\varphi\in\mathcal{S}(\gl_{n+1}(\mathbb{A}))
and
P\in\mathcal{F}_{\mathrm{RS}}
, we put a kernel function
K_{\varphi,P}
on
[\GL_{n+1}]_{P_{n+1}}
by

(3.10)

K_{\varphi,P}(g)=\sum_{M\in\mathfrak{m}(F)}\int_{\mathfrak{n}(\mathbb{A})}\varphi((M+N)\cdot g)\mathrm{d}N,\quad g\in[\GL_{n+1}]_{P_{n+1}}

For
a\in\mathcal{B}(F)
, we put

K_{\varphi,P,a}(g)=\sum_{M\in\mathfrak{m}(F)\cap\gl_{n+1,a}(F)}\int_{\mathfrak{n}(\mathbb{A})}\varphi((M+N)\cdot g)\mathrm{d}N,\quad g\in[\GL_{n+1}]_{P_{n+1}}.

For
T\in\mathfrak{a}_{0}
and
a\in\mathcal{B}(F)
, we define a modified kernel by

K_{\varphi,a}^{T}(g)=\sum_{P\in\mathcal{F}_{\mathrm{RS}}}\varepsilon_{P}\sum_{\gamma\in P_{n}(F)\backslash\GL_{n}(F)}\widehat{\tau}_{P_{n+1}}(H_{P_{n+1}}(\gamma g)-T_{P_{n+1}})K_{\varphi,P}(\gamma g),\quad g\in[\GL_{n}]

More generally, for
Q\in\mathcal{F}_{\mathrm{RS},F}
we put

K_{\varphi,a}^{T,Q}(g)=\sum_{P\in\mathcal{F}}\varepsilon_{P}^{Q}\sum_{\gamma\in P_{n}(F)\backslash Q_{n}(F)}\widetilde{\tau}_{P_{n+1}}^{Q_{n+1}}(H_{P_{n+1}}(\gamma g)-T_{P_{n+1}})K_{\varphi,P,a}(\gamma g),\quad g\in[\GL_{n}]_{Q_{n}}.

The following result is a slight generalization of the main theorem in
[
24
]
.

Theorem 3.2

(Zydor)
.

Let
\xi
be a strictly unitary character of
\mathbb{A}_{E}^{\times}
.

(1)

For any
\varphi\in\mathcal{S}(\gl_{n+1}(\mathbb{A}))
,
s\in\mathbb{C}
and
T\in\mathfrak{a}_{0}
sufficiently positive, we have

\sum_{a\in\mathcal{B}(F)}\int_{[\GL_{n}]}\lvert K_{\varphi,a}^{T}(g)\rvert\lvert\det g\rvert^{s}\mathrm{d}g<\infty.

(2)

For any
a\in\mathcal{B}(F)
, as a function of
T
, the integral

I^{T}_{a}(f,\xi,s):=\int_{[\GL_{n}]}K^{T}_{\varphi,a}(g)\xi(g)\eta(g)\lvert\det g\rvert^{s}\mathrm{d}g

is an exponential polynomial. If
s\not\in\{-1,1\}
, then the pure polynomial term is a constant, denoted by
I_{a}(\varphi,\xi,s)
. For a fixed
\varphi
and
\xi
,
I_{a}(\varphi,\xi,s)
is meromorphic on
\mathbb{C}\setminus\{-1,1\}
. We put
I_{a}(\varphi,\xi):=I(\varphi,\xi,0)
.

(3)

For each
a\in\mathcal{B}(F)
and
s\not\in\{-1,1\}
the distribution
I_{a}(\cdot,\xi,s)
on
\mathcal{S}(\gl_{n+1}(\mathbb{A}))
is continuous and the sum

I(\varphi,\xi,s):=\sum_{a\in\mathcal{B}(F)}I_{a}(\varphi,\xi,s)

is absolutely convergent and defines a continuous distribution
I(\cdot,\xi,s)
on
\mathcal{S}(\gl_{n+1}(\mathbb{A}))
.

Proof.

We proved in Appendix
A
the asymptotic of the modified kernel in the Lie algebra case. (See Proposition
A.5
). Using this, the proof is identical to the proof of Theorem
3.1
.
∎

Remark 3.3
.

There is also a version of infinitesimal Jacquet-Rallis RTF on
\widetilde{\gl_{n}}
. Let
P=MN\in\mathcal{F}_{\mathrm{RS},F}
, we let
\widetilde{\mathfrak{m}}
and
\widetilde{\mathfrak{n}}
be the intersection of
\mathfrak{m}
and
\mathfrak{n}
with
\widetilde{\gl_{n}}
under the identification (
2.10
). Then for
\varphi\in\mathcal{S}(\widetilde{\gl_{n}}(\mathbb{A}))
, we can define
K_{\varphi,P}
in the same way as (
3.10
) replacing
\mathfrak{m},\mathfrak{n}
by
\widetilde{\mathfrak{m}}
and
\widetilde{\mathfrak{n}}
. We then define modified kernels
K_{\varphi,a}^{T}
for any
a\in\mathcal{A}(F)
using the same formula; analogs of Theorem
3.2
hold in this setting.

The theorem also directly generalizes to products of
\widetilde{\gl_{n}}
.

4.
Global Theory I: The case of
\mathrm{G}^{\prime}_{\pm}
-supported test functions

In this section, we keep the notations in Section
3
.

4.1.
Explicit computation of exponents

For
P\in\mathcal{F}_{\mathrm{RS}}
or
\mathcal{F}_{\mathrm{RS},F}
, we say that
P
is
standard
, if
P_{n+1}
is standard, more concretely, if
P_{n}
stabilizes the flag (
3.1
), then
P_{n+1}
stabilizes the flag (
3.2
) with
k=r-1
or the flag (
3.3
) with
k=r
. We say that
P
is
antistandard
, if
P_{n+1}
stabilizes the flag (
3.2
) with
k=0
or the flag (
3.3
) with
k=0
. Let
\mathcal{F}_{\mathrm{RS}}^{\mathrm{st}}
be the set of standard Rankin-Selberg parabolic subgroups, and let
\mathcal{F}_{\mathrm{RS}}^{\mathrm{ast}}
be the set of antistandard Rankin-Selberg parabolic subgroups

Proposition 4.1
.

Let
P\in\mathcal{F}_{\mathrm{RS}}
or
\mathcal{F}_{\mathrm{RS},F}

(1)

If
P
is standard and
\mathrm{Re}(s)<-1
, then for all
\varpi^{\vee}\in\widehat{\Delta}_{P_{n+1}}^{\vee}
,
\mathrm{Re}\langle\underline{\rho}_{P,s},\varpi^{\vee}\rangle<0
.

(2)

If
P
is antistandard and
\mathrm{Re}(s)>1
, then for all
\varpi^{\vee}\in\widehat{\Delta}_{P_{n+1}}^{\vee}
,
\mathrm{Re}\langle\underline{\rho}_{P,s},\varpi^{\vee}\rangle<0
.

Proof.

By the computation in
[
24
]
*Proof of Lemma 4.2, if
P
is standard, then for all
\varpi^{\vee}\in\widehat{\Delta}_{P_{n+1}}^{\vee}
, there exists positive integer
i>0
such that
\langle\underline{\rho}_{P,s},\varpi^{\vee}\rangle=i(1+s)
, hence (1) holds. If
P
is antistandard, then for all
\varpi^{\vee}\in\widehat{\Delta}_{P_{n+1}}^{\vee}
, there exists positive integer
i>0
such that
\langle\underline{\rho}_{P,s},\varpi^{\vee}\rangle=i(1-s)
, hence (2) holds.
∎

4.2.
Geometric distribution for the Jacquet-Rallis RTF

Now we state a theorem that computes the geometric term
I_{b}
under some assumptions on the support of the test function.

Theorem 4.2
.

Let
f\in\mathcal{S}(\mathrm{G}^{\prime}(\mathbb{A}))
. If there exists a place
v
of
F
such that
f
is of the form
f_{v}f^{v}
, with
f_{v}\in\mathcal{S}(\mathrm{G}^{\prime}(F_{v})),f^{v}\in\mathcal{S}(\mathrm{G}^{\prime}(\mathbb{A}^{v}))
and
\supp(f_{v})\subset\mathrm{G}^{\prime}_{+}(F_{v})
(resp.
\mathrm{G}^{\prime}_{-}(F_{v})
), then

(1)

for
s\in\mathcal{H}_{<-1}
(resp.
s\in\mathcal{H}_{>1}
), we have

\sum_{b\in\mathrm{B}(F)}\int_{[\mathrm{H}_{1}]}\int_{[\mathrm{H}_{2}]}\lvert K_{f,\gamma}(h_{1},h_{2})\rvert\lvert\det h_{1}\rvert^{\mathrm{Re}(s)}\mathrm{d}h_{1}\mathrm{d}h_{2}<\infty.

(2)

For
b\in\mathrm{B}(F)
, and
s\in\mathcal{H}_{<-1}
(resp.
\mathcal{H}_{>1}
) the integral

(4.1)

\int_{[\mathrm{H}_{1}]}\int_{[\mathrm{H}_{2}]}K_{f,b}(h_{1},h_{2})\lvert\det h_{1}\rvert^{s}\xi(h_{1})\eta(h_{2})\mathrm{d}h_{1}\mathrm{d}h_{2}

(which is absolutely convergent by (1)) equals to
I_{b}(f,\xi,s)
. In particular, as a function of
s
, the integral (
4.1
) is holomorphic on
\mathcal{H}_{<-1}
(resp.
\mathcal{H}_{>1}
) and has a meromorphic continuation to
\mathbb{C}
, which is holomorphic on
\mathbb{C}\setminus\{-1,1\}
, and equals to
I_{b}(f,\xi)
at
s=0
.

(3)

Pick any
\gamma\in O^{+}(b)
(resp.
\gamma\in O^{-}(b)
), then the integral

(4.2)

\int_{\mathrm{H}_{1}(\mathbb{A})}\int_{\mathrm{H}_{2}(\mathbb{A})}f(h_{1}^{-1}\gamma h_{2})\lvert\det h_{1}\rvert^{s}\xi(h_{1})\eta(h_{2})\mathrm{d}h_{1}\mathrm{d}h_{2}

is absolutely convergent on
\mathcal{H}_{<-1}
(resp.
\mathcal{H}_{>1}
) and equals to
I_{b}(f,\xi,s)
.

Proof of (1).

We stick to the case where
\mathrm{supp}(f_{v})\subset\mathrm{G}^{\prime}_{+}(F_{v})
, the case
\mathrm{supp}(f_{v})\subset\mathrm{G}^{\prime}_{-}(F_{v})
follows from the same argument. Let
P\in\mathcal{F}_{\mathrm{RS}}
and
P
be not standard. Then, for any
m\in M_{P}(F)
and
n\in N_{P}(\mathbb{A})
, we have

\alpha(mn)=\begin{pmatrix}A&b\\
c&d\end{pmatrix}\in P_{n+1}(\mathbb{A})\cap\mathrm{S}(\mathbb{A}).

Where the map
\alpha
is defined in (
2.2
). Since
P
is not standard, there is an integer
k>0
such that the last
k
coordinates of
b
are
0
. Hence

\det(b,Ab,\cdots,A^{n}b)=0.

This implies
f(h_{1}^{-1}mnh_{2})=0
. Therefore, for
b\in\mathrm{B}(F)
we have

K_{f,P,b}(h_{1},h_{2})=\sum_{m\in M_{P}(F)\cap\mathrm{G}^{\prime}_{b}(F)}\int_{N_{P}(\mathbb{A})}f(h_{1}^{-1}mnh_{2})=0

Similar to the calculation as we have done in the proof of part (2) of Theorem
3.1
, we can write

\displaystyle K_{f,b}^{T}(h_{1},h_{2})

\displaystyle=\sum_{P\in\mathcal{F}_{\mathrm{RS}}^{\mathrm{st}}}\epsilon_{P}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\gamma\in P_{\mathrm{H}_{1}}(F)\backslash\mathrm{H}_{1}(F)\\
\delta\in P_{\mathrm{H}_{2}}(F)\backslash\mathrm{H}_{2}(F)\end{subarray}}\tau_{P_{n+1}}(H_{P_{n+1}}(\delta_{n}h_{2,n})-T_{P_{n+1}})K_{f,P,b}(\gamma h_{1},\delta h_{2})

\displaystyle=\sum_{Q\in\mathcal{F}_{\mathrm{RS}}^{\mathrm{st}}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\gamma\in Q_{\mathrm{H}_{1}}(F)\backslash\mathrm{H}_{1}(F)\\
\delta\in Q_{\mathrm{H}_{2}}(F)\backslash\mathrm{H}_{2}(F)\end{subarray}}\Gamma^{\prime}_{Q_{n+1}}(H_{Q_{n+1}}(\delta_{n}h_{2,n})-T^{\prime}_{Q_{n+1}},T_{Q_{n+1}}-T^{\prime}_{Q_{n+1}})K_{f,b}^{Q,T}(\gamma h_{1},\delta h_{2}).

Pick
T^{\prime}\in\mathfrak{a}_{n+1}
such that
T-T^{\prime}\in\mathfrak{a}_{n+1}^{+}
, so for all
Q\in\mathcal{F}_{\mathrm{RS}}
, the values of

\Gamma^{\prime}_{Q_{n+1}}(\cdot,T_{Q_{n+1}}-T^{\prime}_{Q_{n+1}})

are 0 or 1. Then we have

\displaystyle\int_{[\mathrm{H}_{1}]}\int_{[\mathrm{H}_{2}]}\sum_{b\in\mathrm{B}(F)}\lvert K_{f,b}^{T}(h_{1},h_{2})\rvert\lvert\det h_{1}\rvert^{\mathrm{Re}(s)}\mathrm{d}h_{1}\mathrm{d}h_{2}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\sum_{Q\in\mathcal{F}^{\mathrm{st}}_{\mathrm{RS}}}\int_{[\mathrm{H}_{1}]_{Q_{\mathrm{H}_{1}}}\times[\mathrm{H}_{2}]_{Q_{\mathrm{H}_{2}}}}\Gamma^{\prime}_{Q_{n+1}}(H_{Q_{n+1}}(h_{2,n})-T^{\prime}_{Q_{n+1}},T_{Q_{n+1}}-T^{\prime}_{Q_{n+1}})\times

\displaystyle\left(\sum_{b\in\mathrm{B}(F)}\lvert K_{f,b}^{Q,T}(h_{1},h_{2})\rvert\right)\lvert\det h_{1}\rvert^{\mathrm{Re}(s)}\mathrm{d}h_{1}\mathrm{d}h_{2}

By the Iwasawa decomposition, and the fact that for
a\in A_{Q}^{\infty}
,

K_{f,b}^{Q,T}(ax,ay)=e^{\langle 2\rho_{Q},H_{Q}(a)\rangle}K_{f,b}^{Q,T}(x,y),

the the summand corresponds to
Q
in the above expression equals to

\displaystyle\int_{[M_{1}]}\int_{[M_{2}]}

\displaystyle\int_{K_{1}\times K_{2}}e^{\langle-2\rho_{Q_{\mathrm{H}_{1}}},H_{Q_{\mathrm{H}_{1}}}(m_{1})\rangle}e^{\langle-2\rho_{Q_{\mathrm{H}_{2}}},H_{Q_{\mathrm{H}_{2}}}(m_{2})\rangle}\Gamma^{\prime}_{Q_{n+1}}\left(H_{Q_{n+1}}(m_{2,n})-T^{\prime}_{Q_{n+1}},T_{Q_{n+1}}-T^{\prime}_{Q_{n+1}}\right)

\displaystyle\left(\sum_{b\in\mathrm{B}(F)}\lvert K_{f,b}^{Q,T}(m_{1}k_{1},m_{2}k_{2})\rvert\right)\lvert\det m_{1}\rvert^{\mathrm{Re}(s)}\mathrm{d}m_{1}\mathrm{d}m_{2}\mathrm{d}k_{1}\mathrm{d}k_{2}

\displaystyle=\int_{K_{1}\times K_{2}}

\displaystyle\int_{A_{Q,\mathrm{H}_{1}}^{\infty}\backslash([M_{1}]\times[M_{2}])}c_{Q}p_{Q,\mathrm{Re}(s)}(T_{Q_{n+1}}-T^{\prime}_{Q_{n+1}})e^{\langle\underline{\rho}_{Q,s},-H_{Q_{n+1}}(m_{2,n})+T^{\prime}_{Q_{n+1}}\rangle}e^{-\langle 2\rho_{Q_{\mathrm{H}_{1}}},H_{Q_{\mathrm{H}_{1}}}(m_{1})\rangle}

\displaystyle e^{-\langle 2\rho_{Q_{\mathrm{H}_{2}}},H_{Q_{\mathrm{H}_{2}}}(m_{2})\rangle}\left(\sum_{b\in\mathrm{B}(F)}\lvert K_{f,b}^{Q,T}(m_{1}k_{1},m_{2}k_{2})\rvert\right)\lvert\det m_{1}\rvert^{\mathrm{Re}(s)}\mathrm{d}m_{1}\mathrm{d}m_{2}\mathrm{d}k_{1}\mathrm{d}k_{2}.

Note that the natural map
[M_{1}]\times[M_{2}]^{\mathbbm{1}}A^{\infty}_{Q,\mathrm{H}_{1}}\backslash([M_{1}]\times[M_{2}])
is a bijection and is measure-preserving up to a constant
C_{Q}
, hence the expression above can be written as

(4.3)

\displaystyle\begin{split}&c_{Q}C_{Q}p_{Q,\mathrm{Re}(s)}(T_{Q_{n+1}}-T^{\prime}_{Q_{n+1}})e^{\langle\underline{\rho}_{Q,\mathrm{Re}(s)},T^{\prime}_{Q_{n+1}}\rangle}\int_{K_{1}\times K_{2}}\int_{[M_{1}]\times[M_{2}]^{\mathbbm{1}}}e^{-\langle 2\rho_{Q_{\mathrm{H}_{1}}},H_{Q_{\mathrm{H}_{1}}}(m_{1})\rangle}\\
&\left(\sum_{b\in\mathrm{B}(F)}\lvert K_{f,b}^{Q,T}(m_{1}k_{1},m_{2}k_{2})\rvert\right)\lvert\det m_{1}\rvert^{\mathrm{Re}(s)}\mathrm{d}m_{1}\mathrm{d}m_{2}\mathrm{d}k_{1}\mathrm{d}k_{2}.\end{split}

By (
3.6
), for
(m_{1},m_{2})\in[M_{1}]\times[M_{2}]^{\mathbbm{1}}
and
(k_{1},k_{2})\in K_{1}\times K_{2}
, we have

\sum_{b\in\mathrm{B}(F)}\lvert K_{f,b}^{Q,T}(m_{1}k_{1},m_{2}k_{2})-F^{Q_{n+1}}(m_{2,n},T_{Q_{n+1}})K_{f,Q,b}(m_{1}k_{1},m_{2}k_{2})\rvert\ll e^{-N\|T\|}\|m_{1}\|_{M_{1}}^{-N}\|m_{2}\|_{M_{2}}^{-N}.

Using the same argument of the proof of Theorem
3.1
(1), we see that the integral in (
4.3
) is finite. To conclude, the expression

(4.4)

\int_{[\mathrm{H}_{1}]}\int_{[\mathrm{H}_{2}]}\sum_{b\in\mathrm{B}(F)}\lvert K_{f,b}^{T}(h_{1},h_{2})\rvert\lvert\det h_{1}\rvert^{\mathrm{Re}(s)}\mathrm{d}h_{1}\mathrm{d}h_{2}

is bounded by a constant multiple of

\sum_{Q\in\mathcal{F}^{\mathrm{st}}_{\mathrm{RS}}}p_{Q}(T_{Q_{n+1}}-T^{\prime}_{Q_{n+1}}),

which is of the form

\sum_{Q\in\mathcal{F}^{\mathrm{st}}_{\mathrm{RS}}}a_{Q}P_{Q}(T)e^{\langle\underline{\rho}_{Q,\mathrm{Re}(s)},T_{Q_{n+1}}\rangle}

with
a_{Q}\in\mathbb{C},P_{Q}
is a polynomial on
\mathfrak{a}_{n+1}
.

When
T
is sufficiently positive,
T_{Q_{n+1}}
is of the form

T_{Q_{n+1}}=\sum_{\varpi^{\vee}\in\widehat{\Delta}_{Q}^{\vee}}T_{\varpi^{\vee}}\varpi^{\vee}

where each
T_{\varpi^{\vee}}
is a sufficiently positive real number. Therefore, by Proposition
4.1
, the expression (
4.4
) is bounded as
T
varies (and is sufficiently positive).

By (
3.6
), this implies

\int_{[\mathrm{H}_{1}]}\int_{[\mathrm{H}_{2}]}F^{\mathrm{G}_{n+1}^{\prime}}(h_{2,n},T)\left(\sum_{b\in\mathrm{B}(F)}\lvert K_{f,b}(h_{1},h_{2})\rvert\right)\mathrm{d}h_{1}\mathrm{d}h_{2}

is bounded as
T
varies. As
T\infty
(i.e.
\langle\alpha,T\rangle\infty
for any
\alpha\in\Delta_{0}
),
F^{\mathrm{G}^{\prime}_{n+1}}(\cdot,T)1
. Thus part (1) follows from the dominated convergence theorem.
∎

Proof of (2).

Let
f\in\mathcal{S}(\mathrm{G}^{\prime}(\mathbb{A}))
and let
T\in\mathfrak{a}_{n+1}
be sufficiently positive. For
N>0
large enough, by applying (
3.6
) to
Q=\mathrm{G}^{\prime}
, we have

\int_{[\mathrm{H}_{1}]}\int_{[\mathrm{H}_{2}]}\lvert F^{\mathrm{G}^{\prime}_{n+1}}(h_{2,n},T)K_{f,b}(h_{1},h_{2})-K_{f,b}^{T}(h_{1},h_{2})\rvert|\det h_{1}|^{s}\xi(h_{1})\eta(h_{2})\mathrm{d}h_{1}\mathrm{d}h_{2}\ll e^{-N\|T\|}.

When
T\infty
, the integral

\int_{[\mathrm{H}_{1}]}\int_{[\mathrm{H}_{2}]}F^{\mathrm{G}^{\prime}_{n+1}}(h_{2,n},T)K_{f,b}(h_{1},h_{2})|\det h_{1}|^{s}\xi(h_{1})\eta(h_{2})\mathrm{d}h_{1}\mathrm{d}h_{2}

has a limit

\int_{[\mathrm{H}_{1}]}\int_{[\mathrm{H}_{2}]}K_{f,b}(h_{1},h_{2})|\det h_{1}|^{s}\xi(h_{1})\eta(h_{2})\mathrm{d}h_{1}\mathrm{d}h_{2}.

Similar to the computation in part (1) of the proof, the integral

\int_{[\mathrm{H}_{1}]}\int_{[\mathrm{H}_{2}]}K_{f,b}^{T}(h_{1},h_{2})|\det h_{1}|^{s}\xi(h_{1})\eta(h_{2})\mathrm{d}h_{1}\mathrm{d}h_{2}

is of the form

I_{b}(f,\xi,s)+\sum_{Q\neq G\in\mathcal{F}^{\mathrm{st}}_{\mathrm{RS}}}P_{Q}(T)e^{\langle\underline{\rho}_{Q,s},T\rangle}

where
P_{Q}
is polynomial, hence as
T\infty
, it has the limit
I_{b}(f,\xi,s)
. Combining these, (2) is proved.
∎

Proof of (3).

Since
\mathrm{supp}(f_{v})\subset\mathrm{G}^{\prime}_{+}(F_{v})
, by the definition of
K_{f,b}(h_{1},h_{2})
and Lemma
2.1
, we see that
