K_{f,b}(h_{1},h_{2})=\sum_{(\delta_{1},\delta_{2})\in\mathrm{H}_{1}(F)\times\mathrm{H}_{2}(F)}f(\delta_{1}^{-1}\gamma\delta_{2}).

Therefore, the absolute convergence follows from (1), and the remaining part follows from (2).
∎

The integral (
4.2
) is Eulerian, we will then study the local version of it in the next section.

4.3.
An infinitesimal variant

The results in Theorem
4.2
have their infinitesimal analogues.

Theorem 4.3
.

Let
\mathfrak{g}=\mathfrak{g}_{n+1}
or
\widetilde{\gl_{n}}
. Let
\varphi\in\mathcal{S}(\mathfrak{g}(\mathbb{A}))
. If there exists a place
v
of
F
such that
\varphi
is of the form
\varphi_{v}\varphi^{v}
, with
\varphi_{v}\in\mathcal{S}(\mathfrak{g}(F_{v})),f^{v}\in\mathcal{S}(\mathfrak{g}(\mathbb{A}^{v}))
and
\supp(\varphi_{v})\subset\mathfrak{g}_{+}(F_{v})
(resp.
\mathfrak{g}_{-}(F_{v})
), then

(1)

for
s\in\mathcal{H}_{<-1}
(resp.
s\in\mathcal{H}_{>1}
), we have

\sum_{a\in\mathcal{B}(F)}\int_{[\GL_{n}]}\lvert K_{\varphi,a}(g)\rvert\lvert\det g\rvert^{\mathrm{Re}(s)}<\infty.

(2)

For
a\in(\mathfrak{g}/\GL_{n})(F)
, and
s\in\mathcal{H}_{<-1}
(resp.
\mathcal{H}_{>1}
) the integral

(4.5)

\int_{[\GL_{n}]}K_{\varphi,a}(g)\lvert\det g\rvert^{s}\xi(g)\eta(g)\mathrm{d}g

equals to
I_{a}(\varphi,\xi,s)
. In particular, as a function of
s
, the integral (
4.1
) is holomorphic on
\mathcal{H}_{<-1}
(resp.
\mathcal{H}_{>1}
) and has a meromorphic continuation to
\mathbb{C}
which is holomorphic on
\mathbb{C}\setminus\{-1,1\}
, and equals to
I_{a}(\varphi,\xi)
at
s=0
.

(3)

Pick any
\gamma\in O^{+}(a)
(resp.
\gamma\in O^{-}(a)
), then the integral

(4.6)

\int_{\GL_{n}(\mathbb{A})}\varphi(\gamma\cdot g)\lvert\det g\rvert^{s}\xi(g)\eta(g)\mathrm{d}g

is absolutely convergent on
\mathcal{H}_{<-1}
(resp.
\mathcal{H}_{>1}
) and equals to (
4.5
).

Proof.

The proof is identical to the proof of Theorem
4.2
, where we use the asymptotic properties in Proposition
A.5
instead.
∎

5.
Local Theory I: Normalized orbital integral

Throughout this section, we let
F
be a local field of characteristic zero. Let
\gamma\in\mathrm{G}^{\prime}_{\mathrm{reg}}(F)
,
f\in\mathcal{S}(\mathrm{G}^{\prime}(F))
and
\xi:F^{\times}\mathbb{C}^{\times}
be a unitary character. We will define a meromorphic function
I_{\gamma}(f,\xi,s)
in this section and study its properties in Subsection
5.3
. The function
I_{\gamma}(f,\xi,s)
is a regularization of the following integral

\int_{\mathrm{H}_{1}(F)\times\mathrm{H}_{2}(F)}f(h_{1}^{-1}\gamma h_{2})\xi(h_{1})\lvert h_{1}\rvert^{s}\eta(h_{2})\mathrm{d}h_{1}\mathrm{d}h_{2}.

This integral is divergent in general for any
s\in\mathbb{C}
, so how to regularize it will be the main part of this section. We will first study the infinitesimal version
I_{X}(\varphi,\xi,s)
in Subsection
5.1
and
5.2
, and the group version in Subsection
5.3
.

5.0.1.
Notations and Measures

We fix some notation and Haar measures throughout this section as follows: we fix an additive character
\psi
of
F
. If
F^{\prime}/F
is a finite extension, we always choose
\psi^{\prime}:=\psi\circ\mathrm{Tr}_{F^{\prime}/F}
as a fixed additive character on
F^{\prime}
. We endow
F^{\prime}
with the self-dual Haar measure with respect to
\psi^{\prime}
.

We fix the Haar measure on
\GL_{n}(F)
defined by the differential form
\zeta_{F}(1)\cdots\zeta_{F}(n)(\det g_{ij})^{-n}\wedge dg_{ij}
. We denote by
K
the standard maximal compact subgroup of
\GL_{n}(F)
. If
F
is non-Archimedean, then
K=\GL_{n}(\mathcal{O}_{F})
and
\mathrm{vol}(K)=\mathrm{vol}(\mathcal{O}_{F})^{n^{2}}
.

We also denote by
A_{n}
(resp.
N_{n}
) the diagonal subgroup (resp. upper triangular unipotent subgroup) of
\GL_{n}
, and we endow
A_{n}(F)
(resp.
N_{n}(F)
) with the Haar measure defined by differential form
\zeta_{F}(1)^{n}\prod da_{i}/a_{i}
(resp.
\prod dx_{ij}
). If
F
is non-Archimedean, one has
\vol(A_{n}(\mathcal{O}_{F}))=\vol(\mathcal{O}_{F})^{n}
(resp.
\vol(N_{n}(\mathcal{O}_{F}))=\vol(\mathcal{O}_{F})^{\frac{n(n-1)}{2}}
). Iwasawa decomposition yields the integration formula

(5.1)

\int_{\GL_{n}(F)}f(g)\mathrm{d}g=C\int_{A_{n}(F)}\int_{N_{n}(F)}\int_{K}f(ank)\mathrm{d}a\mathrm{d}n\mathrm{d}k,

where
C\in\mathbb{R}_{>0}
is a constant. When
F
is non-Archimedean, then
C=\frac{1}{\vol(\mathcal{O}_{F})^{\frac{n(n+1)}{2}}}
.

Write
B_{n}
for the upper triangular Borel subgroup, and let
\delta_{B_{n}}
be the modulus character on
B_{n}(F)
, for
a=\mathrm{diag}(a_{1},\cdots,a_{n})\in A_{n}(F)
, we have
\delta_{B_{n}}(a)=\lvert a_{1}\rvert^{n-1}\cdots\lvert a_{n}\rvert^{1-n}
.

Let
\langle\cdot,\cdot\rangle
be the
\GL_{n}(F)
-invariant bilinear pairing on
\widetilde{\gl_{n}}(F)
defined by

(5.2)

\langle(X_{1},v_{1},u_{1}),(X_{2},v_{2},u_{2})\rangle=\mathrm{Trace}(X_{1}X_{2})+u_{1}v_{2}+u_{2}v_{1}.

For
\varphi\in\mathcal{S}(\widetilde{\gl_{n}}(F))
, we define its Fourier transform by

\mathcal{F}\varphi(Y)=\int_{\widetilde{\gl_{n}}(F)}\varphi(X)\psi(\langle X,Y\rangle)\mathrm{d}X.

We then endow
\widetilde{\gl_{n}}(F)
with the self-dual measure, one check directly that this measure coincides with the product of measure on
F^{n^{2}}\times F^{n}\times F^{n}
when we use standard coordinates on
\widetilde{\gl_{n}}(F)
.

Let
\mathfrak{n}_{n}
and
\mathfrak{b}_{n}
be the Lie algebra of
N_{n}
and
B_{n}
respectively, and let
\mathfrak{n}_{n}^{\prime}
be the space of matrices
(a_{ij})
such that
a_{ij}=0
unless
j-i\geq 2
. We define

\widetilde{\mathfrak{n}_{n}}=\{(A,v,u)\in\widetilde{\gl_{n}}\mid A\in\mathfrak{n}_{n},v\in F^{n-1},u=0\},\quad\widetilde{\mathfrak{n}^{\prime}_{n}}=\{(A,v,u)\in\widetilde{\gl_{n}}\mid A\in\mathfrak{n}^{\prime}_{n},v\in F^{n-2},u=0\},

where
F^{n-1}
(resp.
F^{n-2}
) stands for the subspace of
F^{n}
with the last (resp. last two) coordinate 0.

We write
\widetilde{\mathfrak{b}_{n}}:=\{(A,v,u)\in\widetilde{\gl_{n}}\mid A\in\mathfrak{b}_{n},u=0\}
. All the vector spaces
\mathfrak{n}_{n},\mathfrak{b}_{n},\widetilde{\mathfrak{n}_{n}},\widetilde{\mathfrak{n}^{\prime}_{n}},\widetilde{\mathfrak{b}_{n}}
has a natural basis and can be identified with
F^{m}
for some
m\geq 0
. We then transport the product measure on
F^{m}
to the Haar measure on these vector spaces via this identification.

Recall the regular element
Z_{\lambda}^{+}
defined in (
2.22
). By
[
22
]
*Lemma 6.8 (also see
[
8
]
*Lemma 5.7.4), the map

(5.3)

N_{n}(F)Z_{\lambda}^{+}+\widetilde{\mathfrak{n}_{n}^{\prime}},\quad nZ_{\lambda}^{+}\cdot n

is a bijection and is measure-preserving.
By
[
8
]
*(5,7,8), we have the integration formula

(5.4)

\int_{\widetilde{\gl_{n}}(F)}f(Y)\mathrm{d}Y=\frac{1}{\zeta_{n}}\int_{N_{n}(F)\backslash\GL_{n}(F)}\int_{\widetilde{\mathfrak{b}_{n}}(F)}f((Z_{0}^{-}+Y)\cdot h)\mathrm{d}Y\mathrm{d}h.

where
f\in\mathcal{S}(\widetilde{\gl_{n}}(F))
and

\zeta_{n}=\zeta_{F}(1)\cdots\zeta_{F}(n).

5.1.
Orbital integrals on the Lie algebra: central and regular semisimple elements

Recall that we have a GIT quotient
\mathcal{A}:=\widetilde{\gl_{n}}/\GL_{n}
and the corresponding quotient map
q:\widetilde{\gl_{n}}\mathcal{A}
. Let
a\in\mathcal{A}(F)
be a central element, by Lemma
2.2
, in the fiber
\widetilde{\gl_{n}}_{,a}
of
a
, there are two regular orbits
O^{+}(a)
and
O^{-}(a)
.

We define the
L
-factors associated to the orbits
O^{+}(a)
and
O^{-}(a)
to be

L_{a}^{+}(s,\xi)=\prod_{i=1}^{n}L(-is-i+1,(\xi\cdot\eta)^{-i}),\quad L_{a}^{-}(s,\xi)=\prod_{i=1}^{n}L(is-i+1,(\xi\cdot\eta)^{i}).

For a regular element
X
in
\widetilde{\gl_{n}}_{,a}(F)
. We put

L_{X}(s,\xi)=\begin{cases}L_{a}^{+}(s,\xi)&\text{ if }X\in O^{+}(a),\\
L_{a}^{-}(s,\xi)&\text{ if }X\in O^{-}(a).\end{cases}

Let
\chi:F^{\times}\mathbb{C}^{\times}
be a character. We say that
\varphi\in\mathcal{S}(\widetilde{\gl_{n}}(F))
is
\chi
-unstable
, if for all regular semisimple element
X\in\widetilde{\gl_{n}}(F)
, we have

\int_{\GL_{n}(F)}\varphi(X\cdot g)\chi(g)\mathrm{d}g=0.

We call a continuous functional (i.e. a distribution)
I:\mathcal{S}(\widetilde{\gl_{n}}(F))\mathbb{C}
is
\chi
-stable
, if for any
\chi
-unstable function
\varphi\in\mathcal{S}(\widetilde{\gl_{n}}(F))
, we have
I(\varphi)=0
.

For
g\in\GL_{n}(F)
and
\varphi\in\mathcal{S}(\widetilde{\gl_{n}}(F))
, we let
\mathrm{R}(g)\varphi
denote the right translation given by
\mathrm{R}(g)\varphi(X)=\varphi(X\cdot g)
. Note that if
I
is a
\chi
-stable distribution, then for any
\varphi\in\mathcal{S}(\widetilde{\gl_{n}}(F))
and
g\in\GL_{n}(F)
, we have

I(\mathrm{R}(g)\varphi)=\chi^{-1}(g)I(\varphi).

Proposition 5.1
.

Let
a\in\mathcal{A}(F)
be a central element and
X\in\widetilde{\gl_{n}}_{,a}(F)
be a regular element. Let
\varphi\in\mathcal{S}(\widetilde{\gl_{n}}(F))
and
s\in\mathbb{C}
. Consider the integral

I_{X}(\varphi,\xi,s):=\int_{\GL_{n}(F)}\varphi(X\cdot g)\xi(g)\eta(g)\lvert\det g\rvert^{s}\mathrm{d}g.

Then we have the following statements:

(1)

If
X\in O^{+}(a)
(resp.
X\in O^{-}(a)
),
I_{X}(\varphi,\xi,s)
is absolutely convergent on
\mathcal{H}_{<-1+\frac{1}{n}}
(resp.
\mathcal{H}_{>1-\frac{1}{n}}
) and has meromorphic continuation to
\mathbb{C}
, with poles contained in the poles of
L_{X}(s,\xi)
.

(2)

For
s\in\mathbb{C}
which is not a pole of
L_{X}(s,\xi)
, the resulting linear map

\mathcal{S}(\widetilde{\gl_{n}}(F))\mathbb{C},\quad\varphi I_{X}(\varphi,\xi,s).

is continuous.

(3)

As a function of
s

I_{X}^{\natural}(\varphi,\xi,s):=\frac{I_{X}(\varphi,\xi,s)}{L_{X}(s,\xi)}

is entire for any
\varphi\in\mathcal{S}(\widetilde{\gl_{n}}(F))
, and we can choose
\varphi
such that it equals to 1.

(4)

If
F
is non-Archimedean with ring of integer
\mathcal{O}
, suppose that
\varphi=1_{\widetilde{\gl}(\mathcal{O})}
,
\xi
and
\eta
are unramified,
\vol(\mathcal{O}_{F})=1
,
X\in\widetilde{\gl_{n}}_{,\pm}(\mathcal{O})
, then

I_{X}(\varphi,s,\xi)=L_{X}(s,\xi).

(5)

For any
s
which is not a pole of
L_{X}(s,\xi)
, the distribution
I_{X}(\cdot,\xi,s)
satisfies the following two properties:

•

I_{X}(\cdot,\xi,s)
is a
\xi\eta\lvert\cdot\rvert^{s}
-stable distribution.

•

I_{X}(\cdot,\xi,s)
is supported on the closure of
\GL_{n}(F)
-orbit of
X
.

Proof.

We prove the case where
X\in O^{+}(a)
, and the case
X\in O^{-}(a)
follows from the same argument. Assume
a
is the image of
(\lambda\cdot\mathrm{id},0,0)\in\widetilde{\gl_{n}(F)}
.

We first prove this proposition when
X=Z_{\lambda}^{+}
. Let
\chi=\xi\cdot\eta
. By Iwasawa decomposition (
5.1
) and the fact that the map (
5.3
) is measure preserving, the integral defining
I_{X}(\varphi,\xi,s)
can be written as

(5.5)

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\int_{N_{n}(F)\backslash\GL_{n}(F)}\int_{\widetilde{\mathfrak{n}^{\prime}_{n}}(F)}\varphi((Z_{\lambda}^{+}+N)\cdot g)\chi(g)\lvert\det g\rvert^{s}\mathrm{d}N\mathrm{d}g\\
=C&\int_{A_{n}(F)}\int_{K}\int_{\widetilde{\mathfrak{n}^{\prime}_{n}}(F)}\delta_{B_{n}}(a)^{-1}\varphi((Z_{\lambda}^{+}+N)\cdot ak)\chi(ak)\lvert\det a\rvert^{s}\mathrm{d}N\mathrm{d}a\mathrm{d}k,\end{split}

where
C
is the constant appearing in (
5.1
). Let
f_{\varphi}
be the Schwartz function on
F^{n}
defined by

f_{\varphi}(x_{1},\cdots,x_{n})=C\int_{\widetilde{\mathfrak{n}^{\prime}_{n}}(F)}\int_{K}\varphi\left((X_{\lambda}(x_{1},\cdots,x_{n})+N)\cdot k\right)\chi(k)\mathrm{d}N\mathrm{d}k,

where

X_{\lambda}(x_{1},\cdots,x_{n})=\left(\begin{pmatrix}\lambda&x_{1}&0&\cdots&0\\
0&\lambda&x_{2}&\cdots&0\\
0&0&\lambda&\cdots&0\\
\cdots&\cdots&\cdots&\cdots&\cdots\\
0&0&0&\cdots&\lambda\end{pmatrix},\begin{pmatrix}0\\
0\\
0\\
\cdots\\
x_{n}\end{pmatrix},0\right)

Then the integral (
5.5
) reduces to

\int_{A_{n}(F)}f_{\varphi}\left(\frac{a_{2}}{a_{1}},\cdots,\frac{a_{n}}{a_{n-1}},\frac{1}{a_{n}}\right)\lvert a_{2}\cdots a_{n}\rvert\lvert\det a\rvert^{s}\mathrm{d}a.

Let
b_{i}=a_{i+1}/a_{i}
for
1\leq i\leq n-1
and
b_{n}=1/a_{n}
, the integral above equals to

(5.6)

\int_{(F^{\times})^{n}}f_{\varphi}(b_{1},b_{2},\cdots,b_{n})\prod_{i=1}^{n}\chi(b_{i})^{-i}\lvert b_{i}\rvert^{-is-i+1}\mathrm{d}b_{1}\cdots\mathrm{d}b_{n}.

Note that
\varphi\in\mathcal{S}(\widetilde{{\gl_{n}}}(F))f_{\varphi}\in\mathcal{S}(F^{n})
is continuous, part (1)–(3) also follows from Tate thesis. For part (4), we compute directly that
f_{\varphi}=1_{\mathcal{O}^{n}}
, by the unramified computation in Tate’s thesis,
I_{Z_{\lambda}^{+}}(\varphi,s,\xi)=L_{Z_{\lambda}^{+}}(s,\xi)
.

We finally show part (5). The support of
I_{X}(\cdot,\xi,s)
lies in the closure of orbit of
X
follows from the definition, to show stability, following the calculation in
[
6
]
*Subsection 5.7, Let
Z^{-}_{0}
be the element in (
2.23
).
Consider the following iterated integral

(5.7)

I_{\lambda}^{1}(\varphi,\xi,s):=\int_{N_{n}(F)\backslash\GL_{n}(F)}\left(\int_{\widetilde{\mathfrak{n}_{n}}(F)}\varphi(Z^{+}_{\lambda}\cdot g)\psi(\langle Z_{0}^{-},X\rangle)\mathrm{d}X\right)\chi(g)\lvert\det g\rvert^{s}\mathrm{d}g.

Using Iwasawa decomposition, the above integral is equal to

\int_{A_{n}(F)}\int_{F^{n}}f_{\varphi}(x_{1},\cdots,x_{n})\psi\left(\frac{a_{1}}{a_{2}}x_{1}+\cdots+\frac{a_{n-1}}{a_{n}}x_{n-1}+a_{n}x_{n}\right)\lvert a_{1}\cdots a_{n}\rvert^{s+1}\chi(a)\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}a.

Let
b_{i}=a_{i}/a_{i+1}
for
1\leq i\leq n-1
and
b_{n}=a_{n}
, this can be written as

\int_{(F^{\times})^{n}}\widehat{f_{\varphi}}(b_{1},\cdots,b_{n})\prod_{i=1}^{n}\lvert b_{i}\rvert^{i(s+1)}\chi(b_{i})^{i}\mathrm{d}b_{1}\cdots\mathrm{d}b_{n}.

We thus see that the integral defining
I^{1}_{\lambda}(\varphi,\xi,s)
is convergent when
\mathrm{Re}(s)>-1
and by the functional equation for local Tate’s zeta integral, we have

I^{1}_{\lambda}(\varphi,\xi,s)=\gamma^{+}_{\xi}(s)I_{Z_{\lambda}^{+}}(\varphi,\xi,s),

where

\gamma^{+}_{\xi}(s)=\prod_{i=1}^{n}\gamma(-is-i+1,(\xi\cdot\eta)^{-i},\psi).

In other words, when
\mathrm{Re}(s)>-1
, the meromorphic continuation of
I_{X}(\varphi,\xi,s)
is given explicitly by

(5.8)

I_{Z_{\lambda}^{+}}(\varphi,\xi,s)=\gamma^{+}_{\xi}(s)^{-1}I^{1}(\varphi,\xi,s),

Let
\omega^{-}_{\xi,s}
be the function on
\widetilde{\gl_{n}}(F)
defined by

\omega^{-}_{\xi,s}(X)=\chi(\delta^{-}(X))\lvert\delta^{-}(X)\rvert^{s}.

Put
\varphi_{\lambda}(X)=\varphi(X+(\lambda,0,0))
.

Direct computation shows that for
Y\in\widetilde{\mathfrak{b}_{n}}(F)
, we have

\omega^{-}_{\xi,s}((Z_{0}^{-}+Y)\cdot g)=\chi(g)\lvert\det g\rvert^{s}\omega_{\xi,s}(Z_{0}^{-}).

By Fourier inversion, we can write

I_{\lambda}^{1}(\varphi,\xi,s)=\int_{N_{n}(F)\backslash\GL_{n}(F)}\int_{\widetilde{\mathfrak{b}_{n}}(F)}\mathcal{F}\varphi_{\lambda}((Z_{0}^{-}+Y)\cdot g)\chi(g)\lvert\det g\rvert^{s}\mathrm{d}Y\mathrm{d}g.

By (
5.4
), we then see that

I_{\lambda}^{1}(\varphi,\xi,s)=\zeta_{n}\omega^{-}_{\xi,s}(Z_{0}^{-})^{-1}\int_{\widetilde{\gl_{n}}(F)}\mathcal{F}\varphi_{\lambda}(X)\omega^{-}_{\xi,s}(X)\mathrm{d}X.

By
[
12
]
*Corollary 3.1.8.2, if
\varphi
is
\xi\eta\lvert\cdot\rvert^{s}
-unstable, then so is
\mathcal{F}\varphi
. (Indeed, in
loc.cit
only proves the case when
\xi
is trivial and
s=0
, but the same proof works in general). Therefore

I_{\lambda}^{1}(\varphi,\xi,s)=\zeta_{n}\omega^{-}_{\xi,s}(Z_{0}^{-})^{-1}\int_{\mathcal{A}_{\mathrm{rs}}(F)}\int_{\GL_{n}(F)}\mathcal{F}\varphi_{\lambda}(X\cdot g)\chi(g)\lvert\det g\rvert^{s}\omega_{\xi,s}^{-}(X)\mathrm{d}g\mathrm{d}X=0.

Together with (
5.8
), we see that the distribution
I_{X}(\cdot,\xi,s)
is
\xi\eta\lvert\cdot\rvert^{s}
-stable. This finishes the proof when
X=Z_{\lambda}^{+}
.

Now let
X\in O^{+}(a)
be a general element, then it can be written of the form
Z_{\lambda}^{+}\cdot g
in (
2.22
). By a change of variable, we see that when convergent, we have

(5.9)

I_{Z_{\lambda}^{+}}(\varphi,\xi,s)=\xi(g)\eta(g)\lvert\det g\rvert^{s}\cdot I_{X}(\varphi,\xi,s).

Part (1),(2), and (5) of the proposition directly follows from this. In the Tate integral (
5.6
), for any
a\in F
we can choose
f_{\varphi}
such that the integral gives
\lvert a\rvert^{s}\cdot L_{Z_{\lambda}^{+}}(s)
, therefore part (3) holds. Part (4) follows from the fact that if
X\in\widetilde{\gl_{n}}_{,+}(\mathcal{O})
then
g\in\GL_{n}(\mathcal{O}_{F})
(see
[
22
]
*Proposition 6.3).
∎

According to the equation (
5.8
) in the proof above, for
\mathrm{Re}(s)>-1
, we have the following expression

(5.10)

I_{Z^{+}_{\lambda}}(\varphi,\xi,s)=\zeta_{n}\omega^{-}_{\xi,s}(Z_{0}^{-})^{-1}\gamma^{+}_{\xi}(s)^{-1}\int_{\widetilde{\gl_{n}}(F)}\mathcal{F}\varphi_{\lambda}(X)\omega^{-}_{\xi,s}(X)\mathrm{d}X.

By the same computation, if we denote by
\omega^{+}_{\xi,s}
the function on
\widetilde{\gl_{n}}(F)
defined by

\omega_{\xi,s}^{+}(X)=\xi^{-1}(\delta^{+}(X))\eta(\delta^{+}(X))\lvert\delta^{+}(X)\rvert^{-s}.

Then for
\mathrm{Re}(s)<1
, we then have

(5.11)

I_{Z_{\lambda}^{-}}(\varphi,\xi,s)=\zeta_{n}\omega^{+}_{\xi,s}(Z_{0}^{+})^{-1}\gamma_{\xi}^{-}(s)^{-1}\int_{\widetilde{\gl_{n}}(F)}\mathcal{F}\varphi_{\lambda}(X)\omega_{\xi,s}^{+}(X)\mathrm{d}X.

where

\gamma^{-}_{\xi}(s)=\prod_{i=1}^{n}\gamma(is-i+1,(\xi\cdot\eta)^{i},\psi).

Now we switch to the case when
a\in\mathcal{A}(F)
is a regular semisimple element. In this case, the fiber
\widetilde{\gl_{n}}_{,a}(F)
forms a single
\GL_{n}(F)
orbit, any element in this orbit is regular. For
X\in\widetilde{\gl_{n}}_{,a}(F)
, we put

L_{X}(s,\xi)=1.

The following proposition is analogous to (and easier than) Proposition
5.1

Proposition 5.2
.

Let
a\in\mathcal{A}(F)
be a regular semisimple element and
X\in\widetilde{\gl_{n}}_{,a}(F)
. Let
\varphi\in\mathcal{S}(\widetilde{\gl_{n}}(F))
. Consider the integral

I_{X}(\varphi,\xi,s):=\int_{\GL_{n}(F)}\varphi(X\cdot g)\xi(g)\eta(g)\lvert\det g\rvert^{s}\mathrm{d}g.

Then we have the following statements:

(1)

The integral
I_{X}(\varphi,\xi,s)
is absolutely convergent for any
s\in\mathbb{C}
and defines an entire function on
\mathbb{C}
.

(2)

For any
s\in\mathbb{C}
, the resulting map

\mathcal{S}(\widetilde{\gl_{n}}(F))\mathbb{C},\quad\varphi I_{X}(\varphi,\xi,\cdot)

is continuous.

(3)

As a function of
s

I_{X}^{\natural}(\varphi,\xi,s):=\frac{I_{X}(\varphi,\xi,s)}{L_{X}(s,\xi)}=I_{X}(\varphi,\xi,s)

is entire for any
\varphi\in\mathcal{S}(\widetilde{\gl_{n}}(F))
,and we can choose
\varphi
such that it equals to 1.

(4)

If
F
is non-archimedean with ring of integer
\mathcal{O}
, suppose that
\varphi=1_{\widetilde{\gl}(\mathcal{O})}
,
X\in\widetilde{\gl_{n}}_{,\mathrm{rs}}(\mathcal{O})
,
\xi
and
\eta
are unramified and
\vol(\mathcal{O}_{F})=1
, then

I_{X}(\varphi,s,\xi)=L_{X}(s,\xi).

(5)

For any
s\in\mathbb{C}
, the distribution
I_{X}(\cdot,\xi,s)
satisfies the following two properties:

•

I_{X}(\cdot,\xi,s)
is a
\xi\eta\lvert\cdot\rvert^{s}
-stable distribution.

•

I_{X}(\cdot,\xi,s)
is supported on the orbit of
X
.

Proof.

Since
X
is regular semisimple, its orbit is closed, and as a function of
g
,
g\varphi_{s}(X\cdot g)
is then compactly supported. The integral defining
I_{X}(\varphi_{s},\xi,s)
is therefore absolutely convergent and entire for
s\in\mathbb{C}
. This proves (1) and (2), for (3), the orbit of
X
is a closed subset in the non-archimedean case and a closed Nash submanifold in the archimedean case. Take a Schwartz function
\varphi^{\prime}
on the orbit of
X
such that

\int_{\GL_{n}(F)}\varphi^{\prime}(X\cdot g)\xi(g)\eta(g)\lvert\det g\rvert^{s}\mathrm{d}g=1.

we can then extend
\varphi^{\prime}
to a Schwartz function
\varphi
on
\widetilde{\gl_{n}}(F)
. (For Archimedean case, see
[
1
]
, Theorem 4.6.1). The function
\varphi
satisfies the requirement of (3).

For part (4), We show that
\varphi(X\cdot g)=1
if and only if
g\in\GL_{n}(\mathcal{O}_{F})
. In fact,
X=(A,v,u)\in\widetilde{\gl_{n}}_{,\mathrm{rs}}(\mathcal{O})
implies that
v,Av,\cdots,A^{n-1}v
is a
\mathcal{O}
-basis of
\mathcal{O}^{n}
, and
u,uA,\cdots,uA^{n-1}
is a
\mathcal{O}
-basis of
\mathcal{O}_{n}
. Therefore, if
X\cdot g\in\gl_{n}(\mathcal{O})
, then
g^{-1}(v,Av,\cdots,A^{n-1}v)\in\gl_{n}(\mathcal{O})
, hence
g^{-1}\in\gl_{n}(\mathcal{O})
, similarly
(u,uA,\cdots,uA^{n-1})g\in\gl_{n}(\mathcal{O})
implies
g\in\gl_{n}(\mathcal{O})
, hence
g\in\GL_{n}(\mathcal{O})
. Therefore, the integral
I^{+}_{a}(\varphi,s,\xi)
is just the volume of
\GL_{n}(\mathcal{O}_{F})
, which is
1
. Finally, since
a
is regular semisimple, part (5) is trivial in this case.
∎

5.2.
Orbital Integral on the Lie algebra: general element

Let
a\in\mathcal{A}(F)
be a general element, we use the notation in descent construction in Subsection
2.5
. From the element
a
, we have an associated embedding
\iota_{\mathfrak{h}}:\widetilde{\mathfrak{h}}^{\prime}\widetilde{\gl_{n}}
, where the induced map
\iota_{\mathcal{A}}:\mathcal{A}_{H}^{\prime}\mathcal{A}
sending
a_{H}
to
a
and each component
a_{i}(0\leq i\leq k)
of
a_{H}
is either regular semisimple (for
i=0
) or central (for
i>0
).

By Lemma
2.2
, there are
2^{k}
regular orbits for the action of
H(F)
on
\widetilde{\mathfrak{h}}_{a_{H}}(F)
. Let
\mathcal{E}
be the set of functions from
\{1,\cdots,k\}
to
\{+,-\}
. For each
\varepsilon\in\mathcal{E}
, there is a regular orbit
O^{\varepsilon}(a)
in
\widetilde{\mathfrak{h}}_{a_{H}}
defined by

O^{\varepsilon}(a_{H}):=\widetilde{\mathfrak{h}_{0}}_{,a_{0}}(F)\times\prod_{1\leq k\leq i}O^{\varepsilon_{i}}(a_{i}),

For each
1\leq i\leq k
, we put

\xi_{i}=\xi\circ\mathrm{Nm}_{F_{i}/F},\quad\eta_{i}=\eta_{(F_{i}\otimes_{F}E)/F_{i}}=\eta\circ\mathrm{Nm}_{F_{i}/F},

both are unitary characters of
F_{i}^{\times}
. By an abuse of notation, we also use
\xi
and
\eta
to denote a character on
H(F)
defined by

\xi(h):=\prod_{i=0}^{k}\xi_{i}(\det h_{i}),\quad\eta(h):=\prod_{i=0}^{k}\eta_{i}(\det h_{i}).

We then define

L_{a_{i}}^{+}(\xi,s)=\prod_{j=1}^{n_{i}}L(-js-j+1,(\xi_{i}\cdot\eta_{i})^{-j}),\quad L_{a_{i}}^{-}(\xi,s)=\prod_{j=1}^{n_{i}}L(js-j+1,(\xi_{i}\cdot\eta_{i})^{j}).

For
\varepsilon\in\mathcal{E}
, we define an
L
-factor by

(5.12)

L_{a_{H}}^{\varepsilon}(\xi,s)=\prod_{i=1}^{k}L_{a_{i}}^{\varepsilon_{i}}(s,\xi).

Let
X\in\widetilde{\mathfrak{h}}_{a_{H}}(F)
be a regular element, suppose that
X\in O^{\varepsilon}(a_{H})
, we then put

(5.13)

L_{X}(s,\xi)=L_{a_{H}}^{\varepsilon}(s,\xi),

By Proposition
5.1
and Proposition
5.2
, for each
0\leq i\leq k
and any
s\in\mathbb{C}
which is not a pole of
L^{\varepsilon}_{a_{H}}(s,\xi)
, there exists a continuous linear map

\mathcal{S}(\widetilde{\mathfrak{h}_{i}}(F))\mathbb{C},\quad\varphi I_{X_{i}}(\varphi,\xi,s).

Identifying
\mathcal{S}(\widetilde{\mathfrak{h}}(F))
with
\widehat{\bigotimes}_{i=0}^{k}\mathcal{S}(\widetilde{\mathfrak{h}_{i}}(F))
, for any regular element
X\in\widetilde{\mathfrak{h}}_{a_{H}}(F)
and
s\in\mathbb{C}
which is not a pole of
L_{X}(s,\xi)
, we define the distribution
I_{X}(\cdot,\xi,s)
as the tensor product of those
I_{X_{i}}(,\xi,s)
. If
\varphi
is factorizable in the sense that

\varphi(X_{0},\cdots,X_{k})=\prod_{i=0}^{k}\varphi_{i}(X_{i})

where
\varphi_{i}\in\mathcal{S}(\widetilde{\mathfrak{h}_{i}}(F))
, then we have

I_{X}(\varphi,\xi,s)=\prod_{i=0}^{k}I_{X_{i}}(\varphi_{i},\xi_{i},s).

Combining Proposition
5.1
and
5.2
, we have following result

Corollary 5.3
.

Let
a_{H}\in\mathcal{A}_{H}(F)
as above and let
\varphi\in\mathcal{S}(\widetilde{\mathfrak{h}}(F))
, and
X
be a regular element in
\widetilde{\mathfrak{h}}_{a_{H}}(F)
. We then have the following assertions

(1)

As a function of
s
,
I_{X}(\varphi,\xi,s)
is meromorphic on
\mathbb{C}
, and the pole is contained in the pole of
L_{X}(s,\xi)
.

(2)

For
s\in\mathbb{C}
which is not a pole of
L_{X}(s,\xi)
, the resulting linear map

\mathcal{S}(\widetilde{\mathfrak{h}}(F))\mathbb{C},\quad\varphi I_{X}(\varphi,\xi,s).

is continuous.

(3)

As a function of
s

I_{X}^{\natural}(\varphi,\xi,s):=\frac{I_{X}(\varphi,\xi,s)}{L_{X}(s,\xi)}

is entire for any
\varphi\in\mathcal{S}(\widetilde{\gl_{n}}(F))
, and we can choose
\varphi
such that it equals to 1.

(4)

If
F
is non-archimedean with ring of integer
\mathcal{O}
, suppose that
\varphi=1_{\widetilde{\mathfrak{h}}(\mathcal{O})}
,
\xi
and
\eta
are unramified, each
F_{i}
is unramified over
F
,
\vol(\mathcal{O}_{F})=1
and
X\in\mathfrak{h}_{0,\mathrm{rs}}(\mathcal{O})\times\widetilde{\mathfrak{h}^{\varepsilon}}(\mathcal{O})
, then

I_{X}(\varphi,s,\xi)=L_{X}(s,\xi).

(5)

For any
s
which is not a pole of
L_{X}(s,\xi)
, the distribution
I_{X}(\cdot,\xi,s)
satisfies the following two properties:

•

I_{X}(\cdot,\xi,s)
is a
\xi\eta\lvert\cdot\rvert^{s}
-stable distribution.

•

I_{X}(\cdot,\xi,s)
is supported on the closure of the
\GL_{n}(F)
orbit of
X
.

The notion of stable distribution on
\widetilde{\mathfrak{h}}(F)
is defined in the same way as in the beginning of Subsection
5.1
.

Now let
X\in\widetilde{\gl_{n}}_{,a}(F)
be a regular element. Assume that
X
is of type
\varepsilon
(see Proposition
2.3
), thus there exists
X_{H}\in O^{\varepsilon}(a_{H})
and
g\in\GL_{n}(F)
such that
\iota_{\mathfrak{h}}(X_{H})=X\cdot g
, we set

(5.14)

L_{X}(s,\xi):=L_{X_{H}}(s,\xi).

For any
s\in\mathbb{C}
which is not a pole of
L_{X}(x,\xi)
, we will now construct a map

\mathcal{S}(\widetilde{\gl_{n}}(F))\mathbb{C},\quad\varphi I_{X}(\varphi,\xi,\cdot),

the construction will consist of several steps.

First of all, since
\iota_{\mathcal{A}}:\mathcal{A}^{\prime}_{H}\mathcal{A}
is étale and sends
a_{H}
to
a
, we can choose an open neighbourhood
\omega_{H}
of
a_{H}
in
\mathcal{A}^{\prime}_{H}(F)
and an open neighbourhood
\omega
of
a
in
\mathcal{A}(F)
such that

•

If
F
is archimedean, both
\omega
and
\omega_{H}
are semi-algebraic
[
9
]
*Proposition 8.1.2.

•

\iota_{\mathcal{A}}
induces a bijection
\omega_{H}\omega
, which is an isomorphism of Nash manifolds if
F
is archimedean, and isomorphism of analytic manifolds if
F
is non-archimedean,

Let
\Omega:=q^{-1}(\omega)
and
\Omega_{H}:=q_{H}^{-1}(\omega_{H})
. Then the top horizontal map in diagram (
2.20
) induces an isomorphism

(5.15)

\Omega_{H}\times^{H(F)}\GL_{n}(F)\Omega,\quad(Y,g)\iota_{\mathfrak{h}}(Y)\cdot g

of Nash manifolds when
F
is archimedean and analytic manifolds if
F
is non-archimedean.

Given
\varphi\in\mathcal{S}(\Omega)
. Let
\varphi^{\prime}\in\mathcal{S}(\Omega_{H}\times\GL_{n}(F))
such that for any
(Y,g)\in\Omega_{H}\times\GL_{n}(F)
we have

(5.16)

\varphi(\iota_{\mathfrak{h}}(Y)\cdot g)=\int_{H(F)}\varphi^{\prime}(Y\cdot h,h^{-1}g)\mathrm{d}h.

The existence of
\varphi^{\prime}
follows from the fact that
\Omega_{H}\times\GL_{n}(F)\Omega_{H}\times^{H(F)}\GL_{n}(F)\cong\Omega
is a submersion. For any
s\in\mathbb{C}
, we put

(5.17)

\varphi_{H,s}(Y)=\int_{\GL_{n}(F)}\varphi^{\prime}(Y,g)\xi(g)\eta(g)\lvert\det g\rvert^{s}\mathrm{d}g,

then
\varphi_{H,s}\in\mathcal{S}(\Omega_{H})
for any
s\in\mathbb{C}
and the map
\mathbb{C}\mathcal{S}(\Omega_{H}),s\varphi_{H,s}
is holomorphic.

Lemma 5.4
.

For
s\in\mathbb{C}
, we can regard
\varphi_{H,s}
as a Schwartz function on
\widetilde{\mathfrak{h}}(F)
, if
s
is not a pole of
L_{X}(s,\xi)
, then the complex number

I_{X_{H}}(\varphi_{H,s},\xi,s)

is independent of the choice of
\varphi^{\prime}
defining
\varphi_{H,s}
.

Proof.

Let
\varphi^{\prime\prime}
be another Schwartz function on
\mathcal{S}(\Omega_{H}\times\GL_{n}(F))
such that for any
(Y,g)\in\mathcal{S}(\Omega_{H}\times\GL_{n}(F)
, the equation

\varphi(\iota_{\mathfrak{h}}(Y)\cdot g)=\int_{H(F)}\varphi^{\prime\prime}(Y\cdot h,h^{-1}g)\mathrm{d}h.

holds. We put

\psi_{H,s}(Y)=\int_{\GL_{n}(F)}\varphi^{\prime\prime}_{s}(Y,g)\xi(g)\eta(g)\lvert\det g\rvert^{s}\mathrm{d}g.

Switching the order of integral shows that the function
\varphi_{H,s}-\psi_{H,s}
is
\xi\eta\lvert\cdot\rvert^{s}
-unstable. By Corollary
5.3
(5),
I_{X_{H}}(\varphi_{H,s},\xi,s)=I_{X_{H}}(\psi_{H,s},\xi,s)
.
∎

Recall that there is a unique
g\in\GL_{n}(F)
such that
X=\iota_{\mathfrak{h}}(X_{H})\cdot g
. For
\varphi\in\mathcal{S}(\Omega)
, we define

(5.18)

I_{X}(\varphi,\xi,s):=\xi(g)^{-1}\eta(g)\lvert\det g\rvert^{-s}I_{X_{H}}(\varphi_{H,s},\xi,s).

By Lemma
5.4
,
I_{X}(\varphi,\xi,s)
is a well-defined meromorphic function on
\mathbb{C}
.

Lemma 5.5
.

For
\varphi\in\mathcal{S}(\Omega)
, the meromorphic function
I_{X}(\varphi,\xi,s)
only depends on the value of
\varphi
on the orbit of
X
(i.e. it is supported on the closure of the orbit of
X
).

Proof.

We are reduced to show that if
\varphi
is zero on the orbit of
X
, then
I_{X}(\varphi,\xi,s)=0
. For this
\varphi
, we can pick the
\varphi^{\prime}\in\mathcal{S}(\Omega_{H}\times\GL_{n}(F))
such that
\varphi^{\prime}
vanishes on
\Omega_{H}\times\{\text{orbit of }X\}
. Then
\varphi_{H,s}
vanishes on orbit of
X_{H}
, the result hence follows from
5.3
(5).
∎

Remark 5.6
.

As we mentioned in Subsection
2.5
, the map
\iota_{\mathfrak{h}}
depends on the choices of the isomorphisms
i
and
i^{0}
, but we can check directly that the definition of
I_{X}(\varphi,\xi,s)
is independent of the choice of these isomorphisms, since different
i
and
i^{0}
differ by conjugation by an element in
\GL_{n}(F)
.

Now we finally give the definition of
I_{X}(\varphi,\xi,s)
.

Definition 5.7
.

Pick
u\in C_{c}^{\infty}(\omega)
with
u(a)=1
. Let
\varphi\in\mathcal{S}(\widetilde{\gl_{n}}(F))
, and let
s\in\mathbb{C}
which is not a pole of
L_{X}(s,\xi)
we define

I_{X}(\varphi,\xi,s):=I_{X}(\varphi\cdot(u\circ q),\xi,s),

where the right hand side is defined as (
5.18
).

By Lemma
5.5
, the definition of
I_{X}(\varphi,\xi,s)
is independent of choice of
u
. We now study the properties of
I_{X}(\varphi,\xi,s)

Proposition 5.8
.

We have the following assertions

(1)

For
\varphi\in\mathcal{S}(\widetilde{\gl_{n}}(F))
, as a function of
s
,
I_{X}(\varphi,\xi,s)
is meromorphic on
\mathbb{C}
, and its pole is contained in the pole of
L_{X}(s,\xi)
.

(2)

For
s\in\mathbb{C}
which is not a pole of
L_{X}(s,\xi)
, the resulting linear map

\mathcal{S}(\widetilde{\gl_{n}}(F))\mathbb{C},\quad\varphi I_{X}(\varphi,\xi,s).

is continuous.

(3)

As a function of
s

\frac{I_{X}(\varphi,\xi,s)}{L_{X}(s,\xi)}

is entire for any
\varphi\in\mathcal{S}(\widetilde{\gl_{n}}(F))
.

(4)

If
F
is non-archimedean with ring of integer
\mathcal{O}
,
F_{i}
are unramified over
F
,
\xi
and
\eta
are unramified and
X\in\widetilde{\gl_{n}}_{,\pm}(\mathcal{O})
and
a_{H}\in\mathcal{A}_{H}^{\prime}(\mathcal{O})
,
\vol(\mathcal{O}_{F})=\vol(\mathcal{O}_{F_{i}})=1
,
X
can be written of the form
\iota_{\mathfrak{h}}(X_{H})\cdot g
, with
X_{H}\in\mathfrak{h}_{0,\mathrm{rs}}(\mathcal{O})\times\mathfrak{h}^{\varepsilon}(\mathcal{O})
and
g\in\GL_{n}(\mathcal{O})
, then

I_{X}(1_{\widetilde{\gl_{n}}(\mathcal{O})},s,\xi)=L_{X}(s,\xi).

(5)

For any
s
which is not a pole of
L_{X}(s,\xi)
, the distribution
I_{X}(\cdot,s,\xi)
satisfies the following two properties:

•

I_{X}(\cdot,\xi,s)
is a
\xi\eta\lvert\cdot\rvert^{s}
-stable distribution.

•

I_{X}(\cdot,\xi,s)
is supported on the closure of
\GL_{n}(F)
orbit of
X
.

Proof.

Without loss of generality, we can assume
\iota_{\mathfrak{h}}(X_{H})=X
(otherwise, we can choose a different
i
and
i^{0}
), part (1) and (2) then follows from Corollary
5.3
(1), (2) and the fact that map
\mathcal{S}(\widetilde{\gl_{n}}(F))\mathcal{S}(\widetilde{\mathfrak{h}}(F)),\varphi\varphi_{H,s}
. For part (3), note that for a fixed
s=s_{0}
, the analytic property of
I_{X}(\varphi,\xi,s)/L_{X}(s,\xi)
only depends on
\varphi_{H,s_{0}}
, therefore (3) follows from Corollary
5.3
(3). Under the assumption of (4), by
[
11
]
*Lemme 3.4.5.1, for all
Y\in\widetilde{\mathfrak{h}}(F)
and
g\in G(F)
, we have

1_{\widetilde{\gl_{n}}(\mathcal{O})}(\iota_{\mathfrak{h}}(Y)\cdot g)=\int_{H(F)}1_{\widetilde{\mathfrak{h}}(\mathcal{O})}(Y\cdot h)\cdot 1_{\GL_{n}(\mathcal{O})}(h^{-1}g)\mathrm{d}h.

Therefore, if we choose any
u\in C_{c}^{\infty}(\omega)
with
u(a)=1
and let
\varphi=1_{\widetilde{\gl_{n}}}\cdot(u\circ q)
. Then
\varphi^{\prime}:(Y,g)1_{\widetilde{\mathfrak{h}}(\mathcal{O})}(Y)u(q(\iota_{\mathfrak{h}}(Y)))1_{\GL_{n}(\mathcal{O})}(g)
satisfies the equation (
5.16
). Then

\varphi_{H,s}(Y)=\int_{\GL_{n}(F)}1_{\widetilde{\mathfrak{h}}(\mathcal{O})}(Y)u(\iota_{\mathfrak{h}}(Y))1_{\GL_{n}(\mathcal{O})}(g)\chi(g)\lvert g\rvert^{s}\mathrm{d}g=1_{\widetilde{\mathfrak{h}}(\mathcal{O})}(Y)u(q(\iota_{\mathfrak{h}}(Y))).

By Lemma
5.5
and our assumptions,
I_{X}(1_{\widetilde{\gl_{n}}(\mathcal{O})},\xi,s)=I_{X}(\varphi,\xi,s)=I_{X_{H}}(\varphi_{H},\xi,s)=I_{X_{H}}(1_{\widetilde{\mathfrak{h}}(\mathcal{O})},\xi,s)
. Therefore (4) follows from Corollary
5.3
part (4). For (5), to show
I_{X}(\cdot,\xi,s)
is a
\xi\eta\lvert\cdot\rvert^{s}
-stable distribution, let
\varphi\in\mathcal{S}(\widetilde{\gl_{n}}(F))
be a
\xi\eta\lvert\cdot\rvert^{s}
-unstable function, replacing
\varphi
by
\varphi\cdot(u\circ q)
, we can assume
\varphi\in\mathcal{S}(\Omega)
, then direct computation shows
\varphi_{H,s}
is also
\xi\eta\lvert\cdot\rvert^{s}
-unstable, therefore, the result follows from the fact that
I_{X_{H}}
is a
\xi\eta\lvert\cdot\rvert^{s}
-stable distribution. The fact that it supports in the closure of the orbit of
X
follows from Lemma
5.5
.

∎

Remark 5.9
.

When
X
is central or regular semisimple, we then have two definitions of
I_{X}
in Subsection
5.1
and
5.2
respectively, these two definitions coincide because, when
a\in\mathcal{A}_{\mathrm{rs}}(F)
, the descent construction associated with
a
is given by
H=H_{0}=\GL_{n}
and
\iota_{H}=\iota_{\mathfrak{h}}=\mathrm{id}
. And when
a\in\mathcal{A}(F)
is central, the descent construction associated to
a
is given by
H_{0}=\{1\}
and
k=1,F_{1}=F,n_{1}=n
, therefore we also have
H=\GL_{n}
and
\iota_{H}=\iota_{\mathfrak{h}}=\mathrm{id}
.

When
X\in\widetilde{\gl_{n}}_{,+}(F)
or
X\in\widetilde{\gl_{n}}_{,-}(F)
, the distribution can be defined in a more direct way.

Proposition 5.10
.

Let
X\in\widetilde{\gl_{n}}_{,+}(F)
(resp.
X\in\widetilde{\gl_{n}}_{,-}(F)
), then for
\varphi\in\widetilde{\gl_{n}}(F)
the integral

(5.19)

\int_{\GL_{n}(F)}\varphi(X\cdot g)\xi(g)\eta(g)\lvert g\rvert^{s}\mathrm{d}g

is absolutely convergent when
\mathrm{Re}(s)>-1+\frac{1}{n}
(resp.
\mathrm{Re}(s)<1-\frac{1}{n}
), and coincides with
I_{X}(\varphi,\xi,s)
in the convergence domain.

Proof.

We choose a non-negative function
\psi^{\prime}\in C_{c}(\Omega_{H}\times\GL_{n}(F))
such that for any
(Y,g)\in\Omega_{H}\times\GL_{n}(F)
, we have

\lvert\varphi(\iota_{\mathfrak{h}}(Y)\cdot g)\rvert=\int_{H(F)}\psi^{\prime}(Y\cdot h,h^{-1}g)\mathrm{d}h.

For
s\in\mathbb{C}
, let
\psi_{H,s}\in C_{c}(\widetilde{\mathfrak{h}}(F))
be the non-negative function defined by

\psi_{H,s}(Y)=\int_{\GL_{n}(F)}\psi^{\prime}(Y,g)\lvert\det g\rvert^{\mathrm{Re}(s)}\mathrm{d}g.

Then the absolute convergence of the integral (
5.19
) is equivalent to the absolute convergence of

\int_{H(F)}\psi_{H,s}(Y\cdot h)\lvert\det h\rvert^{s}\mathrm{d}h

which follows from and Proposition
2.3
(2) and Proposition
5.1
(although in Proposition
5.1
, we require the functions are Schwartz, but the proof of part (1) only needs the condition that the functions are in
C_{c}(\widetilde{\mathfrak{h}}(F))
). After the knowledge of absolute convergence, Fubini theorem implies that the integral (
5.19
) coincides with
I_{X}(\varphi,\xi,s)
in the domain of convergence.
∎

5.2.1.
A variant

We provide a variant of the construction above, replacing
\widetilde{\gl_{n}}
by
\gl_{n+1}
. Recall that
\mathcal{B}=\gl_{n+1}/\GL_{n}
and we have a canonical identification
\mathcal{B}\cong\mathcal{A}\times F
induced by the map (
2.10
).

For a regular element
X=\begin{pmatrix}A&v\\
u&d\end{pmatrix}\in\gl_{n+1}(F)
, we define

L_{X}(s,\xi):=L_{(A,v,u)}(s,\xi),

where the right-hand side is defined in (
5.14
). For
\varphi\in\mathcal{S}(\gl_{n+1}(F))
, and
s\in\mathbb{C}
which is not a pole of
L_{X}(s,\xi)
, we put

I_{X}(\varphi,\xi,s):=I_{(A,v,u)}(\varphi_{d},\xi,s),

where
\varphi_{d}\in\mathcal{S}(\widetilde{\gl_{n}}(F))
is defined by

\varphi_{d}(A^{\prime},v^{\prime},u^{\prime})=\varphi\begin{pmatrix}A^{\prime}&v^{\prime}\\
u^{\prime}&d\end{pmatrix}.

Remark 5.11
.

By definition, it is easy to see that
I_{X}
admits similar properties as those listed in Proposition
5.8
.

5.3.
Orbital integrals on the group

Let
f\in\mathcal{S}(\mathrm{G}^{\prime}(F))
and
s\in\mathbb{C}
, we define
f^{\mathrm{S}}_{s}\in\mathcal{S}(\mathrm{S}(F))
by

(5.20)

f^{\mathrm{S}}_{s}(x)=\begin{cases}\int_{\mathrm{H}_{1}(F)}\int_{\mathrm{H}_{2,n+1}(F)}f(h_{1}^{-1},h_{1}^{-1}\nu^{-1}(x)h_{2,n+1})\xi(h_{1})\lvert\det h_{1}\rvert^{s}\mu(\nu^{-1}(x)h_{2,n+1}),&n\text{ odd},\\
\int_{\mathrm{H}_{1}(F)}\int_{\mathrm{H}_{2,n+1}(F)}f(h_{1}^{-1},h_{1}^{-1}\nu^{-1}(x)h_{2,n+1})\xi(h_{1})\lvert\det h_{1}\rvert^{s},&n\text{ even}.\end{cases}

Fix
\gamma=(\gamma_{n},\gamma_{n+1})\in\mathrm{G}_{\mathrm{reg}}^{\prime}(F)
, let
x=\alpha(\gamma)\in\mathrm{S}(F)
, and let
b=q(x)\in\mathrm{B}(F)
. Choose
\sigma\in E^{\times}
with
\sigma\sigma^{\mathtt{c}}=1
such that
x\in\mathrm{S}^{\sigma}(F)
, so the Cayley transform
\mathfrak{c}_{\sigma}
is defined. We use
\mathfrak{c}_{\sigma}
to transport
f_{s}^{\mathrm{S}}
to a Schwartz function
f_{s}^{\gl}
on
\gl_{n+1}(F)
: pick
u\in C_{c}^{\infty}(\mathrm{B}^{\sigma}(F))
such that
u(b)=1
, and define
f^{\gl}_{s}\in\mathcal{S}(\gl_{n+1}^{\tau}(F))
by

(5.21)

f^{\gl}_{s}(X)=(u\cdot f^{\mathrm{S}}_{s})(\mathfrak{c}_{\sigma}(X)),

and extends
f^{\gl}_{s}
to an element of
\mathcal{S}(\gl_{n+1}(F))
using extension by zero, we still denote it by
f^{\gl}_{s}
.

We put an
L
-factor by

L_{\gamma}(s,\xi):=L_{\mathfrak{c}_{\sigma}^{-1}(x)}(s,\xi),

and for
s\in\mathbb{C}
which is not a pole of
L_{\gamma}(s,\xi)
, we put

I^{\sigma}_{\gamma}(f,\xi,s):=\begin{cases}I_{\mathfrak{c}_{\sigma}(x)}(f^{\gl}_{s},\xi,s)\mu(\gamma_{n}\gamma_{n+1}^{-1})&n\text{ odd}\\
I_{\mathfrak{c}_{\sigma}(x)}(f^{\gl}_{s},\xi,s)&n\text{ even}\end{cases}.

Note that, by Proposition
5.8
(5) (see also Remark
5.11
), the definition of
I^{\sigma}_{\gamma}
does not depend on the choice of
u\in C_{c}^{\infty}(\mathrm{B}^{\sigma}(F))
.

We say
f\in\mathcal{S}(\mathrm{G}^{\prime}(F))
is
(\xi\lvert\cdot\rvert^{s},\eta)
-unstable, if for any
\gamma\in\mathrm{G}^{\prime}_{\mathrm{rs}}(F)
, the orbital integral

\int_{\mathrm{H}_{1}(F)}\int_{\mathrm{H}_{2}(F)}f(h_{1}^{-1}\gamma h_{2})\lvert\det h_{1}\rvert^{s}\xi(h_{1})\eta(h_{2})\mathrm{d}h_{1}\mathrm{d}h_{2}

vanishes. A distribution
I:\mathcal{S}(\mathrm{G}^{\prime}(F))\mathbb{C}
is called
(\xi\lvert\cdot\rvert^{s},\eta)
-stable if
I(f)=0
for any
f
that is
(\xi\lvert\cdot\rvert^{s},\eta)
-unstable.

We now list the properties of
I^{\sigma}_{\gamma}
.

Proposition 5.12
.

We have the following assertions

(1)

The function
sL_{\gamma}(f,\xi,s)
is meromorphic, and the pole is contained in the pole of
L_{\gamma}(s,\xi)
.

(2)

If
s
is not a pole of
L_{\gamma}(s,\xi)
, then
I^{\sigma}(\cdot,\xi,s)
is continuous.

(3)

As a function of
s
, for any
f\in\mathcal{S}(\mathrm{G}^{\prime}(F))

I^{\sigma,\natural}(f,\xi,s):=\frac{I^{\sigma}_{\gamma}(f,\xi,s)}{L_{\gamma}(s,\xi)}

is entire.

(4)

If
F
is non-Archimedean with ring of integer
\mathcal{O}
, suppose that we can choose
\sigma
such that

•

\xi,\eta
and
\mu
are unramified,

•

2\tau\sigma\in\mathcal{O}^{\times}
,

•

\gamma\in\mathrm{G}^{\prime}(\mathcal{O})
,

•

a_{H}\in\mathcal{A}_{H}^{\prime}(\mathcal{O})
,

•

X=\mathfrak{c}_{\sigma}^{-1}(x)
satisfies the condition of (4) in Proposition
5.8
.

Then
I^{\sigma}_{\gamma}(1_{\mathrm{G}^{\prime}(\mathcal{O})},\xi,s)=L_{\gamma}(s,\xi)

(5)

For any
s\in\mathbb{C}
which is not a pole of
L_{\gamma}(s,\xi)
,
I^{\sigma}_{\gamma}(\cdot,\xi,s)
is a
\xi\eta\lvert\cdot\rvert^{s}
-stable distribution and supported on the closure of the
\mathrm{H}_{1}(F)\times\mathrm{H}_{2}(F)
orbit of
\gamma
.

Proof.

Part (1) follows from Proposition
5.8
(1) and the fact that
sf^{\gl}_{s}
is holomorphic and valued in
\mathcal{S}(\gl_{n+1}(F))
. (2) and (3) also follow from their corresponding part of Proposition
5.8
. Put
f=1_{\mathrm{G}^{\prime}(\mathcal{O})}
Under the condition of (4), one directly checks that
f^{\mathrm{S}}_{s}=1_{\mathrm{S}(\mathcal{O})}
for any
s\in\mathbb{C}
and
I^{\sigma}_{\gamma}(f,\xi,s)=I_{\mathfrak{c}_{\sigma}(x)}(1_{\widetilde{\gl_{n}}(\mathcal{O})},\xi,s)
. Therefore, part (4) follows from Proposition
5.8
(4), and part (5) also follows from Proposition
5.8
(5).
∎

Remark 5.13
.

It is expected that
I_{\gamma}^{\sigma}
depends only on
\gamma
(but does not depend on
\sigma
), for example, the following lemma shows that this holds when
\gamma\in\mathrm{G}^{\prime}_{+}(F)
or
\mathrm{G}^{\prime}_{-}(F)
, but the author does not have a proof now.

However, we can show that
L_{\gamma}
is independent of the choice of
\sigma
, for the following reason: for any
s_{0}\in\mathbb{C}
the order of the pole of
L_{\gamma}(s,\xi)
at
s=s_{0}
is the maximum of that of
I^{\sigma}_{\gamma}(f,\xi,s)
as
f
runs through elements in
\mathrm{S}(\mathrm{G}^{\prime}(F))
. This follows from Corollary
5.3
(3), and this quantity is independent of the choice of
\sigma
.

When
\gamma\in\mathrm{G}^{\prime}_{+}(F)
or
\gamma\in\mathrm{G}^{\prime}_{-}(F)
,
I_{\gamma}
can be described more explicitly. The following lemma can be proved using the same strategy as that in the proof of Proposition
5.10
.

Lemma 5.14
.

We have the following assertions:

•

If
\gamma
is regular semisimple, then

I^{\sigma}_{\gamma}(f,\xi,s)=\int_{\mathrm{H}_{1}(F)}\int_{\mathrm{H}_{2}(F)}f(h_{1}^{-1}\gamma h_{2})\xi(h_{1})\lvert\det h_{1}\rvert^{s}\eta(h_{2})\mathrm{d}h_{1}\mathrm{d}h_{2},

where the integral converges absolutely for any
s\in\mathbb{C}
.

•

If
\gamma\in\mathrm{G}^{\prime}_{+}(F)
(resp.
\mathrm{G}^{\prime}_{-}(F)
), then the integral

\int_{\mathrm{H}_{1}(F)}\int_{\mathrm{H}_{2}(F)}f(h_{1}^{-1}\gamma h_{2})\xi(h_{1})\lvert\det h_{1}\rvert^{s}\eta(h_{2})\mathrm{d}h_{1}\mathrm{d}h_{2},

is absolutely convergent for
s\in\mathcal{H}_{<-1+\frac{1}{n}}
(resp.
s\in\mathcal{H}_{>1-\frac{1}{n}}
) and equals
I^{\sigma}_{\gamma}(f,\xi,s)
there.

In particular, in these cases, the definition of
I^{\sigma}_{\gamma}(f,\xi,s)
does not depend on the choice of
\sigma

6.
Global Theory II: Regular supported functions

In this section, we set back to our notation in Section
4
. So
F
will be a number field. The main result in this section is Theorem
6.1
, which computes the geometric side of Jacquet-Rallis RTF when the test function is regular supported at a place.

6.1.
Global orbital integral

Fix an additive character
\psi:F\backslash\mathbb{A}_{F}\mathbb{C}^{\times}
, for each place
v
of
F
, the local component
\psi_{v}:F_{v}\mathbb{C}^{\times}
of
\psi
determines measures on various algebraic groups over
F_{v}
as discussed in
5.0.1
. If
F^{\prime}/F
is a finite extension, we put
\psi\circ\mathrm{Tr}_{F^{\prime}/F}
as the additive character on
\mathbb{A}_{F^{\prime}}
and it can be used to define measures on various local groups over
F^{\prime}_{w}
where
w
is a place of
F^{\prime}
. For any reductive group
G
over
F
, we write
\Delta_{G}^{*}
for the leading Laurant coefficient of the Artin-Tate
L
-function
L_{G}(s)
associated to
G
(See
[
3
]
*2.3.2). For
m>0
, the Tamagawa measure on
\GL_{m}(\mathbb{A}_{F^{\prime}})
is given by

\mathrm{d}g=\Delta_{\GL_{m,F^{\prime}}}^{*,-1}\prod_{w}\mathrm{d}g_{w}.

In this case,
\Delta_{\GL_{m,F^{\prime}}}=\zeta^{*}_{F^{\prime}}(1)\zeta_{F^{\prime}}(2)\cdots\zeta_{F^{\prime}}(m)
where
\zeta^{*}_{F^{\prime}}(1)
denotes the residue of
\zeta_{F}(s)
(the completed Dedekind zeta function) at
s=1
, and the product runs through the places of
F^{\prime}
.

We consider the three cases for a group
G
acting on
X
as in (
2.12
), excluding the case
(G,X)=(\GL_{n},\mathrm{S})
. So the notation
G
and
X
will mean any pair
(G,X)
in these three cases. Let
\xi:\mathbb{A}_{E}^{\times}\mathbb{C}^{\times}
be a strictly unitary character. For a place
v
of
F
, an element
\gamma\in X_{\mathrm{reg}}(F)
and
f\in\mathcal{S}(X(F_{v}))
, in Section
5
, we have defined the local factor
L_{\gamma}(s,\xi_{v})
and regularized orbital integral
I_{\gamma}(f,\xi_{v},s)
(If
G=\mathrm{H}_{1}\times\mathrm{H}_{2}
, it depends on a choice of a norm 1 element
\sigma
in
E_{v}
, which we assume it comes from a global element, and it should be denoted by
I_{\gamma}^{\sigma}
, similar for the notations later). We will denote them by
L_{\gamma,v}
and
I_{\gamma,v}
here. Using the compatibility of descent and base change (see the last sentences of Subsection
2.5
), the local factor
L_{\gamma,v}
is the local component of a global
L
-factor
L_{\gamma}(s,\xi)

(which is defined by the global analogue of (
5.12
)). For
f\in\mathcal{S}(X(F_{v}))
, we put

I_{\gamma,v}^{\natural}(f,\xi_{v},s):=\frac{I_{\gamma,v}(f,\xi_{v},s)}{L_{\gamma,v}(s,\xi_{v})}.

For
f\in\mathcal{S}(X(\mathbb{A}))
, if
f=\otimes f_{v}
is factorizable, by results in Section
5
,
I_{\gamma,v}^{\natural}(f_{v},\xi_{v},s)=1
for almost all places
v
, hence we can define

I^{\natural}_{\gamma}(f,\xi,s):=\prod_{v}I^{\natural}_{\gamma,v}(f_{v},\xi_{v},s).

The definition of
I_{\gamma}^{\natural}
extends by continuity to any
f\in\mathcal{S}(X(\mathbb{A}))
. We then define

(6.1)

I_{\gamma}(f,\xi,s):=\Delta_{G}^{*,-1}L_{\gamma}(s,\xi)I_{\gamma}^{\natural}(f,\xi,s).

By definition,
I_{\gamma}(f,\xi,s)
depends only on the value of
f
on the
G(\mathbb{A})
-orbit of
\gamma
and for any
g\in G(F),I_{\gamma\cdot g}(f)=I_{\gamma}(f)
, and when
X=\mathrm{G}^{\prime},G=\mathrm{H}_{1}\times\mathrm{H}_{2}
, it depends on the choice of norm one element
\sigma
.

The construction directly generalizes to the product of cases we consider. Fix
a\in\mathcal{A}(F)
we, therefore, have
\widetilde{\mathfrak{h}}
as discussed in Subsection
2.5
. Then for any
\varphi\in\mathcal{S}(\widetilde{\mathfrak{h}}(\mathbb{A}))
and regular element
Y\in\widetilde{\mathfrak{h}}(F)
, we can define
I_{Y}(\varphi,\xi,s)
in the same way.

6.2.
Main result

We now state our main result.

Theorem 6.1
.

Let
f=f_{v}f^{v}\in\mathcal{S}(\mathrm{G}^{\prime}(\mathbb{A}))
with
f_{v}\in\mathcal{S}(\mathrm{G}^{\prime}(F_{v})),f^{v}\in\mathcal{S}(\mathrm{G}^{\prime}(\mathbb{A}^{v}))
and
\mathrm{supp}(f_{v})\subset\mathrm{G}^{\prime}_{\mathrm{reg}}(F_{v})
, then for
b\in\mathrm{B}(F)
, we have

(6.2)

I_{b}(f,\xi,s)=\sum_{\gamma}I^{\sigma}_{\gamma}(f,\xi,s).

Where
I_{b}
is the distribution from relative trace formula in Theorem
3.1
(2), and
I^{\sigma}_{\gamma}
is defined in
(
6.1
)
, the sum runs through all the representative of
\mathrm{H}_{1}(F)\times\mathrm{H}_{2}(F)
orbits in
\mathrm{G}^{\prime}_{b}(F)\cap\mathrm{G}^{\prime}_{\mathrm{reg}}(F)
.

We will derive Theorem
6.1
from a Lie algebra version of it.

Proposition 6.2
.

Let
\mathfrak{g}
denote either
\gl_{n+1}
or
\widetilde{\gl_{n}}
. Let
\varphi=\varphi_{v}\varphi^{v}\in\mathcal{S}(\mathfrak{g}(\mathbb{A}))
with
\varphi_{v}\in\mathcal{S}(\mathfrak{g}(F_{v})),\varphi^{v}\in\mathcal{S}(\mathfrak{g}(\mathbb{A}^{v}))
and
\mathrm{supp}(\varphi_{v})\subset\mathfrak{g}_{\mathrm{reg}}(F_{v})
, then for
a\in(\mathfrak{g}/\GL_{n})(F)
, we have

(6.3)

I_{a}(\varphi,\xi,s)=\sum_{X}I_{X}(\varphi,\xi,s).

Where
I_{a}
is the distribution from relative trace formula (see Theorem
3.2
and Remark
3.3
) and
I_{X}
is defined in
(
6.1
)
, the sum runs through all the representative of
\GL_{n}(F)
orbits in
\mathfrak{g}_{a}(F)\cap\mathfrak{g}_{\mathrm{reg}}(F)
.

Fix
a\in\mathcal{A}(F)
. By the descent construction in Subsection
2.5
, there is
\widetilde{\mathfrak{h}}
which is a product of
\widetilde{\gl_{n_{i},F_{i}}}
of smaller size such that there is
a_{H}\in\mathcal{A}_{H}^{\prime}
maps to
a
under the étale map
\iota_{\mathcal{A}}:\mathcal{A}_{H}^{\prime}\mathcal{A}
. We first show a version of Proposition
6.2
for
a_{H}
.

Lemma 6.3
.

Let
\varphi=\varphi_{v}\varphi^{v}\in\mathcal{S}(\widetilde{\mathfrak{h}}(\mathbb{A}))
with
\varphi_{v}\in\mathcal{S}(\widetilde{\mathfrak{h}}(F_{v})),\varphi^{v}\in\mathcal{S}(\widetilde{\mathfrak{h}}(\mathbb{A}^{v}))
and
\mathrm{supp}(\varphi_{v})\subset\widetilde{\mathfrak{h}}_{\mathrm{reg}}(F_{v})
, then we have

(6.4)

I_{a_{H}}(\varphi,\xi,s)=\sum_{X}I_{X}(\varphi,\xi,s).

Where
I_{a_{H}}
is the distribution from relative trace formula (see Remark
3.3
), the sum runs through all the representative of
H(F)
orbits in
\widetilde{\mathfrak{h}}_{a_{H}}(F)\cap\widetilde{\mathfrak{h}}_{\mathrm{reg}}(F)
.

Proof.

We can assume
\varphi
is a pure tensor
\otimes\varphi_{v}
and each
\varphi_{v}
is of the form
\varphi_{v}=\otimes_{i=0}^{k}\varphi_{v,i}
by continuity, where
\varphi_{v,i}\in\mathcal{S}(\gl_{n_{i}}(F_{i}))
. Put
\varphi_{i}=\otimes\varphi_{v,i}\in\mathcal{S}(\widetilde{\gl_{n_{i}}}(\mathbb{A}_{F_{i}}))
, so that
\varphi=\otimes\varphi_{i}
. Therefore,

I_{a_{H}}(\varphi,\xi,s)=\prod_{i=0}^{k}I_{a_{i}}(\varphi_{i},\xi_{i},s),

and

\sum_{X}I_{X}(\varphi,\xi,s)=\prod_{i=0}^{k}\left(\sum_{X_{i}}I_{X_{i}}(\varphi_{i},\xi_{i},s)\right),

where
\xi_{i}:=\xi\circ\mathrm{Nm}_{(E\otimes_{F}F_{i})/E}
, and in the sum on the right-hand side, for each
0\leq i\leq k
,
X_{i}
runs through the regular orbits in
\widetilde{\gl_{n_{i},F_{i}}}_{,a_{i}}(F_{i})
. Therefore up to possibly replacing
F
by
F_{i}
, we are reduced to prove Proposition
6.2
, for
\mathfrak{g}=\widetilde{\gl_{n}}
and
a
is either central or regular semisimple.

If
a
is regular semisimple, then both sides of (
6.4
) equals to

\int_{\GL_{n}(\mathbb{A})}\varphi(X\cdot g)\xi(g)\eta(g)\lvert\det g\rvert^{s}\mathrm{d}g,

therefore (
6.4
) holds. We now assume
a
is central. By Lemma
2.2
, all regular orbits in the fiber of
a
are
O^{+}(a)
and
O^{-}(a)
. Note that we have the following open cover of
\widetilde{\gl_{n}}_{,\mathrm{reg}}(F_{v})
:

\widetilde{\gl_{n}}_{,\mathrm{reg}}(F_{v})=\widetilde{\gl_{n}}_{,+}(F_{v})\cup\widetilde{\gl_{n}}_{,-}(F_{v})\cup(\widetilde{\gl_{n}}_{,\mathrm{reg}}\setminus\widetilde{\gl_{n}}_{,a})(F_{v}).

We choose functions
\alpha_{0},\alpha_{+},\alpha_{-}\in C^{\infty}(\widetilde{\gl_{n}}_{,\mathrm{reg}}(F_{v}))
(a partition of unity), such that

•

\alpha_{+}+\alpha_{-}+\alpha_{0}=1
,

•

\mathrm{supp}(\alpha_{0})\subset(\widetilde{\gl_{n}}_{,\mathrm{reg}}\setminus\widetilde{\gl_{n}}_{,a})(F_{v})
,
\mathrm{supp}(\alpha_{+})\subset\widetilde{\gl}_{n,+}(F_{v}),\mathrm{supp}(\alpha_{-})\subset\widetilde{\gl}_{n,-}(F_{v})
,

•

for any
f\in\mathcal{S}(\widetilde{\gl_{n}}_{,\mathrm{reg}}(F_{v}))
and any
i\in\{0,+,-\}
,
\alpha_{i}f\in\mathcal{S}(\widetilde{\gl_{n}}_{,\mathrm{reg}}(F_{v}))
.

The existence of these functions is easy for non-Archimedean
v
and for
v
Archimedean, see
[
1
]
*Theorem 4.4.1.

For
i\in\{0,+,-\}
, let
\varphi_{i}\in\mathcal{S}(\widetilde{\gl_{n}}(\mathbb{A}))
be the function
(\alpha_{i}\varphi_{v})\cdot\varphi^{v}
. Then since the distribution
I_{a}(\cdot,\xi,s)
is supported on
\widetilde{\gl_{n}}_{,a}(\mathbb{A})
, we see that

(6.5)

I_{a}(\varphi_{0},\xi,s)=0.

Since
\varphi_{+}
satisfies the assumption of Theorem
4.3
, by part (3) of this theorem, we see that for
s\in\mathcal{H}_{<-1}

(6.6)

I_{a}(\varphi_{+},\xi,s)=\int_{\GL_{n}(\mathbb{A})}\varphi_{+}(X\cdot g)\xi(g)\eta(g)\lvert\det g\rvert^{s}\mathrm{d}g=\int_{\GL_{n}(\mathbb{A})}\varphi_{+}(X\cdot g)\xi(g)\eta(g)\lvert\det g\rvert^{s}\mathrm{d}g,

where the integral in (
6.6
) is absolutely convergent. That is, the infinite product

\Delta_{\GL_{n}}^{*,-1}\prod_{v}I_{X_{+},v}(\varphi_{v},\xi_{v},s)

is convergent when
s\in\mathcal{H}_{<-1}
, and is therefore equals to
I_{X_{+}}(\varphi,\xi,s)
. Since both
I_{a}(\varphi_{+},\xi,s)
and
I_{X_{+}}(\varphi,\xi,s)
are meromorphic in
s
, we see that whenever they are holomorphic, we have
I_{a}(\varphi_{+},\xi,s)=I_{X_{+}}(\varphi,\xi,s)
. By a similar argument,
I_{a}(\varphi_{-},\xi,s)=I_{X_{-}}(\varphi_{-},\xi,s)
. Together with (
6.5
), we see that

I_{a}(\varphi,\xi,s)=I_{a}(\varphi_{+},\xi,s)+I_{a}(\varphi_{-},\xi,s)+I_{a}(\varphi_{0},\xi,s)=I_{X_{+}}(\varphi,\xi,s)+I_{X_{-}}(\varphi,\xi,s),

which is exactly the equality we want for the central element
a
.
∎

Now we give the proof of Proposition
6.2
.

Proof of Proposition
6.2
.

We first show the case when
\mathfrak{g}=\widetilde{\gl_{n}}
. We will use the notation introduced in Subsection
2.5
.

Let
\mathtt{S}
be a sufficiently large finite set of places of
F
, containing all archimedean places. For any
v\in\mathtt{S}
, pick an open neighbourhood
\omega_{v}
of
a
in
\mathcal{A}(F_{v})
and a neighbourhood
\omega_{H,v}
of
a_{H}
in
\mathcal{A}_{H}(F_{v})
such that
\omega_{v}
and
\omega_{H,v}
are semi-algebraic if
v
is Archimedean and
\iota_{\mathcal{A}}:\mathcal{A}_{H}^{\prime}\mathcal{A}
induces an isomorphism
\omega_{H,v}\omega_{H}
. Let
\omega_{\mathtt{S}}:=\prod_{v\in\mathtt{S}}\omega_{v}
and
\omega_{H,\mathtt{S}}:=\prod_{v\in\mathtt{S}}\omega_{H,v}
. We choose
u\in C_{c}^{\infty}(\omega_{\mathtt{S}})
such that
u(a)=1
. Denote by
\Omega_{\mathtt{S}}
(resp.
\Omega_{H,\mathtt{S}}
) the preimage of
\omega
(resp.
\omega_{H,v}
) under the quotient map
q
(resp.
q_{H}
).

Since both sides of (
6.4
) only depend on the value of
\varphi
on
\widetilde{\gl_{n}}_{,a}(\mathbb{A})
. After replacing
\varphi
by
\varphi\cdot(u\circ q)
and enlarging
\mathtt{S}
, we can assume
\varphi=\varphi_{\mathtt{S}}\otimes 1_{\widetilde{\gl_{n}}(\mathcal{O}_{F}^{\mathtt{S}})}
, where
\varphi_{\mathtt{S}}\in\mathcal{S}(\widetilde{\gl_{n}}(F_{\mathtt{S}}))
. We can choose
\varphi^{\prime}\in\mathcal{S}(\Omega_{H,\mathtt{S}}\times\GL_{n}(F_{\mathtt{S}}))
(see Subsection
5.2
) such that for any
(X,g)\in\Omega_{H,\mathtt{S}}\times\GL_{n}(F_{\mathtt{S}})
, we have

\varphi(\iota_{\mathfrak{h}}(X)\cdot g)=\int_{H_{\mathtt{S}}(F)}\varphi^{\prime}(Xh,\iota_{H}(h)^{-1}g)\mathrm{d}h.

For any
X_{H}\in\Omega_{H,\mathtt{S}}
and
s\in\mathbb{C}
, we put

\varphi_{H,\mathtt{S},s}(X_{H})=\int_{\GL_{n}(F_{\mathtt{S}})}\varphi^{\prime}(X_{H},g)\xi_{\mathtt{S}}(g)\eta_{\mathtt{S}}(g)\lvert\det g\rvert^{s}\mathrm{d}g.

Define
\varphi_{H,s}\in\mathcal{S}(\widetilde{\mathfrak{h}}(\mathbb{A}))
by
\varphi_{H,s}=\varphi_{H,\mathtt{S},s}\otimes 1_{\widetilde{\mathfrak{h}}(\mathcal{O}_{F}^{\mathtt{S}})}
. Up to enlarging
\mathtt{S}
, by
[
11
]
*Théorème 6.4.6.1, we have

I_{a}(\varphi,\xi,s)=\Delta_{\GL_{n}}^{*,-1}\Delta_{H}^{*}I_{a_{H}}(\varphi_{H,s},\xi,s).

(In
loc. cit
, this result is only proved for the case when
\xi
is trivial and
s=0
, but the same proof works for general case). By our construction of regularized orbital integral
I_{X}
in Subsection
5.2
, for any regular element
X_{H}\in\widetilde{\mathfrak{h}}(F)
, we have

I_{X_{H}}(\varphi_{H,s},\xi,s)=\Delta_{\GL_{n}}^{*,-1}\Delta_{H}^{*}I_{X}(\varphi,\xi,s).

Therefore, Proposition
6.2
follows from Lemma
6.3
and Proposition
2.3
.
∎

Now we can prove Theorem
6.1
.

Proof of Theorem
6.1
.

Choose
\sigma\in E
such that
\mathrm{Nm}_{E/F}(\sigma)=1
and
b\in\mathrm{B}^{\sigma}(F)
. So Cayley transform
\mathfrak{c}_{\sigma}(b)\in\mathcal{B}(F)
is defined.

Choose a sufficiently large set of finite places
\mathtt{S}
of
F
. We assume that
f
is of the form
f_{\mathtt{S}}\otimes 1_{\mathrm{G}^{\prime}(\mathcal{O}_{F}^{\mathtt{S}})}
, where
f_{\mathtt{S}}\in\mathcal{S}(F_{\mathtt{S}})
, and we define
f^{\gl}_{\mathtt{S},s}
by a similar formula as in (
5.21
), replacing
F
there by
F_{\mathtt{S}}
, and we put
f^{\gl}_{s}:=f^{\gl}_{\mathtt{S},s}\otimes 1_{\widetilde{\gl_{n}}(\mathcal{O}^{\mathtt{S}})}\in\mathcal{S}(\widetilde{\gl_{n}}(\mathbb{A}))
. When
\mathtt{S}
is sufficiently large, by
[
11
]
*Lemme 14.4.4.2, we have

I_{b}(f,\xi,s)=\Delta_{\mathrm{H}_{1}}^{*,-1}\Delta_{\mathrm{H}_{2}}^{*,-1}\Delta_{\GL_{n}}^{*}I_{\mathfrak{c}_{\sigma}(b)}(f^{\gl}_{s}).

Let
\gamma\in\mathrm{G}^{\prime}(F)
be regular element, by our construction of
I^{\sigma}_{\gamma}(f,\xi,s)
, we have

I^{\sigma}_{\gamma}(f,\xi,s)=\Delta_{\mathrm{H}_{1}}^{*,-1}\Delta_{\mathrm{H}_{2}}^{*,-1}\Delta_{\GL_{n}}^{*}I_{\mathfrak{c}_{\sigma}(\alpha(\gamma))}(f^{\gl}_{s},\xi,s).

Therefore, the result follows from Proposition
6.2
.
∎

7.
Local Theory II: Local singular transfer

We retain the notation in Section
5
so that
F
is a local field of characteristic 0 and
E/F
is a quadratic étale algebra. Recall that in Section
5
, we have defined, for any
f\in\mathcal{S}(\mathrm{G}^{\prime}(F))
, and any
\gamma\in\mathrm{G}^{\prime}_{\mathrm{reg}}(F)
and character
\xi:E^{\times}\mathbb{C}^{\times}
, a regularized orbital integral
I_{\gamma}^{\sigma}(f,\xi,s)
and a normalized version
I_{\gamma}^{\sigma,\natural}(f,\xi,s)
which depends on a choice of norm one element
\sigma
in
E
.

In this section, we will study a transfer relation between
I^{\sigma}_{\gamma}(f)
and semisimple orbital integrals on the unitary groups. We will first introduce orbital integral unitary groups and matching in Subsection
7.1
and
7.2
, we then review the descent on
\widetilde{\mathfrak{u}}^{V}
in
7.3
, which is a unitary analogue of what we have done in Subsection
2.5
. Then we study the Lie algebra version of local singular transfer in Subsection
7.4
and the group version in Subsection
7.5
.

7.0.1.
Notations and measure

Let
\mathcal{H}
denote the isometric classes of
n
-dimensional
E/F
Hermitian space. Let
(V,h)\in\mathcal{H}
, denote the
discriminant
of
V
by
\mathrm{disc}(V)
, which is the determinant of the gram matrix of
V
in any basis, regarded as an element of
F^{\times}/\mathrm{Nm}_{E/F}(F^{\times})
. Let
\U(V)
be the unitary group associated to
V
, and let
V+Ee_{0}
be the Hermitian space formed by orthogonal direct sum of
V
and 1-dimensional
E/F
-Hermitian space
(Ee_{0},h_{0})
spanned by
e_{0}
with
h_{0}(e_{0},e_{0})=1
. Define

\mathrm{G}^{V}=\U(V)\times\U(V\oplus Ee_{0}),\quad\mathrm{H}^{V}=(h,\mathrm{diag}(h,1)),h\in\U(V).

So
\mathrm{H}^{V}
is a subgroup of
\mathrm{G}^{V}
isomorphic to
\U(V)
. The group
\mathrm{H}^{V}\times\mathrm{H}^{V}
acts on the right on
\mathrm{G}^{V}
by

g\cdot(h_{1},h_{2}):=h_{1}^{-1}gh_{2}.

For any
\gamma\in\mathrm{G}^{V}(F)
, let
(\mathrm{H}^{V}\times\mathrm{H}^{V})_{\gamma}
denote the stabilizer of
\gamma
under this action. We call
\gamma\in\mathrm{G}^{V}(F)
semisimple (resp. regular semisimple), if its
\mathrm{H}^{V}\times\mathrm{H}^{V}
orbit is Zariski closed (resp. is semisimple and has trivial stabilizer). We call any
\U(V)(F)
orbits of a semisimple element a
semisimple orbit
. We write
\mathrm{G}^{V}_{\mathrm{rs}}
for the open subset of
\mathrm{G}^{V}
consisting of regular semisimple elements.

Let
\mathfrak{u}^{V}
be the
F
-subspace of
\mathrm{End}_{E}(V)
consisting of self-adjoint operators, so it is
\tau
times the Lie algebra of
\U(V)
, where
\tau
is any purely imaginary element (i.e.
\mathrm{Tr}_{E/F}\tau=0
) in
E
. We put
\widetilde{\mathfrak{u}}^{V}:=\mathfrak{u}^{V}\times\mathrm{Res}_{E/F}V
, which is an
F
-vector space with a right
\U(V)
action by

(A,v)\cdot g=(g^{-1}Ag,g^{-1}v).

When we want to emphasize the rule of the Hermitian form
h
on
V
, we will also write
\widetilde{\mathfrak{u}}^{h}:=\widetilde{\mathfrak{u}}^{V}
.

For
X\in\widetilde{\mathfrak{u}}^{V}(F)
, we denote its stabilizer under this action by
\U(V)_{X}
. An element
X\in\widetilde{\mathfrak{u}}^{V}(F)
is called semisimple (resp. regular semisimple) if its orbit under the
\U(V)
action is Zariski closed (resp. is semisimple and has stabilizer). Let
\widetilde{\mathfrak{u}}^{V}_{\mathrm{rs}}
denote the open subset of
\widetilde{\mathfrak{u}}^{V}
consisting of regular semisimple elements.

As in
5.0.1
, we fix an additive character
\psi
on
F
. For any
m
-dimensional Hermitian space
V^{\prime}
over
F
, choose a finite extension
F^{\prime}/F
such that
\U(V^{\prime})
is split over
F
. (e.g.
F^{\prime}=F
if
E
is split, and
F^{\prime}=E
if
E
is a field). We have an embedding
\U(V^{\prime})\hookrightarrow\GL(V^{\prime})
. We put the measure on
\U(V^{\prime})(F)
defined by the
F^{\prime}
-valued differential form
\prod_{i=1}^{m}L(i,\eta^{i})\det(g_{ij})^{-m}\bigwedge dg_{ij}
on
\GL(V^{\prime})(F^{\prime})
pulled back to
\U(V^{\prime})(F)
. We put the product measure on
\mathrm{G}^{V}(F)
and put the Haar measure on
\mathrm{H}^{V}(F)
via the natural identification
\mathrm{H}(V)\cong\U(V)
. Note that when
E=F\times F
, the measure on
\U(V)\cong\GL_{n}
coincides with the measure we gave in
5.0.1
.

Let
F^{\prime}/F
be any finite field extension, we put an additive character
\psi^{\prime}
on
F^{\prime}
by
\psi\circ\mathrm{Tr}_{F^{\prime}/F}
. So that we can also put a measure on
\U(V^{\prime})(F^{\prime})
for any
(E\otimes_{F}F^{\prime})/F^{\prime}
Hermitian space
V^{\prime}
. For
V\in\mathcal{H}
, let
\gamma\in\mathrm{G}^{V}(F)
or
X\in\widetilde{\mathfrak{u}}^{V}(F)
be a semisimple element, we will see in Subsection
7.3
that the stabilizer of
\gamma
or
X
is a reductive group which is isomorphic to finitely many product of
\mathrm{Res}_{F^{\prime}/F}\U(V^{\prime})
, we choose the measure on
(\mathrm{H}^{V}\times\mathrm{H}^{V})_{\gamma}(F)
or
\U(V)_{X}(F)
to be the product of the measures we have fixed.

We put a non-degenerate bilinear form on
\widetilde{\mathfrak{u}}^{V}(F)
by
\langle(A,v),(B,w)\rangle=\mathrm{Tr}(AB)+\mathrm{Tr}_{E/F}h(v,w)
. For
\varphi^{V}\in\mathcal{S}(\widetilde{\mathfrak{u}}^{V}(F))
, we then define its Fourier transform
\mathcal{F}\varphi^{V}\in\mathcal{S}(\widetilde{\mathfrak{u}}^{V}(F))
to be

\mathcal{F}\varphi(Y)=\int_{\widetilde{\mathfrak{u}}(F)}\varphi(X)\psi(\langle X,Y\rangle)\mathrm{d}Y,

where the measure on
\widetilde{\mathfrak{u}}^{V}(F)
is chosen to be self-dual.

7.1.
Orbital integral on the unitary groups

For a semisimple element
\gamma\in\mathrm{G}^{V}(F)
and
f\in\mathcal{S}(\mathrm{G}^{V}(F))
, we define its orbital integral by

J^{V}_{\gamma}(f)=J_{\gamma}(f):=\int_{(\mathrm{H}^{V}\times\mathrm{H}^{V})_{\gamma}(F)\backslash(\mathrm{H}^{V}\times\mathrm{H}^{V})(F)}f(\gamma\cdot h)\mathrm{d}h.

The semisimplicity of
\gamma
guarantees
h\gamma\cdot h
is a Schwartz function on the orbit of
\gamma
, so the integral is absolutely convergent.
J^{V}_{\gamma}
only depends on the semisimple orbit
\mathfrak{o}
of
\gamma
. Let
\mathfrak{o}
be a semisimple orbit, we write

J_{\mathfrak{o}}(f)=J_{\gamma}(f),

where
\gamma
is any element in
\mathfrak{o}
. When
\gamma
is regular semisimple, the orbital integral is then given by

J_{\gamma}(f)=\int_{\mathrm{H}^{V}(F)\times\mathrm{H}^{V}(F)}f(\gamma\cdot h)\mathrm{d}h.

For a semisimple element
X\in\widetilde{\mathfrak{u}}^{V}(F)
and for
\varphi\in\mathcal{S}(\widetilde{\mathfrak{u}}(F))
, we define its orbital integral by

J_{X}(\varphi)=J_{X}^{V}(\varphi):=\int_{\U(V)_{X}(F)\backslash\U(V)(F)}\varphi(X\cdot h)\mathrm{d}h.

The integral is absolutely convergent for the same reason and depends only on the orbit
\U(V)(F)
of
X
. Let
\mathfrak{o}
be a semisimple orbit in
\widetilde{\mathfrak{u}}^{V}(F)
, we define
J_{\mathfrak{o}}(\varphi)
to be
J_{X}(\varphi)
, for any
X\in\mathfrak{o}
. When
X
is regular semisimple, this reduces to

J_{X}(\varphi)=\int_{\U(V)(F)}\varphi(X\cdot h)\mathrm{d}h.

For
\lambda\in F
, we write
Z_{\lambda}
for the element

(\lambda\cdot\mathrm{id},0,0)\in\widetilde{\mathfrak{u}}^{V}
. The subspace of
\widetilde{\mathfrak{u}}^{V}
formed by
Z_{\lambda}
is the center of
\widetilde{\mathfrak{u}}^{V}
under the
\U(V)
action. Its image in
\mathcal{A}
coincides with the center we considered in Subsection
2.5
. By definition,
Z_{\lambda}
is semisimple and for
\varphi\in\mathcal{S}(\widetilde{\mathfrak{u}}^{V}(F))
we have

J_{Z_{\lambda}}(\varphi)=\varphi(Z_{\lambda}).

7.2.
Transfer of functions

The GIT quotient
\mathrm{G}^{V}/\mathrm{H}^{V}\times\mathrm{H}^{V}
(resp.
\widetilde{\mathfrak{u}}^{V}(F)/\U(V)
) can be canonically identified with
\mathrm{B}
(resp.
\mathcal{A}
), (see
[
11
]
*Lemme 15.1.4.1 and Subsection 8.1). For
\gamma^{V}=(x,y)\in\mathrm{G}^{V}(F)
and
\gamma=(g_{n},g_{n+1})\in\mathrm{G}^{\prime}(F)
, we put
s=(g_{n}^{-1}g_{n+1})(g_{n}^{-1}g_{n+1})^{\mathtt{c},-1}
and
g=x^{-1}y
. Then
\gamma
and
\gamma^{V}
have the same image in
\mathrm{B}(F)
if and only if
s
and
g
have the same characteristic polynomial and
e_{n+1}^{t}s^{i}e_{n+1}=h(g^{i}e_{0},e_{0})
for
1\leq i\leq n
.

If we use the identification (
2.11
), then for
(A,v)\in\widetilde{\mathfrak{u}}^{V}
, the quotient map
q_{V}:\widetilde{\mathfrak{u}}^{V}\mathcal{A}
can be identified with

(7.1)

(A,v)(\mathrm{Tr}(\wedge^{i}A)_{1\leq i\leq n},h(A^{i}v,v)_{0\leq i\leq i-1}).

So that for
X=(A,v,u)\in\widetilde{\gl_{n}}(F)
and
X^{V}=(A^{\prime},v^{\prime})\in\widetilde{\mathfrak{u}}^{V}
,
X
and
X^{V}
have the same image in
\mathcal{A}(F)
if and only if
A^{\prime}
and
A
have the same characteristic polynomial and
uA^{i}v=h(A‘^{i}v’,v‘)
for
1\leq i\leq n
.

These identifications induce bijections

\mathrm{G}_{\mathrm{rs}}^{\prime}(F)/\mathrm{H}_{1}(F)\times\mathrm{H}_{2}(F)\longleftrightarrow\bigsqcup_{V\in\mathcal{H}}\mathrm{G}_{\mathrm{rs}}^{V}(F)/\mathrm{H}^{V}(F)\times\mathrm{H}^{V}(F),

and

(7.2)

\widetilde{\gl_{n}}_{,\mathrm{rs}}(F)/\GL_{n}(F)\longleftrightarrow\bigsqcup_{V\in\mathcal{H}}\widetilde{\mathfrak{u}}^{V}_{\mathrm{rs}}(F)/\U(V)

We say
\gamma\in\mathrm{G}_{\mathrm{rs}}^{\prime}(F)
(resp.
X\in\widetilde{\gl_{n}}(F)
) matches with
\gamma^{V}\in\mathrm{G}^{V}_{\mathrm{rs}}(F)
(resp.
X^{V}\in\widetilde{\mathfrak{u}}^{V}_{\mathrm{rs}}(V)
) if they correspond to each other under the bijection above, equivalently, their image in
\mathrm{B}(F)
(resp.
\mathcal{A}(F)
) are the same.

We define the transfer factors

\Omega^{+},\Omega^{-}:\mathrm{G}^{\prime}_{\mathrm{rs}}(F)\mathbb{C}^{\times},\text{and }\omega^{+},\omega^{-}:\widetilde{\gl_{n}}_{,\mathrm{rs}}(F)\mathbb{C}^{\times}

by

\Omega^{\varepsilon}(\gamma_{n},\gamma_{n+1})=\begin{cases}\mu(\gamma_{n}^{-1}\gamma_{n+1})\mu(x)^{-\frac{n+1}{2}}\mu(\Delta^{\varepsilon}(x))&n\text{ odd}\\
\mu(x)^{-\frac{n}{2}}\mu(\Delta^{\varepsilon}(x))&n\text{ even}\end{cases},

where
x=\nu(\gamma_{n}^{-1}\gamma_{n+1})
and

\omega^{\varepsilon}(X)=\eta(\delta^{\varepsilon}(X)).

where
\varepsilon\in\{+,-\}
.

We say that
f\in\mathcal{S}(\mathrm{G}^{\prime}(F))
and
(f^{V}\in\mathcal{S}(\mathrm{G}^{V}(F)))_{V\in\mathcal{H}}
are plus-transfer (resp. minus-transfer) of each other, if whenever the element
\gamma\in\mathrm{G}^{\prime}_{\mathrm{rs}}(F)
and
\gamma^{V}\in\mathrm{G}^{V}_{\mathrm{rs}}(F)
match, we have

J^{V}_{\gamma^{V}}(f^{V})=\Omega^{+}(\gamma)\cdot I_{\gamma}(f)\text{ (resp. }J^{V}_{\gamma^{V}}(f^{V})=\Omega^{-}(\gamma)\cdot I_{\gamma}(f)\text{)}

We write
f\xleftrightarrow{+}(f^{V})_{V}
(resp.
f\xleftrightarrow{-}(f^{V})_{V}
) if they are plus-transfer (resp. minus transfer).

Similarly, we define
\varphi\in\mathcal{S}(\widetilde{\gl_{n}}(F))\xleftrightarrow{+}(\varphi^{V}\in\mathcal{S}(\widetilde{\mathfrak{u}}^{V}(F))_{V\in\mathcal{H}}
(resp.
\varphi\xleftrightarrow{-}(\varphi^{V})_{V}
) if whenever
X\in\widetilde{\gl_{n}}_{,\mathrm{rs}}(F)
and
X^{V}\in\widetilde{\mathfrak{u}}_{\mathrm{rs}}^{V}(F)
match, we have

J^{V}_{X^{V}}(\varphi^{V})=\omega^{+}(X)\cdot I_{X}(\varphi)\text{ (resp. }J^{V}_{X^{V}}(\varphi^{V})=\omega^{-}(X)\cdot I_{X}(\varphi)\text{)}

Lemma 7.1
.

Given
\varphi\in\mathcal{S}(\widetilde{\gl_{n}}(F))
and
\varphi^{V}\in\mathcal{S}(\widetilde{\mathfrak{u}}^{V}(F))
for each
V\in\mathcal{H}
. Then

\varphi\xleftrightarrow{+}(\varphi^{V})\iff\varphi\xleftrightarrow{-}(\eta(\mathrm{disc}(V))\cdot\varphi^{V})

Proof.

For
(V,h)\in\mathcal{H}
and
X^{V}=(A,v)\in\widetilde{\mathfrak{u}}^{V}(F)
, define
d_{n}(X)=\det(h(A^{i+j-2}v,v)_{1\leq i,j\leq n})
. For
X\in\widetilde{\gl_{n}}(F)
, we have defined
d_{n}(X)
in (
2.13
).

Now
\varphi\xleftrightarrow{+}(\varphi^{V})
if and only if for any matching of regular semisimple elements
X
and
X^{V}
, we have

J^{V}_{X^{V}}(\varphi^{V})=\omega^{+}(X)I_{X}(\varphi).

Note that
d_{n}(X)=\det(\delta^{+}(X)\delta^{-}(X))
, so the condition above is the same as

J^{V}_{X^{V}}(\varphi^{V})\eta(d_{n}(X))=\omega^{-}(X)I_{X}(\varphi).

The matching of
X
and
X^{V}
implies
d_{n}(X)=d_{n}(X^{V})
. Moreover,
\eta(d_{n}(X^{V}))=\eta(\mathrm{disc}(V))
, since
(v,Av,\cdots,A^{n-1}v)
is a basis for
V
. So the last equation is equivalent to

J^{V}_{X^{V}}(\varphi^{V})\eta(\mathrm{disc}(V))=\omega^{-}(X)I_{X}(\varphi),

which is equivalent to
\varphi\xleftrightarrow{-}(\eta(\mathrm{disc}(V))\cdot\varphi^{V})
.
∎

We also recall the deep results of Zhang and Xue:

Theorem 7.2

(Zhang
[
23
]
,Xue
[
19
]
)
.

Given
\varphi\in\mathcal{S}(\widetilde{\gl_{n}}(F))
and
\varphi^{V}\in\mathcal{S}(\widetilde{\mathfrak{u}}^{V}(F))
for each
V\in\mathcal{H}
. Then

\varphi\xleftrightarrow{+}(\varphi^{V})\iff\mathcal{F}\varphi\xleftrightarrow{+}\left(\eta(\mathrm{disc}(V))^{n}\varepsilon(\eta,\frac{1}{2},\psi)^{\frac{n(n+1)}{2}}\mathcal{F}\varphi^{V}\right)

Remark 7.3
.

Suppose that for each
0\leq i\leq k
, we have a field extension
F_{i}/F
. Write
E_{i}=F_{i}\otimes_{F}E
, and suppose that for each
i
, we have an
n_{i}
-dimensional
E_{i}/F_{i}
Hermitian space
V_{i}
. Let

\widetilde{\mathfrak{g}}=\prod_{i=0}^{k}\mathrm{Res}_{F_{i}/F}\widetilde{\gl_{n_{i},F_{i}}},\quad\widetilde{\mathfrak{u}}=\prod_{i=0}^{k}\mathrm{Res}_{F_{i}/F}\widetilde{\mathfrak{u}}^{V_{i}}.

We write
\eta_{i}:=\eta_{E\otimes F_{i}/F_{i}}=\eta\circ\mathrm{Nm}_{F_{i}/F}
for the quadratic character on
F_{i}^{\times}
. For
X=(X_{i})\in\widetilde{\mathfrak{g}}(F)
, let
\omega^{\pm}(X):=\prod_{i=0}^{k}\eta_{i}(\det\delta^{\pm}(X_{i}))
.

The orbital integral and transfers then directly generalize to
\varphi\in\mathcal{S}(\widetilde{\mathfrak{g}}(F))
and
\varphi^{V}\in\mathcal{S}(\widetilde{\mathfrak{u}}(F))
.

7.3.
Descent on
\tilde{\mathfrak{u}}

7.3.1.
Descent construction

There is a descent construction on
\widetilde{\mathfrak{u}}^{V}
which is similar to the descent for
\widetilde{\gl_{n}}
as we discussed in Subsection
2.5
. Recall that we have a quotient map
q=q^{V}:\widetilde{\mathfrak{u}}^{V}\mathcal{A}
. For
X=(A,b)\in\widetilde{\mathfrak{u}}^{V}(F)
, let
d_{r}(X)=\det(h(A^{i+j-2}b,b)_{1\leq i,j\leq r}
. Then
d_{r}
descends to a function on
\mathcal{A}
and coincides with the function in (
2.13
). The functions
d_{r}
induce a stratification
\widetilde{\mathfrak{u}}^{V,(r)}
on
\widetilde{\mathfrak{u}}^{V}
in the same way as we discussed after (
2.13
).

Fix
a\in\mathcal{A}^{(r)}(F)
. Let
\mathcal{A}_{0}:=\widetilde{\gl_{r}}/\GL_{r},\mathcal{A}^{0}:=\widetilde{\gl_{n-r}}/\GL_{n-r}
. As we discussed in Subsection
2.5
,
a
can be written uniquely
\iota(a_{0},a^{0})
with
a_{0}\in\mathcal{A}_{0}(F),a^{0}\in\mathcal{A}^{0}(F)
, and
a^{0}
is determined by a polynomial
P\in F[x]
, which factorize as
P=P_{1}^{n_{1}}\cdots P_{k}^{n_{k}}
. Let
F_{i}=F[x]/(P_{i})
and
\alpha_{i}
be the image of
x
in
F_{i}
. Put
E_{i}=F_{i}\otimes_{F}E
, and we fix an
r
-dimensional
E
vector space
V_{0}
and
n_{i}
-dimensional
E_{i}
vector spaces for each
1\leq i\leq k
. Let
\mathcal{H}^{\flat}
be the isometric class of the family
(h_{i})_{0\leq i\leq n}
, where each
h_{i}
is a non-degenerate Hermitian form on
V_{i}
. Take
h^{\flat}=(h_{i})\in\mathcal{H}^{\flat}
, put

(7.3)

(V^{0},h^{\flat,0}):=\bigoplus_{i=1}^{k}\mathrm{Res}_{E_{i}/E}(V_{i},h_{i}),

where
\mathrm{Res}_{E_{i}/E}(V_{i},h_{i})
is the
E/F
Hermitian space given by
(\mathrm{Res}_{E_{i}/E}V,\mathrm{Tr}_{E_{i}/E}\circ h_{i})
, and
\oplus
denotes the orthogonal direct sum. We also write
h^{\flat}_{\oplus}
for the
E
vector space
V_{0}\oplus V^{0}
equipped with the Hermitian form
h_{0}\oplus h^{\flat,0}
.

Note that
\mathcal{A}_{0}
and
\mathcal{A}^{0}
can be canonically identified with
\widetilde{\mathfrak{u}_{0}}/\U(V_{0})
and
\widetilde{\mathfrak{u}}^{V^{0}}/\U(V^{0})
respectively.

Put

\U_{h_{0}}:=\U(V_{0},h_{0}),\quad\U_{h_{i}}:=\mathrm{Res}_{F_{i}/F}\U(V_{i},h_{i}),\quad\U_{h^{\flat}}^{0}:=\prod_{i=1}^{k}\U_{h_{i}},\quad\U_{h^{\flat}}:=\prod_{i=0}^{k}\U_{h_{i}}.

For each
1\leq i\leq k
, we regard
\widetilde{\mathfrak{u}}^{h_{i}}
as an
F
-vector space and define

\quad\widetilde{\mathfrak{u}}^{h^{\flat},0}:=\prod_{i=1}^{k}\widetilde{\mathfrak{u}}^{h_{i}},\quad\widetilde{\mathfrak{u}}^{h^{\flat}}:=\prod_{i=0}^{k}\widetilde{\mathfrak{u}}^{h_{i}}.

We then have GIT quotients:

\mathcal{A}_{i}:=\widetilde{\mathfrak{u}}^{h_{i}}/\U_{h_{i}},\quad\mathcal{A}_{h^{\flat}}^{0}:=\widetilde{\mathfrak{u}}^{h^{\flat},0}/\U_{h^{\flat}}^{0}\cong\prod_{i=1}^{k}\mathcal{A}_{i},\quad\mathcal{A}_{h^{\flat}}:=\widetilde{\mathfrak{u}}^{h^{\flat}}/\U_{h^{\flat}}\cong\prod_{i=0}^{k}\mathcal{A}_{i}

Using (
7.3
), we have embeddings:

\iota_{\U^{0}}:\U^{0}_{h^{\flat}}\U(V^{0}),\quad\iota_{\mathfrak{u}^{0}}:\widetilde{\mathfrak{u}}^{h^{\flat},0}\widetilde{\mathfrak{u}}^{V^{0}}.

Let
V\in\mathcal{H}
such that there exists an isometry

i:h^{\flat}_{\oplus}\cong V.

The map
i
then determines an embedding

(7.4)

\iota:\widetilde{\mathfrak{u}}^{h_{0},(r)}\times\widetilde{\mathfrak{u}}^{V^{0}}\widetilde{\mathfrak{u}}^{V}

similar to what we have considered in (
2.14
), see
[
11
]
*(8.2.2.4). It descends to a map on the GIT quotient which coincides with
\iota
in (
2.16
).

Let
\widetilde{\mathfrak{u}}^{h^{\flat},0,\prime}
be the open subset of
\widetilde{\mathfrak{u}}^{h^{\flat},0}
consists of
(A_{i},v_{i})
with
\det Q_{i}(A_{j})\neq 0
for all
i\neq j
, where
Q_{i}
is the characteristic polynomial of
A_{i}
, it descends to an open subset
\mathcal{A}^{0,\prime}_{h^{\flat}}
of
\mathcal{A}^{0}_{h^{\flat}}
. Denote by
\widetilde{\mathfrak{u}}^{h^{\flat},\prime}=\widetilde{\mathfrak{u}}_{0}^{(r)}\times\widetilde{\mathfrak{u}}^{h^{\flat},0,\prime}
, it descends to a subset
\mathcal{A}^{\prime}_{h^{\flat}}
of
\mathcal{A}_{h^{\flat}}
. Composing
\iota
in (
7.4
) and
\iota_{\mathfrak{u}^{0}}
, we get a map

\iota_{\mathfrak{u}^{\flat}}:\widetilde{\mathfrak{u}}^{h^{\flat},\prime}\widetilde{\mathfrak{u}},

it descends to

\iota_{\mathcal{A}}:\mathcal{A}^{\prime}_{h^{\flat}}\mathcal{A}.

Note that although the embedding
\iota_{\mathfrak{u}^{\flat}}
depends on the choice of the isometry
i
,
\iota_{\mathcal{A}}
does not, and it coincides with the map
\iota_{\mathcal{A}}
introduced in Subsection
2.5
. The isometry
i
also induces an embedding

\iota_{\U}:\U_{h^{\flat}}\hookrightarrow\U(V).

By
[
23
]
*Appendix B,
\iota_{\mathcal{A}}
is étale, and we have a pullback diagram

(7.5)

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\widetilde{\mathfrak{u}}^{h^{\flat},\prime}\times^{\U_{h^{\flat}}}\U(V)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\widetilde{\mathfrak{u}}^{V}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{A}^{\prime}_{h^{\flat}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{A}}

where the top horizontal map sends
(X,g)
to
\iota_{\mathfrak{u}^{\flat}}(X)\cdot g
.

•

The right vertical map is the quotient map, the left vertical map is induced by the quotient map
\widetilde{\mathfrak{u}}^{\flat,\prime}\mathcal{A}^{\flat,\prime}
, trivial on the second component,

•

The bottom horizontal map is
\iota_{\mathcal{A}}
, the top horizontal map sends
(X,g)
to
\iota_{\mathfrak{u}^{\flat}}(X)\cdot g
.

Since
a_{0}\in\mathcal{A}_{0}(F)
is regular semisimple, the bijection (
7.2
) implies that there exists a unique Hermitian form
h_{0}=h_{0}^{a_{0}}
on
V_{0}
up to isometry such that there exists
X_{0}\in\widetilde{\mathfrak{u}}_{\mathrm{rs}}^{h^{a_{0}}_{0}}(F)
mapping to
a_{0}\in\mathcal{A}_{0}(F)
. Let
a_{i}
be the image of
Z_{\alpha_{i}}
in
\mathcal{A}_{i}(F)
and
a_{h^{\flat}}=(a_{0},a_{1},\cdots,a_{k})\in\mathcal{A}_{h^{\flat}}(F)
.

Let
\mathcal{O}_{a}
be the set

(7.6)

\{(V,\mathfrak{o})\mid V\in\mathcal{H},\mathfrak{o}\subset\widetilde{\mathfrak{u}}^{V}_{a}(F)\text{ is a semisimple orbit}\}.

By
[
11
]
*Corollaire 8.4.6.2, there is a bijection

(7.7)

\{h^{\flat}=(h_{i})\in\mathcal{H}^{\flat}|h_{0}=h_{0}^{a_{0}}\}\leftrightarrow\mathcal{O}_{a},

sending
h^{\flat}
to
h^{\flat}_{\oplus}
together with the orbit of
\iota_{\mathfrak{u}^{\flat}}(X_{0},Z_{\alpha_{1}},\cdots,Z_{\alpha_{k}})
, note that this orbit is independent of the choice of the isometry
i
.

7.3.2.
Descent and transfer

Fix
a\in\mathcal{A}^{(r)}(F)
. In Subsection
2.5
and in
7.3.1
, we have recalled the descent construction with respect to
a
in the general linear setting and the unitary setting respectively. In the setting of Subsection
2.5
, we fix a basis of
V_{i}
so that
\delta^{+}
and
\delta^{-}
are defined. By construction,
\mathcal{A}_{H}
and
\mathcal{A}_{h^{\flat}}
can be canonically identified for any
h^{\flat}\in\mathcal{H}^{\flat}
. Under this identification
\mathcal{A}_{H}^{\prime}
corresponds to
\mathcal{A}^{\prime}_{h^{\flat}}
,
a_{H}
corresponds to
a_{h^{\flat}}
.

Since
\iota_{\mathcal{A}}
is étale and sending
a_{H}
to
a
, we can choose an open neighborhood
\omega_{H}
of
a_{H}
in
\mathcal{A}^{\prime}_{H}(F)
and an open neighbourhood
\omega
of
a
in
\mathcal{A}(F)
such that

•

If
F
is Archimedean, both
\omega
and
\omega_{H}
are semi-algebraic.

•

\omega_{H}\omega
is an isomorphism of Nash manifolds if
F
is Archimedean, and isomorphism of analytic manifolds if
F
is non-Archimedean. (See
[
9
]
*Proposition 8.1.2 for the Archimedean case)

•

There are
c^{+},c^{-}\in\{+1,-1\}
, such that for any
a^{\prime}\in\omega_{H}\cap\mathcal{A}_{H,\mathrm{rs}}(F)
and
X\in\widetilde{\mathfrak{h}}_{a}(F)
, we have

(7.8)

\omega^{+}(\iota(X))=c^{+}\omega^{+}(X),\quad\omega^{-}(\iota(X))=c^{-}\omega^{-}(X).

where
\omega^{+}(X)
and
\omega^{-}(X)
are defined in Remark
7.3
. (See
[
23
]
*Lemma 3.15).

Let
\Omega
and
\Omega_{H}
be the preimage of
\omega
and
\omega_{H}
under the quotient map
\widetilde{\gl_{n}}\mathcal{A}
and
\widetilde{\mathfrak{h}}\mathcal{A}_{H}
respectively. Then the top horizontal map in diagram (
2.20
) induces an isomorphism

(7.9)

\Omega_{H}\times^{H(F)}\GL_{n}(F)\Omega,\quad(Y,g)\iota_{\mathfrak{h}}(Y)\cdot g

of Nash manifolds when
F
is archimedean and analytic manifolds if
F
is non-archimedean.

Let
h^{\flat}\in\mathcal{H}^{\flat}
. Under the identification between
\mathcal{A}_{H}^{\prime}
and
\mathcal{A}^{\prime}_{h^{\flat}}
,
\omega_{H}
corresponds to
\omega_{h^{\flat}}\subset\mathcal{A}^{\prime}_{h^{\flat}}(F)
. Let
\Omega_{V}
and
\Omega_{h^{\flat}}
be the preimage of
\omega
and
\omega_{h^{\flat}}
under the quotient map
\widetilde{\mathfrak{u}}^{V}\mathcal{A}
and
\widetilde{\mathfrak{u}}^{h^{\flat}}\mathcal{A}_{h^{\flat}}
respectively. By the general fact of group action of variety over local field,
\widetilde{\mathfrak{u}}^{h^{\flat},\prime}(F)\times^{\U_{h^{\flat}}(F)}\U(V)(F)
is naturally an open and closed subset of
(\widetilde{\mathfrak{u}}^{h^{\flat},\prime}\times^{\U_{h^{\flat}}}\U(V))(F)
. Let
\Omega_{V}^{h^{\flat}}
be the image of
\Omega_{h^{\flat}}\times^{\U^{\flat}(F)}\U^{V}(F)
under the top horizontal map of (
7.5
). Then
\Omega_{V}^{h^{\flat}}
is independent of choice of
i
(thus only depends on
V
and
h^{\flat}
), and is an open and closed subset of
\Omega_{V}
and we have an isomorphism

(7.10)

\Omega_{h^{\flat}}\times^{\U^{\flat}(F)}\U^{V}(F)\Omega_{V}^{h^{\flat}},\quad(Y,g)\iota^{\flat}(Y)\cdot g

of Nash manifolds when
F
is archimedean and analytic manifolds if
F
is non-archimedean.

Let
\mathcal{H}^{\flat}_{V}
be the subset of
\mathcal{H}^{\flat}
consisting of
h^{\flat}=(h_{i})\in\mathcal{H}^{\flat}
such that
h_{0}=h_{0}^{a_{0}}
and
V\cong h^{\flat}_{\oplus}
. By
[
11
]
*Lemme 13.4.7.1, shrinking
\omega
if necessary, we have a disjoint union decomposition

(7.11)

\Omega_{V}=\bigsqcup_{h^{\flat}\in\mathcal{H}^{\flat}_{V}}\Omega_{V}^{h^{\flat}}.

For
\varphi\in\mathcal{S}(\Omega)
, from
(
7.9
)
, we can choose
\varphi^{\prime}\in\mathcal{S}(\Omega_{H}\times\GL_{n}(F))
such that for any
X\in\Omega_{H}
and
g\in\GL_{n}(F)

\varphi(\iota_{\mathfrak{h}}(X)\cdot g)=\int_{H(F)}\varphi^{\prime}(Xh,\iota_{H}(h)^{-1}g)\mathrm{d}h.

We define
\varphi_{H}\in\mathcal{S}(\Omega_{H})
by

\varphi_{H}(X_{H})=\int_{\GL_{n}(F)}\varphi^{\prime}(X_{H},g)\eta(g)\mathrm{d}g,

where
X_{H}\in\Omega_{H}
.

Suppose that for each
V\in\mathcal{H}
, we have a function
\varphi^{V}\in\mathcal{S}(\Omega_{V})
. For each
h^{\flat}=(h_{i})\in\mathcal{H}^{\flat}
, we define
\varphi^{\flat}\in\mathcal{S}(\Omega_{h^{\flat}})
as follows. If
h_{0}\neq h_{0}^{a_{0}}
, we put
\varphi^{h^{\flat}}=0
. Otherwise, choose an isometry
h^{\flat}_{\oplus}\cong V
for a unique
V\in\mathcal{H}
, and from (
7.10
) and (
7.11
), we can choose a function
\varphi^{h^{\flat},\prime}\in\mathcal{S}(\Omega_{h^{\flat}}\times\U(V)(F))
such that for any
X\in\Omega_{h^{\flat}}
and
g\in\U(V)(F)

\varphi^{V}(\iota_{\mathfrak{u}^{\flat}}(X)\cdot g)=\int_{\U_{h^{\flat}}(F)}\varphi^{h^{\flat},\prime}(Xh,\iota_{\U}(h)^{-1}g)\mathrm{d}h.

Then we put
