\varphi^{h^{\flat}}(X)=\int_{\U(V)(F)}\varphi^{h^{\flat},\prime}(X,g)\mathrm{d}g,

where
X\in\Omega_{h^{\flat}}
. We have
\varphi^{h^{\flat}}\in\mathcal{S}(\Omega_{h^{\flat}})
.

We have the following lemma.

Lemma 7.4
.

Let
\varphi\in\mathcal{S}(\Omega)
and for each
V\in\mathcal{H}
, let
\varphi^{V}\in\mathcal{S}(\Omega_{V})
. We have the following assertions:

(1)

If
\varphi\xleftrightarrow{+}(\varphi^{V})_{V\in\mathcal{H}}
, then

c^{+}\varphi_{H}\xleftrightarrow{+}(\varphi^{h^{\flat}})_{h^{\flat}\in\mathcal{H}^{\flat}}.

(2)

If
\varphi\xleftrightarrow{-}(\varphi^{V})_{V\in\mathcal{H}}
, then

c^{-}\varphi_{H}\xleftrightarrow{-}(\varphi^{h^{\flat}})_{h^{\flat}\in\mathcal{H}^{\flat}}.

Where we recall
c^{\pm}
is defined in (
7.8
).

Proof.

We only prove (1), the proof of (2) is the same as (1). Take any
a^{\prime}\in\mathcal{A}_{H,\mathrm{rs}}(F)=\mathcal{A}_{h^{\flat},\mathrm{rs}}(F)
, assume that
a^{\prime}
is the image of some elements in
\widetilde{\mathfrak{u}}^{h^{\flat}}(F)
.

Take any
X_{H}\in\widetilde{\mathfrak{h}}_{a^{\prime}}(F),X_{\flat}\in\widetilde{\mathfrak{u}}^{h^{\flat}}_{a^{\prime}}(F)
, we need to check that

\omega^{+}(X_{H})I_{X_{H}}(\varphi_{H})=J_{X_{\flat}}(\varphi^{h^{\flat}}).

If
a^{\prime}\not\in\omega_{H}=\omega_{h^{\flat}}
, then by definition

I_{X_{H}}(\varphi_{H})=J_{X_{\flat}}(\varphi^{h^{\flat}})=0.

Thus we only need to consider the case when
a^{\prime}\in\omega_{H}=\omega_{h^{\flat}}
. Since
\omega_{H}\subset\mathcal{A}_{H}^{\prime}
, by
[
11
]
*Lemme 3.4.1.1, we have
\iota_{\mathcal{A}}(a^{\prime})\in\mathcal{A}_{\mathrm{rs}}(F)
. Using the definition of
\varphi_{H}
and
\varphi^{h^{\flat}}
, we check directly that

(7.12)

I_{X_{H}}(\varphi_{H})=I_{\iota_{\mathfrak{h}}(X)}(\varphi),\quad J_{X^{\flat}}(\varphi^{h^{\flat}})=J_{\iota_{\mathfrak{u}^{\flat}}(X_{\flat})}(\varphi^{V}).

The image of
\iota_{\mathfrak{h}}(X)
and
\iota_{\mathfrak{u}^{\flat}}(X_{\flat})
in
\mathcal{A}(F)
are both
\iota_{\mathcal{A}}(a^{\prime})
, hence their orbits match. The lemma then follows from the definition of transfer and
c^{\pm}
.
∎

We also record a lemma whose proof is elementary.

Lemma 7.5
.

Let
u\in C_{c}^{\infty}(\omega)
,
\varphi\in\mathcal{S}(\widetilde{\gl_{n}}(F))
and for each
V\in\mathcal{H}
, let
\varphi^{V}\in\mathcal{S}(\widetilde{\mathfrak{u}}^{V}(F))
. For
\varepsilon\in\{+,-\}
, if
\varphi\xleftrightarrow{\varepsilon}(\varphi^{V})
,
then

\varphi\cdot(q\circ u)\in\mathcal{S}(\Omega)\xleftrightarrow{\varepsilon}(\varphi^{V}\cdot(q_{V}\circ u)\in\mathcal{S}(\Omega_{V})).

Proof.

This is proved by a direct computation.
∎

7.4.
Singular transfer on the Lie algebra

Let
X\in\widetilde{\gl_{n}}(F)
be a regular element and
\varphi\in\mathcal{S}(\widetilde{\gl_{n}}(F))
. Recall that we have defined
I_{X}(\varphi,\xi,s)
in Definition
5.7
.

The function
I_{X}(\varphi,1_{E^{\times}},s)/L_{X}(s,1_{E}^{\times})
is holomorphic at
s=0
. We write
I_{X}^{\natural}(\varphi)
for
I_{X}^{\natural}(\varphi,1_{E^{\times}},0)
.

We now define some constants related to Proposition
7.6
below. Let
F^{\prime}
be a finite extension of
F
, and we write
E^{\prime}:=F^{\prime}\otimes_{F}E
. Let
\eta^{\prime}:=\eta_{E/F}\circ\mathrm{Nm}_{F^{\prime}/F}=\eta_{E^{\prime}/F^{\prime}}
and
\psi^{\prime}=\psi\circ\mathrm{Tr}_{F^{\prime}/F}
. Let
(V^{\prime},h^{\prime})
be an
n^{\prime}
-dimensional
E^{\prime}/F^{\prime}
Hermitian space. We put

c_{V^{\prime}}^{+}=\prod_{i=1}^{n^{\prime}}\varepsilon(1-i,\eta^{\prime,i},\psi^{\prime})^{-1}\eta^{\prime}(\mathrm{disc}(V^{\prime}))^{n^{\prime}+1}\varepsilon(\frac{1}{2},\eta^{\prime},\psi^{\prime})^{\frac{n^{\prime}(n^{\prime}+1)}{2}}\eta^{\prime}(-1)^{\frac{n^{\prime}(n^{\prime}-1)}{2}}.

and

c_{V^{\prime}}^{-}=c_{V^{\prime}}^{+}\cdot\eta^{\prime}(\mathrm{disc}(V^{\prime}))=\prod_{i=1}^{n^{\prime}}\varepsilon(1-i,\eta^{\prime,i},\psi^{\prime})^{-1}\eta^{\prime}(\mathrm{disc}(V^{\prime}))^{n^{\prime}}\varepsilon(\frac{1}{2},\eta^{\prime},\psi^{\prime})^{\frac{n^{\prime}(n^{\prime}+1)}{2}}\eta^{\prime}(-1)^{\frac{n^{\prime}(n^{\prime}-1)}{2}}.

Let
h^{\flat}=(h_{i})\in\mathcal{H}^{\flat}
and let
\varepsilon:\{1,\cdots,k\}\{+,-\}
be a map, we put
c^{\varepsilon}_{h^{\flat}}=\prod_{i=1}^{k}c^{\varepsilon_{i}}_{(V_{i},h_{i})}
. Let
\mathfrak{o}\subset\widetilde{\mathfrak{u}}_{a}^{V}(F)
be a semisimple orbit, by the bijection (
7.7
),
\mathfrak{o}
is the orbit of
\iota_{h^{\flat}}(X_{0},Z_{\alpha_{1}},\cdots,Z_{\alpha_{k}})
for some
h^{\flat}=(h_{i})\in\mathcal{H}^{\flat}_{V}
with
X_{0}\in\widetilde{\mathfrak{u}}^{V_{0}}_{a_{0}}(F)
. We then put

c_{\mathfrak{o}}^{\varepsilon}:=c_{h^{\flat}}^{\varepsilon}.

Let
X\in\widetilde{\gl_{n}}(F)
be a regular element with
q(X)=a
. We use the descent construction as discussed in
7.3.2
.
X
can be written of the form
\iota_{\mathfrak{h}}(X_{H})\cdot g
, where
X_{H}=(X_{0,H},\cdots)\in\Omega_{H}
is a regular element, and
g\in\GL_{n}(F)
. we define a constant
c_{X}\in\{\pm 1\}
by

c_{X}=c^{+}\eta(g)\omega^{+}(X_{0,H}).

where
c^{+}
is defined in (
7.8
). Finally, if
X\in\widetilde{\gl_{n}}(F)
is a regular element of type
\varepsilon
, we put

c_{X,\mathfrak{o}}=c_{X}c_{\mathfrak{o}}^{\varepsilon}.

Now we state our main proposition

Proposition 7.6
.

Let
X\in\widetilde{\gl_{n}}(F)
be a regular element with
q(X)=a
. Suppose
\varphi\in\mathcal{S}(\widetilde{\gl_{n}}(F))\xleftrightarrow{+}(\varphi^{V}\in\mathcal{S}(\widetilde{\mathfrak{u}}^{V}(F)))_{V\in\mathcal{H}}
, then

I^{\natural}_{X}(\varphi)=\sum_{(V,\mathfrak{o})\in\mathcal{O}_{a}}c_{X,\mathfrak{o}}J^{V}_{\mathfrak{o}}(\varphi^{V}),

where we recall that the set
\mathcal{O}_{a}
is defined in (
7.6
).

Proof.

By definition, for any semisimple orbit
\mathfrak{o}
.
J^{V}_{\mathfrak{o}}(\varphi^{V})
only depends on the value of
\varphi^{V}
on
\mathfrak{o}
. By Proposition
5.8
(2),
I_{X}(\varphi)
depends only on the value of
\varphi
at the orbit of
X
. Let
\omega
be the open subset of
\mathcal{A}(F)
as introduced in
7.3.2
. Take
u\in C_{c}^{\infty}(\omega)
with
u(a)=1
. Thus
I_{X}(\varphi\cdot(q\circ u))=I_{X}(\varphi)
, and for any
\mathfrak{o}\in\mathcal{O}_{a}
,
J_{\mathfrak{o}}(\varphi^{V})=J_{\mathfrak{o}}(\varphi^{V}\cdot(q_{V}\circ u))
. By Lemma
7.5
, after replacing
\varphi
and
(\varphi^{V})
by
\varphi\cdot(q\circ u)
and
\varphi^{V}\cdot(q_{V}\circ u)
, we can assume
\varphi\in\mathcal{S}(\Omega)
and
\varphi^{V}\in\mathcal{S}(\Omega_{V})
.

Recall that we have defined
\varphi_{H}\in\mathcal{S}(\Omega_{H})
and
\varphi^{h^{\flat}}\in\mathcal{S}(\Omega_{h^{\flat}})
for each
h^{\flat}\in\mathcal{H}^{\flat}
in
7.3.2
. Choose
X_{H}=(X_{0,H},Z_{\alpha_{1}}^{\varepsilon_{1}},\cdots,Z_{\alpha_{k}}^{\varepsilon_{k}})\in\Omega_{H}
and
g\in\GL_{n}(F)
such that
X=\iota_{\mathfrak{h}}(X_{H})\cdot g
. From the definition of
I_{X}(\varphi)
in (
5.18
), we have
I_{X}(\varphi)=\eta(g)I_{X_{H}}(\varphi_{H})
. For
\mathfrak{o}\in\mathcal{O}_{a}
, thanks to the bijection (
7.7
), we can write
\mathfrak{o}
as the orbit of
\iota_{\mathfrak{u}^{h^{\flat}}}(X_{0},Z_{\alpha_{1}},\cdots,Z_{\alpha_{k}})
, where
h^{\flat}=(h_{i})\in\mathcal{H}^{\flat}
with
h_{0}=h_{0}^{a_{0}}
and
X_{0}\in\widetilde{\mathfrak{u}}^{h_{0}}_{a_{0}}(F)
. Write
X_{h^{\flat}}=(X_{0},Z_{\alpha_{1}},\cdots,Z_{\alpha_{k}})
, then we see that

\displaystyle J_{X}(\varphi^{V})

\displaystyle=\int_{\U(V)_{X}(F)\backslash\U(V)(F)}\int_{\U_{h^{\flat}}(F)}\varphi^{h^{\flat},\prime}(X_{h^{\flat}}\cdot h,\iota_{\U}(h)^{-1}g)\mathrm{d}h\mathrm{d}g

\displaystyle=\int_{\U(V)_{X}(F)\backslash\U(V)(F)}\int_{\U_{h^{\flat}}^{0}(F)\backslash\U_{h^{\flat}}(F)}\int_{\U_{h^{\flat}}^{0}(F)}\varphi^{h^{\flat},\prime}(X_{h^{\flat}}\cdot h,\iota_{\U}(h^{\prime}h)^{-1}g)\mathrm{d}h^{\prime}\mathrm{d}h\mathrm{d}g

\displaystyle=\int_{\U_{h_{0}}(F)}\int_{\U(V)(F)}\varphi^{h^{\flat}}(X_{h^{\flat}}\cdot h)\mathrm{d}h=J_{X^{\flat}}(\varphi^{h^{\flat}}).

Recall if
h_{0}\neq h_{0}^{a_{0}}
, then
\varphi^{h^{\flat}}=0
. By Lemma
7.4
,
c^{+}\varphi_{H}\xleftrightarrow{+}\varphi^{h^{\flat}}
.

Thus we are left show that for any
\varphi_{1}\in\widetilde{\mathfrak{h}}(F)
matches with
(\varphi^{h^{\flat}}_{1}\in\mathcal{S}(\widetilde{\mathfrak{u}}^{h^{\flat}}(F)))_{h^{\flat}\in\mathcal{H}^{\flat}}
, we have

\omega^{+}(X_{0,H})I^{\natural}_{X_{H}}(\varphi_{1})=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}h^{\flat}=(h_{i})\in\mathcal{H}^{\flat}\\
h_{0}=h_{0}^{a_{0}}\end{subarray}}c_{h^{\flat}}^{\varepsilon_{i}}J_{X^{\flat}}(\varphi_{1}^{h^{\flat}}).

We henceforth reduce to the case when both
\varphi
and
\varphi^{h^{\flat}}
are pure tensors. The 0-th component is regular semisimple, hence the equality follows from the definition of transfer. For other components, we are reduced to the Lemma
7.7
below, which itself is a special case of this proposition. This finishes the proof.
∎

Lemma 7.7
.

Let
\varphi\in\mathcal{S}(\widetilde{\gl_{n}}(F))\xleftrightarrow{+}(\varphi^{V}\in\mathcal{S}(\widetilde{\mathfrak{u}}^{V}(F)))_{V\in\mathcal{H}}
and let
\lambda\in F
, we have

I^{\natural}_{Z_{\lambda}^{+}}(\varphi)=\sum_{V\in\mathcal{H}}c_{V}^{+}J_{Z_{\lambda}}(\varphi^{V})\text{ and }I^{\natural}_{Z_{\lambda}^{-}}(\varphi)=\sum_{V\in\mathcal{H}}c_{V}^{-}J_{Z_{\lambda}}(\varphi^{V}).

Proof.

We only prove the assertion for
I^{\natural}_{Z_{\lambda}^{+}}
. The case for
I^{\natural}_{Z_{\lambda}^{-}}
is parallel.
It is clear that
\varphi_{\lambda}\xleftrightarrow{+}(\varphi^{V}_{\lambda})
By Theorem
7.2
and Lemma
7.1
we have

\mathcal{F}\varphi_{\lambda}\xleftrightarrow{-}\left(\eta(\mathrm{disc}(V))^{n+1}\varepsilon(\eta,\frac{1}{2},\psi)^{\frac{n(n+1)}{2}}\mathcal{F}\varphi_{\lambda}^{V}\right).

If we put

\gamma^{+}(s)=\prod_{i=1}^{n}\gamma(-is-i+1,\eta^{i},\psi).

Then

\gamma^{+}(s)L_{Z_{\lambda}^{+}}(s)=\prod_{i=1}^{n}L(is+i,\eta^{i})\varepsilon(-is-i+1,\eta^{i},\psi)

is non-vanishing at
s=0
with value
\prod_{i=1}^{n}L(i,\eta^{i})\varepsilon(1-i,\eta^{i},\psi)
. Also note that
\omega^{-}(Z_{0}^{-})=\eta(-1)^{\frac{n(n-1)}{2}}
.

Combining the discussion above and (
5.10
), we see that

\displaystyle I^{\natural}_{Z_{\lambda}^{+}}(\varphi)

\displaystyle=\zeta_{n}\eta(-1)^{\frac{n(n-1)}{2}}\frac{1}{\prod_{i=1}^{n}L(i,\eta^{i})\varepsilon(1-i,\eta^{i},\psi)}\int_{\widetilde{\gl_{n}}(F)}\mathcal{F}\varphi_{\lambda}(X)\omega^{-}(X)\mathrm{d}X

\displaystyle=\zeta_{n}\eta(-1)^{\frac{n(n-1)}{2}}\frac{1}{\prod_{i=1}^{n}L(i,\eta^{i})\varepsilon(1-i,\eta^{i},\psi)}\int_{\mathcal{A}_{\mathrm{rs}}(F)}\int_{\GL_{n}(F)}\mathcal{F}\varphi_{\lambda}(X\cdot g)\omega^{-}(X\cdot g)\mathrm{d}g\mathrm{d}X.

By
[
8
]
*Lemma 5.2.1, the bijection (
7.2
) is measure preserving up to constant. More precisely, suppose a measurable subset
M
corresponds to
\sqcup M_{V}
, then

\vol(M)=\frac{\prod_{i=1}^{n}L(i,\eta^{i})}{\zeta_{n}}\vol(M_{V}).

Therefore, the expression for
I_{Z_{\lambda}^{+}}^{\natural}(\varphi)
can be written as

\displaystyle\frac{\eta(-1)^{\frac{n(n-1)}{2}}}{\prod_{i=1}^{n}\varepsilon(1-i,\eta^{i},\psi)}\sum_{V\in\mathcal{H}}\int_{\widetilde{\mathfrak{u}}^{V}_{\mathrm{rs}}(F)/\U(V)(F)}\eta(\mathrm{disc}(V))^{n+1}\varepsilon(\eta,\frac{1}{2},\psi)^{\frac{n(n+1)}{2}}\int_{\U(V)(F)}\mathcal{F}\varphi^{V}_{\lambda}(X\cdot g)\mathrm{d}g\mathrm{d}X

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{V\in\mathcal{H}}c_{V}^{+}\int_{\widetilde{\mathfrak{u}}^{V}(F)}\mathcal{F}\varphi_{\lambda}^{V}(X)\mathrm{d}X=\sum_{V\in\mathcal{H}}c_{V}^{+}J^{V}_{Z_{\lambda}}(\varphi^{V}).

Thus the lemma is proved.
∎

When
X
lies in
\widetilde{\gl_{n}}_{,+}(F)
, then the complex number
\omega^{+}(X)I_{X}^{\natural}(\varphi)
only depends on the image
a
of
X
in
\mathcal{A}(F)
. In this case, the Proposition
7.6
can be stated as follows: if
\varphi\xleftrightarrow{+}(\varphi^{V})_{V\in\mathcal{H}}
, then

(7.13)

\omega^{+}(X)I_{X}^{\natural}(\varphi)=\sum_{V,\mathfrak{o}}c^{\prime}_{a,\mathfrak{o}}J_{\mathfrak{o}}^{V}(\varphi^{V}),

where the constant
c^{\prime}_{a,\mathfrak{o}}:=c^{+}c_{\mathfrak{o}}^{+,\prime}
and
c_{\mathfrak{o}}^{+,\prime}
is defined similarly as
c_{\mathfrak{o}}^{+}
, but we omit the constant
\eta^{\prime}(-1)^{\frac{n(n-1)}{2}}
in the definition of
c_{V^{\prime}}^{+}
.

We also record a lemma related to the proof above.

Lemma 7.8
.

Let
V\in\mathcal{H}
and let
\mathfrak{o}\subset\widetilde{\mathfrak{u}}
be a semisimple orbit. Then the distribution
J_{\mathfrak{o}}
is a stable distribution, in the sense that if
\varphi\in\mathcal{S}(\widetilde{\mathfrak{u}}(F))
such that all the regular semisimple orbital integral of
\varphi
is 0, then
J_{\mathfrak{o}}(\varphi)=0
.

Proof.

Let
\varphi\in\mathcal{S}(\widetilde{\mathfrak{u}}^{V}(F))
such that all regular semisimple orbital integrals of
\varphi
is 0. Take
X\in\mathfrak{o}
, assume that
q_{V}(X)=a\in\mathcal{A}(F)
. Replacing
\varphi
by
\varphi^{V}\cdot(q_{V}\circ u)
, we can assume
\varphi\in\mathcal{S}(\Omega_{V})
. Taking
h^{\flat}\in\mathcal{H}^{\flat}
such that there is a semisimple orbit
\mathfrak{o}^{\flat}
corresponds to
\mathfrak{o}
under the bijection (
7.7
). Then as the computation in the proof of Proposition
5.8
,
J_{\mathfrak{o}}(\varphi)=J_{\mathfrak{o}^{\flat}}(\varphi^{\flat})
and
\varphi^{\flat}
also has vanishing regular semisimple orbital integrals, we therefore reduced to the case when
\mathfrak{o}
is a regular semisimple orbit or a central orbit. If
\mathfrak{o}
is regular semisimple,
J_{\mathfrak{o}}(\varphi)
vanishes by definition. If
\mathfrak{o}
is the orbit of
Z_{\lambda}
, after a translation, we can assume
\lambda=0
. Then

J_{\mathfrak{o}}(\varphi)=\varphi(0)=\int_{\widetilde{\mathfrak{u}}^{V}(F)}\mathcal{F}\varphi(X)\mathrm{d}X=\int_{\widetilde{\mathfrak{u}}^{V}_{\mathrm{rs}}(F)/\U(V)(F)}\int_{\U(V)}\varphi(X\cdot g)\mathrm{d}g\mathrm{d}X.

By
[
12
]
*3.2.4,
\mathcal{F}\varphi
also has vanishing regular semisimple orbital integrals, therefore the above expression vanishes.
∎

We introduce a variant of the results in this subsection. Recall from Subsection
2.4
that,
\mathcal{B}=\gl_{n+1}/\GL_{n}
. For
(V,h)\in\mathcal{H}
, we put
(V^{\prime},h^{\prime})=(V\oplus Ee_{0},h\oplus h_{0})
, where
\oplus
denotes the orthogonal direct sum and
h_{0}(e_{0},e_{0})=1
. We have an isomorphism of
\U(V)
representations:

(7.14)

\mathfrak{u}^{V^{\prime}}\cong\widetilde{\mathfrak{u}}^{V}\times F,\quad A((A|_{V},Ae_{0}),h^{\prime}(Ae_{0},e_{0})),

where
\U(V)
acts on
F
trivially. We thus have a canonical identification
\mathfrak{u}^{V^{\prime}}/\U(V)\cong\mathcal{B}
. Semisimple orbital integral
J_{\mathfrak{o}}(\varphi^{V})
directly generalize to
\varphi^{V}\in\mathcal{S}(\mathfrak{u}^{V^{\prime}}(F))
. For
X\in\gl_{n+1}(F)
, let
\omega^{\pm}(X)=\eta(\delta^{\pm}(X))
. Then there is an obvious notion of plus/minus transfer between
\varphi\in\mathcal{S}(\gl_{n+1}(F))
and
(\varphi^{V})\in\mathcal{S}(\mathfrak{u}^{V^{\prime}}(F))_{V\in\mathcal{H}}
. For
b\in\mathcal{B}(F)
, let
\mathcal{O}_{b}
be the set

\{(V,\mathfrak{o})\mid V\in\mathcal{H},\mathfrak{o}\subset\mathfrak{u}^{V^{\prime}}_{a}(F)\text{ is a semisimple orbit}\}.

For
\mathfrak{o}\in\mathcal{O}_{b}
, it projects to a semisimple orbit
\widetilde{\mathfrak{o}}\in\mathcal{O}_{a}
under the isomorphism (
7.14
), where
a
is the image of
b
in
\mathcal{A}
.

For
X\in\gl_{n+1}(F)
, let
Y
is the image of
X
in
\widetilde{\gl_{n}}(F)
under the bijection
\gl_{n+1}\cong\widetilde{\gl_{n}}\times F
. Let
c_{X,\mathfrak{o}}:=c_{Y,\widetilde{\mathfrak{o}}}
. Then Proposition
7.6
implies that, if
X\in\gl_{n+1}(F)
is a regular element with
q(X)=a
of type
\varepsilon
, and
\varphi\in\mathcal{S}(\gl_{n+1}(F))\xleftrightarrow{+}(\varphi^{V}\in\mathcal{S}(\mathfrak{u}^{V^{\prime}}(F)))
, then

(7.15)

I^{\natural}_{X}(\varphi)=\sum_{(V,\mathfrak{o})\in\mathcal{O}_{a}}c_{X,\mathfrak{o}}J_{\mathfrak{o}}^{V}(\varphi^{V}).

7.5.
Singular transfer on the group

We now deduce the singular transfer on the group from the Lie algebra as we discussed in Subsection
7.4
. Fix
b\in\mathrm{B}(F)
, pick
\sigma\in E^{\times}
, with
\sigma\sigma^{\mathtt{c}}=1
, so that
b\in\mathrm{B}^{\sigma}(F)
. For
f\in\mathcal{S}(\mathrm{G}^{\prime}(F))
, let
I_{\gamma}^{\sigma,\natural}(f):=I_{\gamma}^{\sigma,\natural}(f,1_{E^{\times}},0)
.

We use the Cayley map to relate the group
\U(V^{\prime})
the the Lie algebra
\mathfrak{u}^{V^{\prime}}
. Let
\tau,\sigma\in E
such that
\tau^{\mathtt{c}}=-\tau
and
\sigma\sigma^{\mathtt{c}}=1
. Let
\mathfrak{u}^{V^{\prime},\tau}
be the open subscheme of
\mathfrak{u}^{V^{\prime}}
consists of
Y\in\mathfrak{u}^{V^{\prime}}
such that
Y-\tau\cdot 1
is invertible. Let
\U(V^{\prime})^{\sigma}
be the open subscheme of
\U(V^{\prime})
consists of
g\in\U(V^{\prime})
such that
g-\sigma\cdot\mathrm{id}
is invertible and
\mathrm{G}^{V,\sigma}
be the open subscheme of
\mathrm{G}^{V}
consists of
(x,y)\in\mathrm{G}^{V}
such that
x^{-1}y\in\U(V^{\prime})^{\sigma}
. The Cayley map

\mathfrak{c}_{\sigma}^{V}:\mathfrak{u}^{V^{\prime},\tau}\U(V^{\prime})^{\sigma},\quad Y\sigma\frac{1+\tau^{-1}Y}{1-\tau^{-1}Y}

defines a
\U(V)
equivariant isomorphism between
\mathfrak{u}^{V^{\prime},\tau}
and
\U(V^{\prime})^{\sigma}
, and descends to a map
\mathcal{B}^{\tau}\mathrm{B}^{\xi}
which coincides with the map induced by
\mathfrak{c}_{\sigma}
as in (
2.3
).

We define

\mathcal{O}_{b}=\{(V,\mathcal{O})\mid V\in\mathcal{H},\mathcal{O}\subset\mathrm{G}^{V}_{b}(F)\text{ is a }\mathrm{H}^{V}(F)\times\mathrm{H}^{V}(F)\text{ semisimple orbit}\}.

Let
a=\mathfrak{c}_{\sigma}^{-1}(b)
. Note that there is a natural
H^{V}=\U(V)
equivariant isomorphism
\mathrm{G}^{V}/\mathrm{H}^{V}\cong\U(V^{\prime})
. Since
\mathfrak{c}_{\sigma}^{V}
is an isomorphism, the Cayley map induces a bijection
\mathcal{O}_{a}\mathcal{O}_{b}
, which we also denote by
\mathfrak{c}_{\sigma}
. For
\mathcal{O}\in\mathcal{O}_{b}
and
\gamma\in\mathrm{G}^{\prime,\sigma}(F)
, let
x=\nu(\gamma_{n}^{-1}\gamma_{n+1})
and define a constant
c_{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}
by

(7.16)

c_{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}=c_{\mathfrak{c}_{\sigma}^{-1}(x),\mathfrak{c}^{-1}_{\sigma}(\mathcal{O})}\mu(x)^{\frac{n+1}{2}}\mu(\gamma_{n}^{-1}\gamma_{n+1})^{-1}\mu(-2\sigma\tau^{-1})^{-\frac{n(n+1)}{2}}\mu(1-\tau^{-1}\mathfrak{c}_{\sigma}(x))^{-n}.

Now we state the main theorem of this section

Theorem 7.9
.

Let
f\in\mathcal{S}(\mathrm{G}^{\prime}(F))
and for each
V\in\mathcal{H}
let
f^{V}\in\mathcal{S}(\mathrm{G}^{V}(F))
such that
f\xleftrightarrow{+}(f^{V})
. Then for any regular element
\gamma\in\mathrm{G}^{\prime}_{b}(F)
, we have

I^{\sigma,\natural}_{\gamma}(f)=\sum_{(V,\mathcal{O})\in\mathcal{O}_{b}}c_{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}J_{\mathcal{O}}^{V}(f^{V}).

Remark 7.10
.

When
\gamma\in\mathrm{G}^{\prime}_{+}(F)
, recall from Lemma
5.14
that
I_{\gamma}^{\sigma}
is independent of the choice of
\sigma
, we denote it by
I_{\gamma}
here.

Using the equation (
7.13
), we easily see that the above theorem can be written as

(7.17)

\Omega^{+}(\gamma)I_{\gamma}^{\natural}(f)=\sum_{(V,\mathcal{O})\in\mathcal{O}_{b}}c_{X,\mathcal{O}}\omega^{+}(X)J^{V}_{\mathcal{O}}(f^{V}),

where
\mathfrak{c}_{\sigma}(X)=\nu(\gamma_{n}^{-1}\gamma_{n+1})
, and
c_{X,\mathcal{O}}:=c_{X,\mathfrak{c}_{\sigma}^{-1}(\mathcal{O})}
.

Note that the left-hand side of the equation (
7.17
) only depends on
b
. This description removes some factors appearing in (
7.16
).

Proof.

Let us denote by
q
the quotient map
\mathrm{G}^{\prime}\mathrm{B}
, for
V\in\mathcal{H}
, we denote by
q^{V}
the quotient
\mathrm{G}^{V}\mathrm{B}
. Since both
I_{\gamma}(f)
(resp.
J_{\mathcal{O}}
) only depends on the value of
f
on
\mathrm{G}^{\prime}_{b}(F)
(resp.
\mathrm{G}^{V}_{b}(F)
). Take
u\in C_{c}^{\infty}(\mathrm{B}^{\sigma}(F))
such that
u(b)=1
. After replacing
f
(resp.
f^{V}
) by
f\cdot(u\circ Q)
(resp.
f^{V}\cdot(u\circ Q_{V})
),
we can
f\in\mathcal{S}(\mathrm{G}^{\prime,\sigma}(F))
and
f^{V}\in\mathcal{S}(\mathrm{G}^{V,\sigma}(F))
.

Let
f^{\mathrm{S}}:=f^{\mathrm{S}}_{0}
defined in (
5.20
). Then
f^{\mathrm{S}}\in\mathcal{S}(\mathrm{S}^{\sigma}(F))
. We define
f^{\U(V^{\prime})}\in\mathcal{S}(\U(V^{\prime})^{\sigma}(F))
by

f^{\U(V^{\prime})}(x)=\int_{\mathrm{H}^{V}(F)}f(hx)\mathrm{d}h,

where
x\in\U(V^{\prime})(F)
.

Define
\varphi_{f}\in\mathcal{S}(\gl_{n+1}^{\tau}(F))
by

\varphi_{f}(X)=f^{\mathrm{S}}(\mathfrak{c}_{\sigma}(X))\mu(\mathfrak{c}_{\sigma}(X))^{-\frac{n+1}{2}}\mu(-2\sigma\tau^{-1})^{\frac{n(n+1)}{2}}\mu(1-\tau^{-1}X)^{-n},

where
X\in\gl_{n+1}^{\tau}(F)
. For
V\in\mathcal{H}
and
X\in\mathfrak{u}^{V^{\prime},\tau}(F)
we put
\varphi_{f^{V}}(X)=f^{\U(V^{\prime})}(\mathfrak{c}_{\sigma}(X))
, then
\varphi_{f}\in\mathcal{S}(\mathfrak{u}^{V^{\prime},\tau}(F))
. Direct computation shows if
f\xleftrightarrow{+}f^{V}
then
\varphi_{f}\xleftrightarrow{+}\varphi_{f^{V}}
.

Let
x=\nu(\gamma_{n}^{-1}\gamma_{n+1})
. By the definition of
I_{\gamma}
and
J_{\mathcal{O}}
, we have

I^{\natural}_{\mathfrak{c}_{\sigma}^{-1}(x)}(\varphi_{f})=\mu(\gamma_{n}^{-1}\gamma_{n+1})\mu(x)^{-\frac{n+1}{2}}\mu(-2\sigma\tau^{-1})^{\frac{n(n+1)}{2}}\mu(1-\tau^{-1}\mathfrak{c}_{\sigma}^{-1}(x))I^{\sigma,\natural}_{\gamma}(f),

and

J_{\mathcal{O}}^{V}(f)=J_{\mathfrak{c}(\mathcal{O})}^{V}(\varphi_{f}).

Then the theorem follows from the equality (
7.15
).
∎

8.
Fourier-Jacobi case

All the results in this article have their counterparts for the relative trace formula developed by Liu
[
17
]
. The proofs of the results in this section are parallel to their Jacquet-Rallis counterpart, so we will omit their proofs.

8.1.
Notations

Let
F
be a field of characteristic 0 and let
E
be a quadratic ètale algebra over
F
. We redefine
\mathrm{G}^{\prime}:=\mathrm{Res}_{E/F}(\GL_{n,E}\times\GL_{n,E})\times F^{n}\times F_{n}
. We put

\mathrm{S}_{n}=\{x\in\GL_{n,E}\mid xx^{\mathtt{c}}=1\},

the map
\nu:\mathrm{Res}_{E/F}\GL_{n,E}\mathrm{S}_{n},ggg^{-1,\mathtt{c}}
identifies
\mathrm{S}_{n}
with
\GL_{n,E}/\GL_{n,F}
. We redefine
\mathrm{H}_{1}
and
\mathrm{H}_{2}
to be the following subgroups of
\mathrm{Res}_{E/F}(\GL_{n,E}\times\GL_{n,E})
:

\mathrm{H}_{1}:=\{(g,g)\mid g\in\mathrm{Res}_{E/F}\GL_{n,E}\},\quad\mathrm{H}_{2}=\GL_{n,F}\times\GL_{n,F}.

The group
\mathrm{H}_{1}\times\mathrm{H}_{2}
acts on the right on
\mathrm{G}^{\prime}
by:

(g,v,u)\cdot(h_{1},(h_{2,1},h_{2,2}))=(h_{1}^{-1}gh_{2},h_{2,2}^{-1}v,uh_{2,2}).

We redefine
\mathrm{B}
to be the GIT quotient
\mathrm{G}^{\prime}/\mathrm{H}_{1}\times\mathrm{H}_{2}
, it can be identified with the quotient
(\mathrm{S}_{n}\times F^{n}\times F_{n})/\GL_{n}
, where
\GL_{n}
acts by

(s,v,u)\cdot h=(h^{-1}sh,h^{-1}v,uh),

and the identification is made through the map:

\alpha:\mathrm{Res}_{E/F}(\GL_{n,E}\times\GL_{n,E})\mathrm{S}_{n},\quad(g_{1},g_{2})\nu(g_{1}^{-1}g_{2}).

Let
\sigma\in E,\sigma\sigma^{\mathtt{c}}=1
.
\mathrm{S}_{n}
has an open subset
\mathrm{S}_{n}^{\sigma}
consists of
x
such that
x-\sigma\cdot\mathrm{id}
is invertible, it descends to an open subset
\mathrm{B}^{\sigma}
of
\mathrm{B}
. Let
\tau\in E
with
\tau+\tau^{\mathtt{c}}=0
, let
\widetilde{\gl_{n}}^{\tau}
be the open subset of
\widetilde{\gl_{n}}
consisting of
(A,v,u)
such that
A-\tau
is invertible. We define the Cayley transform:

\mathfrak{c}_{\sigma}:\widetilde{\gl_{n}}^{\tau}\mathrm{S}_{n}^{\sigma}\times F^{n}\times F_{n},\quad(A,v,u)\left(-\sigma\frac{1+\tau^{-1}A}{1-\tau^{-1}A},v,u\right)

Let
(\mathrm{S}_{n}\times F^{n}\times F_{n})_{\mathrm{reg}}
be the open subset of the regular elements under this action of
\GL_{n}
, it has open subsets
(\mathrm{S}_{n}\times F^{n}\times F_{n})_{+}
(resp.
(\mathrm{S}_{n}\times F^{n}\times F_{n})_{-}
) consists of
(x,v,u)
such that
(v,xv,\cdots,x^{n-1}v)
forms a basis of
E^{n}
(resp.
(u,ux,\cdots,ux^{n-1})
form a basis of
E_{n}
), its preimage in
\mathrm{G}^{\prime}
will be denoted by
\mathrm{G}^{\prime}_{+}
(resp.
\mathrm{G}^{\prime}_{-}
). For
X\in\widetilde{\gl_{n}}^{\tau}
, and
\bullet\in\{+,-\}

X\in\widetilde{\gl_{n}}_{,\bullet}\iff\mathfrak{c}_{\sigma}(X)\in(\mathrm{S}_{n}^{\sigma}\times F_{n}\times F^{n})_{\bullet}

Let
\mathcal{H}
be the isometric class of
n
-dimensional non-degenerate
E/F
-Hermitian space. For
V\in\mathcal{H}
, we redefine

\mathrm{G}^{V}:=\U(V)\times\U(V)\times V,

let
\mathrm{H}^{V}
be the diagonal subgroup of
\U(V)\times\U(V)
. The group
\mathrm{H}^{V}\times\mathrm{H}^{V}
acts on the right on
\mathrm{G}^{V}
by

(g,v)\cdot(h_{1},h_{2})=(h_{1}^{-1}gh_{2},h_{2}^{-1}v),\quad g\in\U(V)\times\U(V),v\in V.

The GIT quotient
\mathrm{G}^{V}/\mathrm{H}^{V}\times\mathrm{H}^{V}
can be identified with
\mathrm{B}
. Let

\mathcal{O}_{b}=\{(V,\mathcal{O})\mid V\in\mathcal{H},\mathcal{O}\subset\mathrm{G}_{b}^{V}\text{ is a }\mathrm{H}^{V}(F)\times\mathrm{H}^{V}(F)\text{ semisimple orbit}\}

8.2.
Local theory

Now let
F
be a local field. For
f\in\mathcal{S}(\mathrm{G}^{\prime}(F)),(x,v,u)\in\mathrm{S}_{n}(F)\times F^{n}\times F_{n}
and
s\in\mathbb{C}
, we put

(8.1)

f^{\mathrm{S}}_{s}(x,v,u):=\begin{cases}\int_{\mathrm{H}_{1}(F)}\int_{\GL_{n}(F)}f(h_{1}^{-1},h_{1}^{-1}\nu^{-1}(x)(1,h_{2}),v,u)\xi(h_{1})\lvert\det h_{1}\rvert^{s}\mu(\nu^{-1}(x)h_{2,3}),&n\text{ odd},\\
\int_{\mathrm{H}_{1}(F)}\int_{\GL_{n}(F)}f(h_{1}^{-1},h_{1}^{-1}\nu^{-1}(x)(1,h_{2}),v,u)\xi(h_{1})\lvert\det h_{1}\rvert^{s},&n\text{ even}.\end{cases}

Fix
\gamma\in\mathrm{G}^{\prime}(F)
, let
b
be the image of
\gamma
in
\mathrm{B}(F)
. Choose
\sigma\in E,\sigma\sigma^{\mathtt{c}}=1
such that
b\in\mathrm{B}^{\sigma}(F)
, let
u\in C_{c}^{\infty}(\mathrm{B}^{\sigma}(F))
, for
s\in\mathbb{C}
, define
\widetilde{f}_{s}\in\mathcal{S}(\widetilde{\gl_{n}}(F))
by

\widetilde{f}_{s}:=(u\cdot f_{s}^{\mathrm{S}})(\mathfrak{c}_{\sigma}(X)).

We define

L_{\gamma}(s,\xi):=L_{\mathfrak{c}_{\sigma}^{-1}(b)}(s,\xi)

and

I^{\sigma}_{\gamma}(f,\xi,s):=\begin{cases}I_{\mathfrak{c}_{\sigma}(x)}(\widetilde{f}_{s},\xi,s)\mu(\gamma_{n}\gamma_{n+1}^{-1})&n\text{ odd}\\
I_{\mathfrak{c}_{\sigma}(x)}(\widetilde{f}_{s},\xi,s)&n\text{ even}\end{cases}.

Proposition 8.1
.

The definition of
I^{\sigma}(f,\xi,s)
is independent of the choice of
u
, and (1)-(5) of Proposition
5.12
holds (where
\mathrm{G}^{\prime},\mathrm{H}_{1},\mathrm{H}_{2}
are redefined as in this Subsection).

and we also have the following lemma

Lemma 8.2
.

Let
\eta
be a character on
\mathrm{H}_{2}(F)
defined by
\eta(h_{2,1},h_{2,2})=\eta(h_{2,1})^{n+1}\eta(h_{2,2})^{n}
. Let
f\in\mathrm{S}(\mathrm{G}^{\prime}(F))
, we have the following assertions:

•

If
\gamma
is regular semisimple, then

I^{\sigma}_{\gamma}(f,\xi,s)=\int_{\mathrm{H}_{1}(F)}\int_{\mathrm{H}_{2}(F)}f(\gamma\cdot(h_{1},h_{2}))\xi(h_{1})\lvert\det h_{1}\rvert^{s}\eta(h_{2})\mathrm{d}h_{1}\mathrm{d}h_{2},

where the integral converges absolutely for any
s\in\mathbb{C}
.

•

If
\gamma\in\mathrm{G}^{\prime}_{+}(F)
(resp.
\mathrm{G}^{\prime}_{-}(F)
), then the integral

\int_{\mathrm{H}_{1}(F)}\int_{\mathrm{H}_{2}(F)}f(\gamma\cdot(h_{1},h_{2}))\xi(h_{1})\lvert\det h_{1}\rvert^{s}\eta(h_{2})\mathrm{d}h_{1}\mathrm{d}h_{2},

is absolutely convergent for
s\in\mathcal{H}_{<-1+\frac{1}{n}}
(resp.
s\in\mathcal{H}_{>1-\frac{1}{n}}
) and equals
I^{\sigma}_{\gamma}(f,\xi,s)
there.

In particular, in these cases, the definition of
I^{\sigma}_{\gamma}(f,\xi,s)
does not depend on the choice of
\sigma
.

There is a notion of transfer between
f\in\mathrm{S}(\mathrm{G}^{\prime}(F))
and
(f^{V})\in\mathcal{S}(\mathrm{G}^{V}(F))
similar as the situation in Subsection
7.2
. For
(\gamma,v,u)\in\mathrm{G}^{\prime}(F)
, choose
\sigma
such that
\alpha(\gamma)\in\mathrm{S}_{n}^{\sigma}(F)
, let
X=\mathfrak{c}_{\sigma}^{-1}(\alpha(\gamma),v,u)
. Let
b\in\mathrm{B}(F)
, for
\mathcal{O}\in\mathcal{O}_{b}
We define

c_{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}:=c_{X,\mathfrak{c}_{\sigma}^{-1}(\mathcal{O})}\mu(x)^{\frac{n+1}{2}}\mu(\gamma_{1}^{-1}\gamma_{2})^{-1}\mu(2\sigma\tau^{-1})^{\frac{n(n-1)}{2}}\mu(1-\tau^{-1}\mathfrak{c}_{\sigma}(x))^{-n+1},

where
\mathfrak{c}_{\sigma}^{-1}(\mathcal{O})
denotes the semisimple orbit in
\widetilde{\mathfrak{u}}^{V}
corresponding to
\mathcal{O}
and
\gamma=(\gamma_{1},\gamma_{2})
.

Proposition 8.3
.

Let
f\in\mathrm{S}(\mathrm{G}^{\prime}(F))
and for each
V\in\mathcal{H}
let
f^{V}\in\mathcal{S}(\mathrm{G}^{V}(F))
such that
f\leftrightarrow(f^{V})
. Then for any regular element
\gamma
in
\mathrm{G}^{\prime}_{b}(F)
, we have

I^{\sigma,\natural}_{\gamma}(f)=\sum_{(V,\mathcal{O})\in\mathcal{O}_{b}}c_{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}J^{V}_{\mathcal{O}}(f^{V}).

8.3.
Global theory

Let
E/F
be a quadratic extension of number fields. For
f\in\mathrm{S}(\mathrm{G}^{\prime}(\mathbb{A}))
and
T\in\mathfrak{a}_{0}
, one defines a modified kernel
K_{f}^{T}(h_{1},h_{2})
as in
[
10
]
*Section 7.

Using the same strategy as the proof of the Theorem
3.1
, one can prove the following proposition. The corresponding results on the asymptotics of the modified kernel can be found in
[
10
]
*Section 7.

Proposition 8.4
.

Let
\xi
be a strictly unitary character of
\mathbb{A}_{E}^{\times}
.

(1)

For any
f\in\mathcal{S}(\mathrm{G}^{\prime}(\mathbb{A}))
,
s\in\mathbb{C}
and
T
sufficiently positive, we have

(8.2)

\sum_{\gamma\in\mathrm{B}(F)}\int_{[\mathrm{H}_{1}]}\int_{[\mathrm{H}_{2}]}\lvert K_{f,\gamma}^{T}(h_{1},h_{2})\rvert\lvert\det h_{1}\rvert^{\mathrm{Re}(s)}\mathrm{d}h_{1}\mathrm{d}h_{2}<\infty.

(2)

For
\gamma\in\mathrm{B}(F)
, as a function of
T
, the integral

I_{\bullet}^{T}(f,\xi,s):=\int_{[\mathrm{H}_{1}]}\int_{[\mathrm{H}_{2}]}K^{T}_{f,\gamma}(h_{1},h_{2})\xi(h_{1})\lvert\det h_{1}\rvert^{s}\eta(h_{2})\mathrm{d}h_{1}\mathrm{d}h_{2}

is an exponential polynomial. If
s\not\in\{-1,1\}
, then the pure polynomial term is constant, denoted by
I_{\gamma}(f,\xi,s)
. For a fixed
f
and
\xi
,
I_{\gamma}(f,\xi,s)
is meromorphic on
\mathbb{C}\setminus\{-1,1\}
. Let
I_{\gamma}(f,\xi):=I_{\gamma}(f,\xi,0)
.

(3)

For each
\gamma\in\mathrm{B}(F)
and
s\not\in\{-1,1\}
, the distribution
I_{\gamma}(\cdot,\xi,s)
is continuous on
\mathcal{S}(\mathrm{G}^{\prime}(\mathbb{A}))
and we define

I(f,\xi,s):=\sum_{\gamma\in\mathrm{B}(F)}I_{\gamma}(f,\xi,s).

Where the sum on the right-hand side is absolutely convergent.

Similar to the results in Section
4
and
6
, when the test function is regular supported, the distribution
I_{\gamma}
can be described in terms of the normalized orbital integral.

Proposition 8.5
.

Let
f\in\mathcal{S}(\mathrm{G}^{\prime}(\mathbb{A}))
. If there exists a place
v
of
F
such that
f
is of the form
f_{v}f^{v}
, with
f_{v}\in\mathcal{S}(\mathrm{G}^{\prime}(F_{v})),f^{v}\in\mathcal{S}(\mathrm{G}^{\prime}(\mathbb{A}^{v}))
and
\supp(f_{v})\subset\mathrm{G}^{\prime}_{+}(F_{v})
(resp.
\mathrm{G}^{\prime}_{-}(F_{v})
), then (1)-(3) of Theorem
4.2
holds.

Proposition 8.6
.

Let
f=f_{v}f^{v}\in\mathcal{S}(\mathrm{G}^{\prime}(\mathbb{A}))
with
f_{v}\in\mathcal{S}(\mathrm{G}^{\prime}(F_{v})),f^{v}\in\mathcal{S}(\mathrm{G}^{\prime}(\mathbb{A}^{v}))
and
\mathrm{supp}(f_{v})\subset\mathrm{G}^{\prime}_{\mathrm{reg}}(F_{v})
, then for
b\in\mathrm{B}(F)
, we have

(8.3)

I_{b}(f,\xi,s)=\sum_{\gamma}I^{\sigma}_{\gamma}(f,\xi,s).

Where
I_{b}
is the distribution from relative trace formula in Proposition
8.4
(2), and
I^{\sigma}_{\gamma}
is definds as in Section
6
, the sum runs through all the representative of
\mathrm{H}_{1}(F)\times\mathrm{H}_{2}(F)
orbits in
\mathrm{G}^{\prime}_{b}(F)\cap\mathrm{G}^{\prime}_{\mathrm{reg}}(F)
.

Appendix A
Asymptotic of modified kernel

In this appendix, we generalize the result of
[
3
]
*Theorem 3.3.7.1 to asymptotics of the modified kernel associated with parabolic subgroups and their Levi subgroups and introduce a Lie algebra analogue of it. We follow the strategy in
[
3
]
*Section 3.

For a reductive group
G
over
F
, and three semi-standard parabolic subgroups
R\subset S\subset Q
of
G
, we write
\sigma_{R}^{S,Q}
for the characteristic function of
X\in\mathfrak{a}_{0}
satisfying

•

\alpha(X)>0
for all
\alpha\in\Delta_{S}^{R}
,

•

\alpha(X)\leq 0
for all
\alpha\in\Delta_{S}^{Q}\setminus\Delta_{S}^{R}
,

•

\varpi(H)>0
for all
\varpi\in\widehat{\Delta}_{R}^{Q}
.

Proposition A.1
.

Let
Q\in\mathcal{F}_{\mathrm{RS}}
. Then for every
N>0
, there exists a continuous semi-norm
\|\cdot\|
on
\mathcal{S}(\mathrm{G}^{\prime}(\mathbb{A}))
such that

(A.1)

\sum_{\chi\in\mathfrak{X}(\mathrm{G}^{\prime})}\lvert K_{f,\chi}^{Q,T}(h_{1},h_{2})-F^{Q_{n+1}}(h_{2,n},T_{Q_{n+1}})K_{f,Q,\chi}(h_{1},h_{2})\rvert\leq e^{-N\|T\|}\|h_{1}\|_{Q_{\mathrm{H}_{1}}}^{-N}\|h_{2}\|_{Q_{\mathrm{H}_{2}}}^{-N}\|f\|

holds for
f\in\mathcal{S}(\mathrm{G}^{\prime}(\mathbb{A})),(h_{1},h_{2})\in[\mathrm{H}_{1}]_{Q_{\mathrm{H}_{1}}}\times[\mathrm{H}_{2}]^{\mathbbm{1}}_{Q_{\mathrm{H}_{2}}}
and
T\in\mathfrak{a}_{n+1}
sufficiently positive.

We denote by
\mathcal{T}^{Q}_{\mathcal{F}_{\mathrm{RS}},F}
the following space of function

\mathcal{T}^{Q}_{\mathcal{F}_{\mathrm{RS}},F}:=\{({}_{P}\varphi)\in\prod_{\begin{subarray}{c}R\subset Q\\
R\in\mathcal{F}_{\mathrm{RS},F}\end{subarray}}\mathcal{T}([\GL_{n}]_{R_{n}})\mid\varphi_{R}-\varphi_{S}\in\mathcal{S}_{d_{R_{n+1}}^{S_{n+1}}}(R_{n}(F)\backslash\GL_{n}(\mathbb{A}))\text{ for any }R\subset S\subset Q\}

Lemma A.2
.

For
\underline{\varphi}=({}_{P}\varphi)\in\mathcal{T}^{Q}_{\mathcal{F}_{\mathrm{RS},F}}(\GL_{n})
and
g\in[\GL_{n}]_{Q_{n}}^{\mathbbm{1}}
define

\Lambda^{Q,T}\underline{\varphi}(g)=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}P\in\mathcal{F}_{\mathrm{RS}}\\
P\subset Q\end{subarray}}\varepsilon_{P}^{Q}\sum_{\gamma\in P_{n}(F)\backslash Q_{n}(F)}\widehat{\tau}_{P_{n+1}}^{Q_{n+1}}(H_{P_{n+1}}(\gamma g)-T_{P_{n+1}})\cdot{}_{P}\varphi(\gamma g).

We also define

\Pi^{Q,T}\underline{\varphi}(g)=F^{Q_{n+1}}(g,T)\cdot{}_{Q}\varphi(g).

Then for any
c>0,N>0
, there exists a continuous semi-norm
\|\cdot\|
on
\mathcal{T}_{\mathcal{F}_{\mathrm{RS}},F}^{Q}(\GL_{n})
such that

\left\|\Lambda^{Q,T}\underline{\varphi}-\Pi^{Q,T}\underline{\varphi}\right\|_{\infty,N}\leq e^{-c\|T\|}\|\underline{\varphi}\|

Proof.

Using the partition formula in
[
25
]
*Lemme 2.1, for every
g\in[\GL_{n}]_{P_{n}}
, we have

\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}R\in\mathcal{F}_{\mathrm{RS},F}\\
R\subset P\end{subarray}}\sum_{\delta\in R_{n}(F)\backslash P_{n}(F)}F^{R_{n+1}}(\delta g,T_{R_{n+1}})\tau_{R_{n+1}}^{P_{n+1}}(H_{P_{n+1}}(\delta g)-T_{P_{n+1}})=1,

together with the equation

\widehat{\tau}_{P_{n+1}}^{Q_{n+1}}\tau_{R_{n+1}}^{P_{n+1}}=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}S\in\mathcal{F}_{\mathrm{RS},F}\\
P\subset S\subset Q\end{subarray}}\sigma_{R_{n+1}}^{S_{n+1},Q_{n+1}}.

For
R\subset S
and
R,S\in\mathcal{F}_{\mathrm{RS},F}
, define

{}_{R,S}\varphi(g)=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}P\in\mathcal{F}_{\mathrm{RS},F}\\
R\subset P\subset S\end{subarray}}\epsilon_{P}^{Q}\cdot{}_{P}\varphi(g),

then we obtain

\Lambda^{Q,T}\underline{\varphi}(g)=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}R,S\in\mathcal{F}_{\mathrm{RS},F}\\
R\subset S\end{subarray}}\sum_{\gamma\in R_{n}(F)\backslash Q_{n}(F)}F^{R_{n+1}}(\gamma g,T_{R_{n+1}})\sigma_{R_{n+1}}^{S_{n+1},Q_{n+1}}(H_{R_{n+1}}(\gamma g)-T_{R_{n+1}}){}_{R,S}\varphi(\gamma g).

Note that
\sigma_{Q_{n+1}}^{Q_{n+1},Q_{n+1}}=1
and
\sigma_{R_{n+1}}^{R_{n+1},Q_{n+1}}=0
for
R\subsetneq Q
, it follows that

\Lambda^{Q,T}\underline{\varphi}(g)-\Pi^{Q,T}\underline{\varphi}(g)=\sum_{R\subsetneq S}\sum_{\gamma\in R_{n}(F)\backslash Q_{n}(F)}F^{R_{n+1}}(\delta g,T)\sigma_{R_{n+1}}^{S_{n+1},Q_{n+1}}(H_{R_{n+1}}(\gamma g)-T_{R_{n+1}})\cdot{}_{R,S}\varphi(\gamma g).

Since
E_{R_{n}}^{Q_{n}}:f(g\sum_{\gamma\in R_{n}(F)\backslash Q_{n}(F)}f(\gamma g))
sends
\mathcal{S}^{0}([\GL_{n}]_{R_{n}}^{\mathbbm{1}})
to
\mathcal{S}^{0}([\GL_{n}]_{Q_{n}}^{\mathbbm{1}})
, it remains to show that for every
c>0
and
N>0
, there exists a continuous semi-norm
\|\cdot\|
on
\mathcal{T}^{Q}_{\mathrm{RS},F}(\GL_{n})
such that

(A.2)

\lvert{}_{R,S}\varphi(g)\rvert\leq e^{-c\|T\|}\|g\|_{R_{n}}^{-N}\|\varphi\|

for all
\underline{\varphi}\in\mathcal{T}^{Q}_{\mathrm{RS},F}(\GL_{n})
,
T
sufficiently positive and
g\in R_{n}(F)\backslash G_{n}(\mathbb{A})_{Q}^{\mathbbm{1}}
with

F^{R_{n+1}}(g,T)\sigma_{R_{n+1}}^{S_{n+1},Q_{n+1}}(H_{R_{n+1}}(g)-T_{R_{n+1}})\neq 0.

We can assume
P
is standard. Take such
g
and take
z\in A_{Q}^{\mathrm{H}_{1},\infty}
such that
g
can written as
zg^{1}
where
g^{1}\in G_{n+1}(\mathbb{A})_{Q}^{1}
, there exists
r>0
such that
\|g\|_{R_{n}}\ll\|g^{1}\|_{R_{n+1}}^{r_{0}}
. By
[
10
]
*Lemma 4.29 applied to
G=M_{Q_{n+1}}
, there exists
r>0
such that

(A.3)

\displaystyle e^{\|T\|}

\displaystyle\ll\left(\min_{\alpha\in\Delta_{0}^{S_{n+1}}\setminus\Delta_{0}^{R_{n+1}}}d_{R_{n+1},\alpha}(g^{1})\right)^{r}\sim\left(\min_{\alpha\in\Delta_{0}^{S_{n+1}}\setminus\Delta_{0}^{R_{n+1}}}d_{R_{n+1},\alpha}(g)\right)^{r}

(A.4)

\displaystyle\|g\|_{R_{n}}

\displaystyle\ll\|g^{1}\|_{R_{n+1}}^{r_{0}}\ll\left(\max_{\alpha\in\Delta_{0}^{S_{n+1}}\setminus\Delta_{0}^{R_{n+1}}}d_{R_{n+1},\alpha}(g)\right)^{rr_{0}}.

Fix
\alpha\in\Delta_{0}^{S_{n+1}}\setminus\Delta_{0}^{R_{n+1}}
, for any
P\in\mathcal{F}_{\mathrm{RS,F}}
with
\alpha\in\Delta_{0}^{P_{n+1}}
, there is unique
P^{\alpha}\in\mathcal{F}_{\mathrm{RS},F}
such that
\Delta_{0}^{P_{n+1}^{\alpha}}=\Delta_{0}^{P_{n+1}}\setminus\{\alpha\}
. Then there exists
N_{0}>0
such that for any
r>0
, we can find a continuous semi-norm
\|\cdot\|
on
\mathcal{T}^{Q}_{\mathrm{RS},F}(\GL_{n})
with

\lvert{}_{R,S}\varphi(g)\rvert\leq\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}R\subset P\subset S\\
\alpha\in\Delta_{0}^{P_{n+1}}\end{subarray}}\lvert_{P}\varphi(g)-{}_{P^{\alpha}}\varphi(g)\rvert\leq\|g\|_{R_{n}}^{N_{0}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}R\subset P\subset S\\
\alpha\in\Delta_{0}^{P_{n+1}}\end{subarray}}d_{P_{n+1},\alpha}(g)^{-r}\|\underline{\varphi}\|.

The equation (
A.3
) implies
d_{P_{n+1},\alpha}\sim d_{R_{n+1},\alpha}
, hence by (
A.4
), as
\alpha
varies through
\Delta_{0}^{Q_{n+1}}\setminus\Delta_{0}^{P_{n+1}}
, (
A.2
) is proved.
∎

Now we prove Proposition
A.1
.

Proof.

Given
\psi\in\mathcal{T}^{0}([\mathrm{H}_{2,n+1}]_{Q_{\mathrm{H}_{2,n+1}}})
and
\chi\in\mathfrak{X}(\mathrm{G}^{\prime})
, consider the following sequence of maps

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathcal{T}^{0}([\mathrm{H}_{1}]_{Q_{\mathrm{H}_{1}}})\otimes\mathcal{T}^{0}([\mathrm{H}_{2,n+1}]_{Q_{\mathrm{H}_{2,n+1}}})}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathcal{T}^{\Delta,Q}_{\mathcal{F}_{\mathrm{RS},F}}(\mathrm{G}^{\prime})\otimes\mathcal{T}^{0}([\mathrm{H}_{2,n+1}]_{Q_{\mathrm{H}_{2,n+1}}})}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathcal{T}^{\Delta,Q}_{\mathcal{F}_{\mathrm{RS},F}}(\mathrm{H}_{2})}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathcal{T}^{Q}_{\mathcal{F}_{\mathrm{RS},F}}(\mathrm{H}_{2})}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathcal{S}^{0}([\mathrm{H}_{2}]_{Q_{\mathrm{H}_{2}}^{\mathbbm{1}}})}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces R(f^{\vee})_{\chi^{\vee}}\otimes\mathrm{id}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{Res}\otimes\mathrm{id}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\langle\cdot,\cdot\rangle}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Lambda^{Q,T}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Pi^{Q,T}}

Where the first map sends
\varphi\otimes\psi
to
PR_{\chi^{\vee}}(f^{\vee})\varphi_{P_{\mathrm{H}_{1}}}\otimes\psi
, the second map is restriction to
H_{2}\subset\mathrm{G}^{\prime}
, the third map sends
({}_{P}\varphi)\otimes\psi
to
P\langle{}_{P}\varphi,\psi_{P_{\mathrm{H}_{2,n+1}}}\rangle
, the last map is the truncation operator defined in Lemma
A.2
. By
[
3
]
*Proposition 3.4.2.1 and Lemma
A.2
, the images of these maps land in the target, and by the closed graph theorem, they are continuous. Let
L_{f,\chi}^{Q,T}
be the composition of the first three maps and
\Lambda^{Q,T}
at the last, and
P_{f,\chi}^{Q,T}
the composition of first three maps and
\Pi^{Q,T}
at the last. One check directly that

L^{Q,T}_{f,\chi}(\varphi\otimes\psi)(h_{2,n})=\int_{[\mathrm{H}_{1}]_{Q_{\mathrm{H}_{1}}}\times[\mathrm{H}_{2,n+1}]_{Q_{\mathrm{H}_{2,n+1}}}}K_{f,\chi}^{Q,T}(h_{1},h_{2,n},h_{2,n+1})\varphi(h_{1})\psi(h_{2,n+1}).

and

P^{Q,T}_{f,\chi}(\varphi\otimes\psi)(h_{2,n})=F^{Q_{n+1}}(h_{2,n},T_{Q_{n+1}})\int_{[\mathrm{H}_{1}]_{Q_{\mathrm{H}_{1}}}\times[\mathrm{H}_{2,n+1}]_{Q_{\mathrm{H}_{2,n+1}}}}K_{f,Q,\chi}(h_{1},h_{2,n},h_{2,n+1})\varphi(h_{1})\psi(h_{2,n+1}).

By
[
3
]
*Theorem 2.9.4.1.3 and Lemma
A.2
, for every
\varphi\otimes\psi\in\mathcal{T}^{0}([\mathrm{H}_{1}]_{Q_{\mathrm{H}_{1}}})\otimes\mathcal{T}^{0}([\mathrm{H}_{2,n+1}]_{Q_{\mathrm{H}_{2,n+1}}})
and
N>0
, we have

\sum_{\chi\in\mathfrak{X}(\mathrm{G}^{\prime})}\left\|L_{f,\chi}^{Q,T}(\varphi\otimes\psi)-P_{f,\chi}^{Q,T}(\varphi\otimes\psi)\right\|_{\infty,N}\ll_{N,\varphi,\psi}e^{-N\|T\|}.

By the uniformly boundedness principle, for each
\varphi\otimes\psi\in\mathcal{T}^{0}_{N}([\mathrm{H}_{1}]_{Q_{\mathrm{H}_{1}}})\otimes\mathcal{T}^{0}_{N}([\mathrm{H}_{2,n+1}]_{Q_{\mathrm{H}_{2,n+1}}})
, we have

\sum_{\chi\in\mathfrak{X}(\mathrm{G}^{\prime})}\left\|L_{f,\chi}^{Q,T}(\varphi\otimes\psi)-P_{f,\chi}^{Q,T}(\varphi\otimes\psi)\right\|_{\infty,N}\ll_{N}e^{-N\|T\|}\|\varphi\|_{1,-N}\|\psi\|_{1,-N}.

Apply this to
\varphi=\delta_{h_{1}},\psi=\delta_{h_{2,n+1}}
, Proposition
A.1
is proved except the semi-norm on
f
. One then deduces it by applying the uniform boundedness principle again.
∎

There is also an analogous result on the geometric side.

Proposition A.3
.

There exists a continuous semi-norm
\|\cdot\|
on
\mathcal{S}(\mathrm{G}^{\prime}(\mathbb{A}))
such that for any
f\in\mathcal{S}(\mathrm{G}^{\prime}(\mathbb{A}))
and
N>0
, we have

\sum_{\gamma\in\mathrm{B}(F)}\lvert K_{f,\gamma}^{Q,T}(h_{1},h_{2})-F^{Q_{n+1}}(h_{2,n},T_{Q_{n+1}})K_{f,Q,\gamma}(h_{1},h_{2})\rvert\ll e^{-N\|T\|}\|h_{1}\|_{Q_{\mathrm{H}_{1}}}^{-N}\|h_{2}\|_{Q_{\mathrm{H}_{2}}}^{-N}\|f\|.

Proof.

Let
\mathbf{A}^{N}
be the
N
-dimensional affine space over
F
. Choose any closed embedding
i:\mathrm{B}\hookrightarrow\mathbf{A}^{N}
for some
N
. We extend the function
K_{f,\gamma}
for all
\gamma\in\mathbb{A}^{N}
by setting
K_{f,\gamma}=0
if
\gamma
is not in the image of
i
.

Fix
f
, there exists
d\in F
such that
K_{f,\gamma}=0
unless
\gamma\in d\mathcal{O}_{F}^{N}
. Choose
u\in C_{c}^{\infty}(F_{\infty}^{N})
support around
0
such that
\supp u\cap d\mathcal{O}_{F}^{N}=\{0\}
. Let
p
be the composition
\mathrm{G}^{\prime}\mathrm{B}\hookrightarrow\mathbf{A}^{N}
. For
\gamma\in(d\mathcal{O}_{F})^{N}
, define
f_{\gamma}(g)=f(g)\cdot u(p(g_{\infty})-\gamma)
. Then by
[
10
]
*Proposition 4.30, we have
f_{\gamma}\in\mathcal{S}(\mathrm{G}^{\prime}(\mathbb{A}))
and for any continuous semi-norm
\|\cdot\|_{\mathcal{S}}
on
\mathcal{S}(\mathrm{G}^{\prime}(\mathbb{A}))
, the sum

\sum_{\gamma\in\mathrm{B}(F)}\|f_{\gamma}\|_{\mathcal{S}}

is finite, hence defines a continuous semi-norm on
\mathcal{S}(\mathrm{G}^{\prime}(\mathbb{A}))
by uniform boundedness principle.
One check directly that
K_{f_{\gamma},P}(h_{1},h_{2})=K_{f,P,\gamma}(h_{1},h_{2})
for any
P\in\mathcal{F}_{\mathrm{RS}}
and
(h_{1},h_{2})\in[\mathrm{H}_{1}]_{P_{\mathrm{H}_{1}}}\times[\mathrm{H}_{2}]_{P_{\mathrm{H}_{2}}}
, hence
K_{f_{\gamma}}^{Q,T}(h_{1},h_{2})=K_{f,\gamma}^{Q,T}(h_{1},h_{2})
.

Proposition
A.1
implies there exists a semi-norm
\|\cdot\|_{\mathcal{S}}
on
\mathcal{S}(\mathrm{G}^{\prime}(\mathbb{A})
such that

\lvert K_{f}^{Q,T}(h_{1},h_{2})-F^{Q_{n+1}}(h_{2,n},T_{Q_{n+1}})K_{f,Q}(h_{1},h_{2})\rvert\leq e^{-N\|T\|}\|h_{1}\|_{Q_{\mathrm{H}_{1}}}^{-N}\|h_{2}\|_{Q_{\mathrm{H}_{2}}}^{-N}\|f\|_{\mathcal{S}}.

Therefore

\sum_{\gamma\in\mathrm{B}(F)}\lvert K_{f,\gamma}^{Q,T}(h_{1},h_{2})-F^{Q_{n+1}}(h_{2,n},T_{Q_{n+1}})K_{f,Q,\gamma}(h_{1},h_{2})\rvert\leq\left(\sum_{\gamma\in\mathrm{B}(F)}\|f_{\gamma}\|_{\mathcal{S}}\right)e^{-N\|T\|}\|h_{1}\|_{Q_{\mathrm{H}_{1}}}^{-N}\|h_{2}\|_{Q_{\mathrm{H}_{2}}}^{-N}.

∎

We state the asymptotic of the modified kernel on the Levi subgroup, the proof is similar so we omit the proof.

Let
Q\in\mathcal{F}_{\mathrm{RS}}
with Levi decomposition
P=MN
. Let
f\in\mathcal{S}(M(\mathbb{A}))
. For
\chi\in\mathfrak{X}(M)
and
T\in\mathfrak{a}_{0}
, we put

K^{M,T}_{f,\chi}(h_{1},h_{2})=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}P\in\mathcal{F}_{\mathrm{RS}}\\
P\subset Q\end{subarray}}\epsilon_{P}^{Q}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\gamma\in P_{M_{\mathrm{H}_{1}}}(F)\backslash M_{\mathrm{H}_{1}}(F)\\
\delta\in P_{M_{\mathrm{H}_{2}}}(F)\backslash M_{\mathrm{H}_{2}}(F)\end{subarray}}\widehat{\tau}_{P_{n+1}}^{Q_{n+1}}(H_{P_{n+1}}(\delta_{n}h_{2,n})-T_{P_{n+1}})K_{f,P_{M},\chi}(\gamma h_{1},\delta h_{2}).

where
(h_{1},h_{2})\in[M_{\mathrm{H}_{1}}]\times[M_{\mathrm{H}_{2}}]
.

Proposition A.4
.

For every
N>0
, there exists a continuous semi-norm
\|\cdot\|
on
\mathcal{S}(M(\mathbb{A}))
such that

(A.5)

\sum_{\chi\in\mathfrak{X}(M)}\lvert K_{f,\chi}^{M,T}(h_{1},h_{2})-F^{Q_{n+1}}(h_{2,n},T_{Q_{n+1}})K_{f,\chi}(h_{1},h_{2})\rvert\leq e^{-N\|T\|}\|h_{1}\|_{M_{\mathrm{H}_{1}}}^{-N}\|h_{2}\|_{M_{\mathrm{H}_{2}}}^{-N}\|f\|.

holds for
f\in\mathcal{S}(M(\mathbb{A})),(h_{1},h_{2})\in[M_{\mathrm{H}_{1}}]\times[M_{\mathrm{H}_{2}}]^{\mathbbm{1}}
and
T\in\mathfrak{a}_{n+1}
sufficiently positive.

On the Lie algebra, we have similar result

Proposition A.5
.

Let
\mathfrak{g}
denote either
\gl_{n+1}
or
\widetilde{\gl_{n}}
. Let
Q\in\mathcal{F}_{\mathrm{RS},F}
, then for every
N>0
, there exists a continuous semi-norm
\|\cdot\|
on
\mathcal{S}(\mathfrak{g}(\mathbb{A}))
such that

\sum_{a\in(\mathfrak{g}/\GL_{n})(F)}\lvert K^{Q,T}_{\varphi,a}(g)-F^{Q_{n+1}}(g,T_{Q_{n+1}})K_{\varphi,Q,a}(g)\rvert\leq e^{-N\|T\|}\|g\|^{-N}\|\varphi\|

for all
\varphi\in\mathcal{S}(\mathfrak{g}(\mathbb{A}))
,
g\in[\GL_{n}]_{Q_{n}}^{\mathbbm{1}}
and
T\in\mathfrak{a}_{n+1}
sufficiently positive.

Proof.

By the same argument as in the proof of Proposition
A.3
, we only need to prove that there exists a continuous semi-norm
\|\cdot\|
on
\mathcal{S}(\mathfrak{g}(\mathbb{A}))
such that

\lvert K_{\varphi}^{Q,T}(g)-F^{Q_{n+1}}(g,T_{Q_{n+1}})K_{\varphi,Q}(g)\rvert\leq e^{-N\|T\|}\|g\|^{-N}\|\varphi\|

holds for all
\varphi\in\mathcal{S}(\mathfrak{g}(\mathbb{A}))
and
g\in[\GL_{n}]^{\mathbbm{1}}_{Q_{n}}
.

By Lemma
A.2
, we are then reduced to check that the family
PK_{\varphi,P}
belongs to
\mathcal{T}^{Q}_{\mathcal{F}_{\mathrm{RS}},F}(\GL_{n})
.

We first show this for
\mathfrak{g}=\gl_{n+1}
. Let
R,S\in\mathcal{F}_{\mathrm{RS},F}
with
R\subset S\subset Q
. For
P\in\mathcal{F}_{\mathrm{RS}}
, we will write
\mathfrak{m}_{P}
and
\mathfrak{n}_{P}
the the Lie algebra of
M_{P_{n+1}}
and
N_{P_{n+1}}
respectively. We need to show there exists
N>0
such that for any
r>0
and
X\in\mathcal{U}(\gl_{\infty})
, we have

\lvert R(X)K_{\varphi,R}(g)-R(X)K_{\varphi,S}(g)\rvert\ll d_{R}^{S}(g)^{-r}\|g\|^{N}.

Replacing
\varphi
by
X\cdot\varphi
, we can assume
X=1
. For any
P\in\mathcal{F}_{\mathrm{RS}}
, we can extend the definition of
K_{\varphi,P}
to any
g\in[\GL_{n+1}]_{P_{n+1}}
by the same formula as in (
3.10
). After this extension, we can write

K_{\varphi,S}(g)-K_{\varphi,R}(g)=K_{\varphi,1}(g)+K_{\varphi_{2}}(g),

where

K_{\varphi,1}(g)=\sum_{M\in\mathfrak{m}_{R}(S)}\left(\int_{\mathfrak{n}_{S}(\mathbb{A})}\varphi((M+N)\cdot g)\mathrm{d}N-\int_{\mathfrak{n}_{R}(\mathbb{A})}\varphi((M+N)\cdot g)\mathrm{d}N\right)

and

K_{\varphi,2}(g)=\sum_{M\in\mathfrak{m}_{S}(F)\setminus\mathfrak{m}_{R}(F)}\int_{\mathfrak{n}_{S}(\mathbb{A})}\varphi((M+N)\cdot g)\mathrm{d}N.

We have

K_{\varphi,1}(g)=-K_{\varphi,S}(g)+(K_{\varphi,S})_{R_{n+1}}(g),

here
-_{R_{n+1}}
is the constant term operator. By
[
3
]
*Proposition 2.5.14.1, there exists
N>0
such that for any
r>0
, we have

(A.6)

\lvert K_{\varphi,1}(g)\rvert\ll_{r}d_{R_{n+1}}^{S_{n+1}}(g)^{-r}\|g\|^{N}

holds for any
g\in R_{n+1}(F)\backslash\GL_{n+1}(\mathbb{A})
. Thus it remains to show that there exists
N>0
such that for any
r>0
and
g\in R_{n+1}(F)\backslash\GL_{n+1}(\mathbb{A})
we have

(A.7)

\lvert K_{\varphi,2}(g)\rvert\ll_{r}d_{R_{n+1}}^{S_{n+1}}(g)^{-r}\|g\|^{N}.

After replacing
R_{n+1}
by its conjugation, we can assume
R_{n+1}
is standard. We then can assume
g
is in the Siegel set
\mathfrak{s}^{R_{n+1}}
of
R_{n+1}
, where we recall that any
g\in\mathfrak{s}^{R_{n+1}}
can be written as
ac
, where
a\in A_{n+1}^{\infty}
such that
\langle\alpha,a\rangle>t
for any
\alpha\in\Delta_{0}^{R_{n+1}}
and some
t>0
, and
c
lies in some compact subset
C
. We now proceed to show that there exists
N>0
such that for any
r>0
and
N_{1}>0
, there exists a continuous semi-norm
\|\cdot\|
on
\mathcal{S}(\gl_{n+1}(\mathbb{A}))
such that

(A.8)

\sum_{M\in\mathfrak{m}_{S}(F)\backslash\mathfrak{m}_{R}(F)}\varphi((M+Y)\cdot g)\ll\|Y\|^{-N_{1}}\|g\|^{N}d_{R_{n+1}}^{S_{n+1}}(g)^{-r}\|\varphi\|

holds for any
g\in\mathfrak{s}^{R_{n+1}}
,
Y\in\mathfrak{n}_{S}(\mathbb{A})
. Note that
(
A.8
)
would imply (
A.7
). To show
(
A.8
)
, after replacing
\|\cdot\|
by
\sup_{c\in C}\|R(c)\cdot\|
, we can assume
g=a\in A_{n+1}^{\infty}
with
a\in A_{n+1}^{\infty}
such that
\langle\alpha,a\rangle>t
for any
\alpha\in\Delta_{0}^{R_{n+1}}
. Regarding elements of
\gl_{n+1}(\mathbb{A})
as
(n+1)\times(n+1)
matrices, the action of
a
preserve each entries. Therefore (
A.8
) directly follows from the following two facts:

•

For any
N
sufficiently large, we have

\sum_{x\in F}\|ax\|^{-N}\ll\|a\|^{N}

holds for all
a\in\mathbb{A}^{\times}
.

•

Fix
t>0
, for any
r>0
there exists
N>0
such that

\sum_{x\in F\setminus\{0\}}\|ax\|^{-N}\ll|a|^{-r}

holds for all
a\in\mathbb{R}_{>t}\subset\mathbb{A}^{\times}
.

We thus finish the proof when
\mathfrak{g}=\gl_{n+1}
. For the case
\mathfrak{g}=\widetilde{\gl_{n}}
, choose a non-negative
\varphi^{\prime}\in\mathcal{S}(\mathbb{A})
with
\varphi^{\prime}(0)>0
. Given
\varphi\in\mathcal{S}(\widetilde{\gl_{n}}(\mathbb{A}))
, put
\varphi_{1}\in\mathcal{S}(\gl_{n+1}(\mathbb{A}))
by

\varphi_{1}\begin{pmatrix}A&v\\
u&d\end{pmatrix}=\varphi(A,v,u)\varphi^{\prime}(d).

Then there exists a constant
C>0
such that
K_{\varphi_{1},P}(g)=CK_{\varphi,P}(g)
. Therefore, the result follows from the case for
\mathfrak{g}=\gl_{n+1}
.
∎
